## Dumb Othmar / 失声的奥特玛

===Original===
Once upon a time there was a village on the top of a mountain, where during the winter months the villagers saw no one but each other, for the mountain was so steep and the path so narrow that, when it was blocked with ice, it was dangerous to ascend: so during the winter months the people lived by themselves, and cheered themselves as they might in the long dreary evenings, with games and dancing and singing and playing on pipes, for they were cheerful folk, joyous and lighthearted. The sweetest singer in the village was a lad named Othmar; his voice was as sweet as a nightingale’s before the dawn, and also he was the handsomest young fellow in all the country round. Strangers turned to look after him as he went by; he was tall and straight, and had curly brown hair, and big brown eyes, and lips that always smiled. He lived with his old mother, who was a widow, and he worked for them both in the fields and on the farms. When he was a boy he learned the notes of all the birds, and could imitate them so exactly that they would fly down to him and settle on his shoulders. When the farmers had sown their fields, and the birds would have come to pick up the grain, they sent for Othmar, and he sang and whistled till they all left the field and flew after him. So often he was called the bird-boy.

===Chinese===
从前，在一座高山顶上有个村子。每逢冬月，村民除了彼此，谁也见不着，因为山势陡峭，山路狭窄，一旦结冰封路，上山便有性命之虞。所以整整一个冬天，村里人都自个儿过活，在漫长而沉闷的夜晚里想法子取乐：做游戏、跳舞、唱歌、吹牧笛——因为他们都是快活人，无忧无虑，乐天知命。村里唱得最动听的，是个名叫奥特玛（Othmar）的小伙子；他的嗓音甜如黎明前的夜莺，他又是方圆一带最俊美的少年。陌生人走过他身旁，都要回头张望；他身量高挑挺拔，一头棕色鬈发，一双棕色大眼睛，唇边总挂着笑意。他同守寡的老母亲一起生活，为了母子二人的生计，在田里和农庄上做工。他还是个孩子的时候，就学会了各种鸟儿的鸣声，学得惟妙惟肖，鸟儿们会飞下来落在他的肩头。农夫们播完了种，鸟儿要来啄食谷粒时，便打发人去请奥特玛，他一唱一吹口哨，鸟儿便统统离开田地，跟着他飞走了。于是人们常叫他“鸟孩子”（bird-boy）。

===Original===
One evening before the winter had set in, or the roads were blocked with ice, there came along the high road into the village, a dwarf in a yellow cap leading a donkey, on whose back were fastened numberless musical instruments. Fiddles of all sorts, and viols, horns, trumpets, and pipes, and a big drum, and a small one with triangles and cymbals. In the middle of the village the little man stopped and looked about him. “Who would like to hear my music?” he cried, and then as the villagers came crowding around him, he bade them all sit down while he unpacked his mule, but he forbade any of them to help him, or to touch one of the instruments. “For mine is no common music,” he quoth; “all these I have made myself, and in each is a machine which makes it go on playing by itself, if once I start it. See here!” and he took up a long pipe and began to blow, and there came forth the sweetest notes that had ever been heard from any pipe. The little man paused for a minute, with the pipe in his hand, and then laid it down on the ground, when, wonderful to relate, it went on playing of itself.

===Chinese===
冬天还没来、道路还未封冰的一个傍晚，大路上走进村来一个戴黄帽子的矮人（dwarf），牵着一头驴，驴背上驮着数不清的乐器：各式提琴、维奥尔琴，还有号角、小号、牧笛，一面大鼓、一面小鼓，外加三角铁和钹。到了村子中央，小个子（the little man）站住脚，四下张望。“谁想听听我的音乐？”他喊道。村民们围拢过来，他便叫大家统统坐下，看他卸骡子上的东西，却不许任何人帮忙，也不许碰一件乐器。“因为我的可不是寻常的音乐，”他说，“这些都是我亲手做的，每件里头都装着一个机括，只要我一开动，它就能自己演奏下去。瞧！”他拿起一支长笛吹了起来，那笛声之甜美，是任何笛子从未发出过的。小个子把笛子握在手里停了片刻，然后把它放在地上——说来奇妙，那笛子竟自己接着吹了下去。

===Original===
All the villagers stared with surprise, and some called out that it was magic, and crossed themselves, but the little man took up another pipe, and set that going too, and then the horns and the trumpets, and the drums and the cymbals, and then he took a fiddle and drew the bow across it, and how it played! It made the people weep and laugh. Othmar lay on the ground listening, and it seemed to him as if the sound were made of silver, and when the musician had started them, and all the other fiddles were playing together, he felt as if he should go mad for joy to hear anything so lovely.

===Chinese===
村民们个个看得目瞪口呆，有人喊这是妖法，忙在胸前画十字。可小个子又拿起一支牧笛，让那支也奏起来，接着是号角、小号、鼓和钹；然后他取过一把提琴，弓子一拉——你听它奏得多么好！它叫人们又哭又笑。奥特玛躺在地上听着，只觉得那声音仿佛是银子做的；等乐师（the musician）把所有乐器都开动起来、所有的提琴一齐奏响时，他听得心醉神迷，几乎要为这样美妙的声音欢喜得发狂。

===Original===
Just behind where Othmar lay sat a young girl, named Hulda. She was an orphan, and dwelt alone with an old woman, who gave her food and lodging for sweeping out her room, and cleaning and cooking. Also Hulda made money for her by going out to work for the other women in the village. She was neither pretty nor clever, but she was a good girl, and if any of the villagers were ill or in trouble, it was for Hulda they would send at once, because they knew she would spare no pains to help them, and would think nothing too much trouble. She had played with Othmar ever since they were babies, and loved him dearly. She was the only one who listened to the music who did not think it beautiful. She shuddered as she heard it, and she sat and watched Othmar and saw that there were tears in his eyes, and she grieved that she did not love it as he did.

===Chinese===
奥特玛躺卧的地方后面，坐着一个少女，名叫胡尔达（Hulda）。她是个孤儿，独自借住在一个老太婆家里，替老太婆扫地、浆洗、做饭，换口饭吃、找个住处。此外胡尔达还出门替村里别的女人做工，挣钱糊口。她既不漂亮，也不聪明，却是个好心的姑娘；村里谁病了、谁遇了难，人们总立刻去请胡尔达，因为他们知道她定会不辞辛劳地帮忙，再麻烦也不嫌。她从襁褓时起就同奥特玛一起玩耍，深深地爱着他。听音乐的人里，唯独她一个不觉得那音乐美。她听着直打寒战，坐在那里望着奥特玛，看见他眼里含着泪，心里很难过，恨自己不能像他那样爱这音乐。

===Original===
When at last the instruments stopped and the listeners began to feel for money for the musician, he laughed and said, “You need not give me money, for I am very rich, and don’t need it. In return for my playing, I only ask for one recompense. Let any of your young folks who sing, sing me a song, for I too love hearing music that is not my own.” On this the villagers began to look round for all who could sing, and they chose out some three or four, and begged them to sing their very best to the wonderful musician. Among them was Othmar, but they all bid him wait to the last, as his would be the best. Whilst the others were singing, the little man did not seem to heed them much, though they tried hard to sing well, and chose their prettiest songs, but their voices sounded very rough and poor after the playing.

===Chinese===
乐器终于停下来，听众们开始摸钱给乐师，他却笑道：“不必给我钱，我很富，用不着。作为我演奏的回报，我只求一件酬谢：你们中间会唱歌的年轻人，随便哪一个，给我唱一支歌吧，我也爱听听不是我自己做的音乐。”村民们听了，便四下寻找会唱的人，挑出三四个来，求他们拿出最好本事，唱给这位神奇的乐师听。奥特玛也在其中，可大家都叫他留到最后唱，因为他的歌必定是最好的。旁人唱的时候，小个子似乎并不大留意，尽管他们卖力地唱，挑了最动听的歌，可听过那些乐器的演奏之后，他们的嗓音显得又粗涩又贫乏。

===Original===
But when Othmar began he stopped twanging the fiddle-strings and watched him. Othmar’s voice rang out clear and sweet, and all the village folk felt proud of his singing, even after hearing the wonderful instruments. When he ceased the little man rose, and said﻿—

===Chinese===
可奥特玛一开口，他便住了手，不再拨弄琴弦，望着他。奥特玛的歌声清亮甜美地响起来，全村人都为他的歌感到骄傲，哪怕是刚听过那些神奇的乐器。他唱完，小个子站起身来，说道——

===Original===
“You have a sweet voice, my boy, be sure that you always use it well; and now I must be going my way, but as I am a stranger here, perhaps you would not mind setting me on my road, and showing me the best way over the hill.”

===Chinese===
“你有一副好嗓子，孩子，务必永远好好用它。如今我该上路了，只是我在此地人生地疏，也许你不介意送我一程，给我指一条翻山最好的路吧。”

===Original===
Othmar sprang up, delighted to go with him, but Hulda, who watched from behind, came up to him and whispered﻿—

===Chinese===
奥特玛一跃而起，欢欢喜喜愿与他同行；胡尔达在后面看着，走上前来，低声说——

===Original===
“Oh, Othmar, don’t let him take you far﻿—come back soon.”

===Chinese===
“哦，奥特玛，别让他带你走远了——早些回来。”

===Original===
“How silly you are, Hulda!” said Othmar, almost angrily. “Of course I shall come back, but I shall go with him as far as he wants, and then, perhaps, he will let me hear him play again.”

===Chinese===
“你多傻呀，胡尔达！”奥特玛说，几乎有些恼了。“我当然会回来。他要我送多远我就送多远，说不定，他还会让我再听他演奏一回呢。”

===Original===
Othmar and the little dwarf started with the mule laden with instruments, and Othmar led the way down the best road. The dwarf did not speak at all, and so they went on in silence till they had got on to the top of a high hill where they could see the country all round for miles, and the moon was beginning to rise. Here the musician stopped his mule, and stood for a while looking all round. Then he turned, and said to Othmar﻿—“I know now where I am, and here will I stay for tonight, but first before you leave me, would you not like to hear my fiddles and horns again?”

===Chinese===
奥特玛同小矮人牵着驮乐器的骡子出发了，奥特玛领路，走的是最好的那条道。矮人一言不发，两人便这样默默前行，直到登上一座高山顶上，从那里可以望见方圆数里的原野，月亮正要升起来。乐师在这里勒住骡子，站了一会儿环顾四周，然后回过头对奥特玛说——“我认得路了，今晚我就歇在这儿。不过在你离开我之前，不想再听听我的提琴和号角吗？”

===Original===
“That I would,” cried Othmar, and he sat himself down on the ground delighted, while the little man unloaded the mule.

===Chinese===
“那可太想了！”奥特玛喊道，欢喜地坐在地上，看小个子卸下骡子。

===Original===
“And now,” he said to Othmar with a twisted smile, “you shall hear them play as no one has ever heard them. Yes, and you shall see them too;” then he laid them in rows﻿—the fiddles first, and the viols, and then the horns and trumpets, and last the drums and cymbals and triangles, and clapped his hands, giving a long, shrill whistle. As he whistled, the instruments rose from the ground, and they began to swell, and their shapes to change till no longer did they look like musical instruments, but like human beings, only each had in a strange way kept the shape it had formerly. The flutes and pipes were tall and thin, and they and the violins had changed into beautiful girls with slender throats, and the trumpets were all men and boys of different sizes, but the drum was the strangest of all, for it was a fat man with very short legs. The moon had risen and Othmar could see them all quite clearly, and though he trembled with fear and his heart beat high, yet still he watched. They stood silent together for a space in a weird crowd, and then the dwarf waved his arms and called, “Ay! are you all there, my children?﻿—yes, one, two three four five, six seven eight, nine ten eleven twelve, thirteen﻿—that is right. Come practice, practice, practice, and then you shall have a game, and see who Othmar loves best, and who he will kiss first.”

===Chinese===
“现在，”他带着一丝扭曲的笑对奥特玛说，“我要让你听听谁也没听过的演奏。对，你还能亲眼见见它们。”他把乐器一行行摆开——先是提琴，再是维奥尔琴，然后是号角和小号，最后是鼓、钹和三角铁——随即拍起手来，打了一声悠长尖利的呼哨。呼哨声里，乐器纷纷离地而起，开始膨胀变形，直到不再是乐器的模样，而成了一个个的人，只是每一件都以一种奇怪的方式保留着原先的形状。笛子们又高又瘦，它们和小提琴一起变成了脖颈纤长的美丽少女；小号们成了高矮不一的男人和男孩；最古怪的要数那面鼓，它变成了一个腿极短的胖子。月亮已经升起，奥特玛把他们看得清清楚楚；他虽然怕得发抖，心怦怦直跳，眼睛却还是盯着看。他们一群怪人默默站了一会儿，矮人便挥起手臂喊道：“哎！都到齐了吗，我的孩子们？——好，一、二、三、四、五，六、七、八，九、十、十一、十二，十三——不错。来，练习，练习，练习，练完了让你们玩个游戏，看看奥特玛最爱谁，看他先亲谁。”

===Original===
Then they all began to sing together, but each voice was like the sound of its own instrument, only it said words through its tones, and in Othmar’s ears their music sounded as never music had sounded before. The voices of the violin girls were so sweet that he felt as if he must weep to hear them, while the sound of the pipes and trumpets filled him with longing to go into the world and fight and win battles. He sat on the ground and listened to them like one in a trance, and he felt as if he never wished to rise or go away again. The dwarf sat on a hillock near, and did not seem to heed them much. When Othmar took his eyes from the dancers for a minute, he found that the place was quite full of all the animals who are never seen by day, but who fly out by night. There were crowds of bats and owls, and odd moths, all poised in the air, and seeming to watch the musicians and listen to their singing. Then when he looked on the ground, he saw that strange wood-snakes and toads had come out boldly, and with their heads turned towards the dancers remained motionless and watched them, whilst near him green and brown lizards lay still as stone, with heads on one side, all staring through the dusky night at the singers. Othmar thought he only had watched them for a few minutes, when suddenly the dwarf cried out﻿—“The dawn, the dawn, my children; see, there is red in the sky. Come, be quick, see who will win Othmar’s gift before we go on our way.” When he was silent all the singing girls approached Othmar, but before the others came one who looked slighter and younger, and whose voice though as sweet was weaker.

===Chinese===
于是他们齐声唱了起来，可每个嗓音都像它原本那件乐器的声音，只是那声音里吐出了字句；在奥特玛耳中，这音乐是世上从未有过的。小提琴少女们的声音那么甜，他听着几乎要落下泪来；牧笛和小号的声音又让他满心渴望，想投奔到世上去驰骋厮杀、赢得战役。他坐在地上听她们唱，像入了迷一样，觉得自己再也不想站起来、再也不想离开了。矮人坐在近旁的小土丘上，似乎并不大留意。奥特玛把目光从跳舞的人身上移开片刻，发现这地方已聚满了白日绝见不到、只在夜间飞出来的动物：成群的蝙蝠和猫头鹰，还有怪模怪样的飞蛾，全都悬在半空，仿佛在望着乐师们、听他们歌唱。再低头看地上，只见怪异的林蛇和蟾蜍也大着胆子出来了，头朝着跳舞的人们，一动不动地凝望；他身旁绿的和棕的蜥蜴静得像石头，个个歪着头，穿过昏暗的夜色盯着唱歌的人。奥特玛以为自己只看了几分钟，矮人忽然喊道——“天亮了，天亮了，孩子们；瞧，天上发红了。来，快些，趁我们上路之前，看看谁先赢得奥特玛的礼物。”他一住口，唱歌的少女们便都朝奥特玛走来，走在众人前头的是一个看上去更纤细、更年少的，她的嗓音虽同样甜美，却更单薄。

===Original===
“See, Othmar,” she cried, “before we go on and leave you, let us try to sing one song together. Sing you as I do.” And Othmar sang with her as she sang, in a clear voice like a bird’s﻿—

===Chinese===
“瞧，奥特玛，”她喊道，“在我们继续赶路、离开你之前，让我们一起合着唱一支歌吧。你照着我唱。”于是奥特玛随她唱起来，用清亮的、鸟儿一般的嗓音唱道——

===Original===
“Ere the sun shines in the sky,

We will sing together, my love and I;

But none shall hear him sing again,

’Neath moon or sun, in shine or rain.”

===Chinese===
“趁太阳还未照上天空，

我与我的爱人要一同歌唱；

只是从此再无人能听他歌唱，

无论月下日下，晴天雨天。”

===Original===
And then ere the last notes had left Othmar’s mouth, she bent forward, and clasped him in her slender arms, and kissed him on the lips while still they were open to sing.

===Chinese===
最后一个音还未离开奥特玛的唇，她便俯身向前，用纤细的手臂搂住他，趁他张着嘴歌唱时，在他的唇上亲了一吻。

===Original===
“Goodbye, Othmar,” cried she, “and that will be your last note for many a long year, for surely you will have no need to sing after I am gone,” and at that all the strange folk standing near gave a laugh that was more a chord of music than a laugh. And when her lips touched Othmar’s he quivered all over as a fiddle-string does when the bow is drawn across it; and he gave a cry which was like the sweet sound of a bell.

===Chinese===
“再见了，奥特玛，”她喊道，“那便是你往后许多许多年的最后一个音了，因为我走了以后，你必定再也不用唱歌了。”她周围站着的怪人听了，齐声发出一阵大笑，那笑声不像笑，倒像一串和音。她的唇一触到奥特玛的唇，他便浑身一颤，像琴弓拉过时琴弦的颤动；他发出一声叫喊，那声音像钟声一般清甜。

===Original===
“Mine, mine!” cried the girl, as he fell back from her frightened. “Now my voice will be the sweetest and best of all, for I have got Othmar’s too. No one will hear Othmar now﻿—Othmar who sang like the birds. And never will he call the birds again, but I can sing as he sang, and all who hear me will think that Othmar sings too. Rejoice, my sisters, sing and rejoice,” but at that moment the dwarf started up crying out﻿—

===Chinese===
“我的，我的！”少女喊道，奥特玛吓得直往后退。“如今我的嗓音是所有里面最甜美、最好的了，因为连奥特玛的我也拿到了。再没有人听得见奥特玛了——那个唱歌像鸟儿一样的奥特玛。他再也不能唤鸟儿来了，可我能照他那样唱，听见我唱的人都会以为奥特玛也在唱呢。欢喜吧，姐妹们，唱吧，欢喜吧。”可就在这时，矮人跳起来喊道——

===Original===
“The dawn, the dawn, my children; see, the sun, the sun; beware, beware its rays.” Then came a great burst of sound like a chord from all the music folk, followed by a flash of light like lightning, and when it had cleared away, the singing men and girls had gone, and in their places there lay upon the ground all the musical instruments﻿—fiddles, viols, pipes, horns, and cymbals. Othmar stood staring as if he had been turned to stone, and watched as if he were in a dream, while the little man quietly packed the instruments on to the mule, and went away leading it by the bridle as he had come.

===Chinese===
“天亮了，天亮了，孩子们；瞧，太阳，太阳；当心，当心它的光线。”随即传来一阵轰然巨响，像所有乐人奏出的一记和音，接着闪电般一道亮光；亮光散去，唱歌的男人和少女们都不见了，原地的地上躺着一件件乐器——提琴、维奥尔琴、牧笛、号角和钹。奥特玛站在那里呆望着，像变成了石头，像在梦里一般，看着小个子不声不响把乐器捆回骡背上，像来时那样牵着笼头走了。

===Original===
“Goodbye, Othmar,” he called back, “goodbye. When you hear my fiddles again, they will be sweeter than ever, for I have added your voice to them.” And he went on his way over the hillside and disappeared beyond the ridge. Othmar ran after him, but he stumbled and fell. He tried to call out, but no voice would come! Tears ran down his cheeks, and he sobbed bitterly, but no sound came with the sobs, and he knew that his voice had left him. The singing girl had stolen it, and he could never sing or cry out again!

===Chinese===
“再见了，奥特玛，”他回头喊道，“再见吧。等你再听见我的提琴，它们会比从前更甜美，因为我把你的嗓音添进去了。”他沿着山坡走自己的路，翻过山脊不见了。奥特玛拔腿追去，却绊倒在地。他想喊，却发不出一点声音！眼泪顺着脸颊淌下来，他呜呜地哭，哭声里却带不出一点声响，他明白：他的嗓音离开他了。是那唱歌的少女把它偷走了，他再也不能唱歌、不能再喊出声了！

===Original===
The sun was rising high in the heavens. The green lizards, slowworms, frogs and beetles were still ranged around, and gazed at Othmar with their heads wonderingly on one side. The birds sang louder and louder, and their voices sounded sweet in the morning air. Othmar bent his head and wept because he knew that never could he call them to him again. Then from behind a bush there rose a big black raven, who cast a long shadow behind him which almost covered Othmar as he sat, and it gave a deep croak and then spoke quite clearly﻿—

===Chinese===
太阳高高地升上天去。绿的蜥蜴、蛇蜥、青蛙和甲虫仍旧环列四周，歪着头，诧异地望着奥特玛。鸟儿越唱越响，它们的嗓音在清晨的空气里听来那么甜美。奥特玛低下头哭了，因为他知道自己再也不能把它们唤到身边来了。这时，一丛灌木后面飞起一只大黑渡鸦（raven），它身后拖下一道长长的影子，几乎罩住了坐在那里的奥特玛。它低低地呱了一声，接着清清楚楚地说起话来——

===Original===
“Poor Othmar!” it said, “she has stolen your voice!” and he hopped down. “You will never speak nor sing again. Poor Othmar!﻿—ah! they stole my voice too; once I could sing far better than the birds you hear now. That was thousands of years ago, but the dwarf came to my nest, and told me if I would go with him he would teach me how to whistle so that the worms should rise out of the ground and jump into my mouth when they heard me, and he called one of his trumpet-men to teach me﻿—one you saw dance﻿—and he bid me lay my beak below his lips while he sang; then he stole my voice, all but a croak, which he did not want because it was so harsh, but all your voice was sweet, therefore she has got it all﻿—poor Othmar, poor Othmar!”

===Chinese===
“可怜的奥特玛！”它说，“她把你的嗓音偷走了！”它跳了下来。“你再也说不了话、唱不了歌了。可怜的奥特玛！——唉，他们也偷过我的嗓音；从前我唱得比你现在听见的这些鸟儿好得多呢。那是几千年前的事了。矮人到我的巢里来，说只要我跟他走，他就教我吹口哨，吹得蚯蚓一听见就会从地里钻出来、蹦进我的嘴里；他叫来一个小号手教我——就是你看见跳舞的那些里的一个——他叫我趁他唱着的时候，把喙凑在他的唇下；然后他就偷走了我的嗓音，只给我留下一声呱叫，因为那声音太粗粝，他不要；可你的嗓音全是甜的，所以她把它整个儿拿去了——可怜的奥特玛，可怜的奥特玛！”

===Original===
Then Othmar raised himself, with the tears running from his eyes, and turned to find his way back to the village. It seemed a long distance, for he missed his path, and it was near nightfall before he saw the tops of the cottages and his own little home; but as he neared the village, he could see Hulda standing in the road, shading her eyes from the sun, and watching the way he came.

===Chinese===
于是奥特玛站起来，眼泪汪汪地转身找路回村。这条路显得很长很长，因为他走错了道，直到快天黑才望见村里的茅屋顶和自己小小的家。可就在他快到村子时，他看见胡尔达站在路上，手搭凉棚挡着太阳，朝他来的方向张望。

===Original===
“Othmar,” she cried when she saw him, “is it you? I have been to search for you far and near, and there are others now looking for you, for we were afraid lest you had fallen down some crevice, or slipped over the rocks.”

===Chinese===
“奥特玛，”她一见他便喊道，“真是你吗？我到处找你，远近都找遍了，如今还有别人在找，我们都怕你跌进了哪条石缝，或是滑下岩石摔了。”

===Original===
Othmar came up to her, and put out his hand, and she saw how pale he was, and that his eyes were full of tears, but he said nothing.

===Chinese===
奥特玛走到她跟前，伸出手来。她看见他脸色多么苍白，眼里满是泪水，可他一言不发。

===Original===
“Othmar, tell me,” cried Hulda; “what has happened? why don’t you speak?” but still Othmar was silent. “Are you hurt, Othmar? Did the dwarf do you any harm?”

===Chinese===
“奥特玛，告诉我，”胡尔达喊道，“出什么事了？你怎么不说话？”奥特玛仍旧不作声。“你受伤了吗，奥特玛？是不是那矮人害了你？”

===Original===
Then Othmar flung himself on the ground, and began to sob, but his sobs gave no sound, though the ground was wet with his tears, and Hulda knew that Othmar was dumb.

===Chinese===
奥特玛扑倒在地上呜呜地哭，可他的哭喊发不出一点声音，虽然泪水把地面都打湿了。胡尔达明白了：奥特玛失声了。

===Original===
“Poor Othmar, poor Othmar!” croaked the raven who had kept close to Othmar, and flew overhead, but Hulda did not understand it, only she wept to see his grief.

===Chinese===
“可怜的奥特玛，可怜的奥特玛！”那只一直紧随着奥特玛、在他头顶盘旋的渡鸦呱呱叫道。可胡尔达听不懂它的话，只是看见他这样悲伤，也跟着落泪。

===Original===
“Never fear, Othmar,” she said tenderly, “your voice will soon come back; it was the long cold night, and the fear that has driven it away. Come home with me, and let me nurse you, and you shall soon be well.”

===Chinese===
“别怕，奥特玛，”她柔声说，“你的嗓音很快会回来的；是那漫长寒冷的夜，是那惊吓把它吓跑了。跟我回家去，让我来照看你，你很快就会好起来的。”

===Original===
Othmar shook his head, and the tears fell from his eyes, but he let her take his hand and lead him into the village where his old mother sat and waited for him; but still, although she sprang forward to greet him, and put her arms around his neck, he could not speak, and his deep sobs gave no sound. At first the villagers said he was ill, and soon he would be well again, but as the days passed and he never spoke, they knew that he was struck dumb. Some said it was the cold, and some that he had been frightened; only Hulda said to herself, “it was the wicked little man.”

===Chinese===
奥特玛摇了摇头，泪水从眼中落下，可他还是让她牵着手，领他进了村。他的老母亲正坐在家里等他；虽然她扑上前来迎接他，搂住他的脖子，他仍旧说不出话，他深重的呜咽发不出一点声音。起初村里人都说他病了，很快就会好；可日子一天天过去，他始终一言不发，他们才知道他是被夺去嗓音了。有人说是冻的，有人说是受了惊吓；只有胡尔达心里说：“是那个狠毒的小个子干的。”

===Original===
So the days passed, and Othmar remained silent and worked with the other young men of the village without speaking, and no longer could he sing or call the birds to him. Always he looked white and sad, but saddest of all when there was any village merrymaking, and the villagers sang and danced together. Then when he heard them he would put his fingers in his ears and hide his eyes so as not to see them and run afar off by himself; for the sound of any music was quite horrible to him after the singing of the travelling musicians. So a year passed, and Othmar never spoke, and instead of calling him the bird-boy, the village people called him “dumb Othmar.”

===Chinese===
日子就这样过去，奥特玛默默无言，同村里别的年轻人一起干活，却从不开口，再也不能唱歌，再也不能唤鸟儿来了。他总是面色苍白、神情悲伤，最悲伤的莫过于村里有欢庆、大家又唱又跳的时候。一听见歌声乐声，他便捂住耳朵、遮住眼睛，不愿看他们，独自跑到远远的地方去；因为听过那班流浪乐师的演奏之后，任何音乐的声音在他听来都可怕极了。就这样过了一年，奥特玛始终不说话，村里人不再叫他“鸟孩子”，改口叫他“失声的奥特玛”了。

===Original===
It was midsummer-night, and the villagers had been having a merrymaking and dancing cheerily on the green in the village. Othmar was not with them; he had left the village and went and sat apart on the top of a rocky hill, from where afar the sea could be seen when the weather was clear. The moon was wonderfully bright, and the country was almost as light as by day. Othmar could hear the sound of their laughter, but he never laughed, and as he sat with his head bowed upon his knees he wept silently. So he remained alone till far into the night when all the singing and dancing was done, and the villagers had gone home, but just when the clocks struck twelve he saw Hulda, who came slowly to him, and he saw that she too was crying.

===Chinese===
那是仲夏夜，村民们一直在村里的草地上欢庆、快活地跳舞。奥特玛没有同他们在一起；他离开村子，独自坐在一座岩石山顶上，天气晴朗的时候，从那里远远可以望见大海。月亮亮得出奇，四下里亮得几乎同白昼一样。奥特玛听得见他们的笑声，可他从不笑；他低头伏在膝上，默默地流泪。他就这样独自待到深夜，歌舞都散了，村民们都回家了；可正当钟敲十二下的时候，他看见胡尔达慢慢朝他走来，他看见她也在哭。

===Original===
“Othmar,” she said, “I have thought and thought, and I know that the little man with the fiddles was a wicked fairy.” Othmar nodded. “So I am going into the big world to find him, for if he has done you this ill he will know how to cure you, and I have saved all my money for a year.”

===Chinese===
“奥特玛，”她说，“我想了又想，我知道了：那个带提琴的小个子，是个狠毒的仙子。”奥特玛点了点头。“所以我要到广阔的世界上找他去。既然是他害你遭了这祸，他必定知道怎么解救你。这一年，我攒下了所有的钱。”

===Original===
Then Othmar took her hand, and kissed it, but still wept, as he shook his head and made signs to her that she must not go, as it would be all in vain. But Hulda did not heed him.

===Chinese===
奥特玛握住她的手，吻了吻，可他仍旧流着泪，摇着头，做手势叫她别去，说去了也是白费。胡尔达却不理会。

===Original===
“And now,” she said, “I am going, Othmar, and it may be long years before I return, so you must do three things. First, you must give me a long curl of your brown hair, that I may lay it next my heart and wear it day and night, not to forget you. Then you must kiss me on my lips to say goodbye; and then you must promise that my name shall be the first words your lips say when they again can speak.” Then Othmar took his knife and cut from his head the longest, brightest curl of his hair, and drew her to him and kissed her thrice upon the lips, and then he took her hand and with it wrote upon his lips her name, “Hulda,” as a promise that her name should be the first thing they said.

===Chinese===
“如今，”她说，“我要走了，奥特玛，也许要过许多许多年我才能回来，所以你要为我做三件事。第一，把你棕色鬈发里最长的一绺剪给我，让我贴着心口戴上，日夜不离，好不忘掉你。第二，你要亲亲我的嘴唇，作为道别。第三，你要答应我：等你的嘴再能说话时，最先说出的，便是我的名字。”于是奥特玛取过刀子，从头上剪下最长最亮的一绺头发，把她拉到跟前，在她唇上亲了三下，然后拉起她的手，用它在自己的唇上写下她的名字——“胡尔达”，以此许诺：他的嘴最先要说的，便是这个名字。

===Original===
“Goodbye, Othmar,” she said; “you will wait for me.” Then she turned away and started alone to go down the mountainside, and she looked back as she went and called back, “Goodbye, Othmar,” as long as he could see or hear her.

===Chinese===
“再见了，奥特玛，”她说，“你会等我的。”她转身独自下山去了，一路走一路回头，只要他还望得见、听得见，她便回头喊：“再见了，奥特玛。”

===Original===
She went straight down the hill and journeyed for a long way, till the dawn began to show red in the sky, and she lay under a tree and slept soundly till the sun had risen and woke her.

===Chinese===
她径直下了山，走了很远很远，直到天边泛起红色曙光，才躺到一棵树下，沉沉睡去，一觉睡到太阳升起把她唤醒。

===Original===
She sat and thought which way she should go. “I must seek out some wise man who knows about fairies and wicked witches,” she said to herself, “and who will tell me where to search. And I will ask everyone I meet where the wisest person is to be found.” So she went on for many days till she came to a tiny village, outside which, in a field, she saw a shepherd minding sheep. Hulda stopped and asked if he could tell her where she could find a very wise man who could answer her question.

===Chinese===
她坐下来想该往哪边走。“我得去寻一位通晓仙子和恶女巫的智者，”她自言自语道，“让他告诉我该往哪里找。我还要见人就问，哪里能找到最有智慧的人。”她这样走了许多天，来到一个小小的村子。村外一块地里，她看见一个牧人在放羊。胡尔达停下脚，问他能否告诉她，哪里能找到一位极有智慧、能解答她问题的人。

===Original===
The shepherd thought a bit, and then he said, “The wisest man in these parts lives up in the little cottage on the other side of the village. He cured my sheep two years back when all the flock were sick and many died﻿—a little cottage with a red gate.” Hulda thanked the shepherd, and went on till she came to the little cottage with the red gate. When she had knocked at the door a tall man came out, and she asked him if ’twas he who had cured the shepherd’s sheep, and as he was so clever, if he would tell her what to do. She told him she wanted to find a dwarf who led a donkey covered with musical instruments, and whom she knew to be a wicked sorcerer, since he struck folk dumb.

===Chinese===
牧人想了一会儿，说：“这一带最有智慧的人，住在村子那边的小屋里。两年前我的羊群全病了、死了许多，是他把它们治好的——一座红门的小屋。”胡尔达谢过牧人，继续往前走，来到那座红门小屋前。她敲了门，一个高个子男人出来。她问他，是不是他治好了牧人的羊；既然他这样有本事，能不能告诉她该怎么办。她说她要找一个矮人，他牵着一头驮满乐器的驴，她认定他是个狠毒的妖术师，因为他叫人失声。

===Original===
The tall man looked at her and said, “My business is to cure sheep, cows, and horses, and I know it right well; but I know nothing of dwarfs and witches, and how can I tell you which way he has gone, or anything about him?”

===Chinese===
高个子男人看着她说：“我的本事是医治羊、牛和马，这个我在行；矮人和女巫，我是一窍不通，我怎么告诉你他往哪儿去了，又怎么说他呢？”

===Original===
“Then of whom had I best ask?” said Hulda. “Tell me who is the wisest and most learned man in these parts, and I will go to him.”

===Chinese===
“那我最好去问谁呢？”胡尔达说，“请告诉我这一带谁最聪明、最有学问，我就去找他。”

===Original===
The tall man rubbed his head and considered. “I suppose,” quoth he, “that the old schoolmaster at the village school yonder would be said to be the most learned man hereabouts, for he teaches the children all sorts of things that they forget when they grow up. That is the schoolhouse on the hill.” So on went Hulda again to the school.

===Chinese===
高个子男人挠着头想了想。“我看，”他说，“大家会说，那边村里学堂的老塾师要算这一带最有学问的人了，因为他教孩子们各种各样的学问，等他们长大了又全都忘光。山上的那座就是学堂。”于是胡尔达又往学堂去了。

===Original===
As she came near she could hear the children calling out their lessons, and their master, who was an old priest, teaching them. So she waited about till school hours were over, and the children had all come out, and then she timidly went in and curtseyed to the old schoolmaster, and told him her tale, and asked him, as he was so very learned, if he would advise her what to do; but instead of answering her the old man at first stared at her in bewilderment, and then he said, “I can teach you to read and write and many wonderful things, but of dwarfs who can steal a boy’s voice I know nothing. You would do best not to think more of it.”

===Chinese===
她走近时，听得见孩子们诵功课的声音，还有他们的老师——一位老神父——在教他们。她一直等到放学、孩子们都出来了，才怯生生地走进去，向老塾师行了个屈膝礼，把自己的遭遇讲给他听，请他既然这样有学问，就指点她该怎么办。可老头儿没有回答她，先是愣愣地盯着她看，然后说：“我能教你读书写字，教你许多奇妙的东西；可会偷人嗓音的矮人，我一无所知。你最好别再想这件事了。”

===Original===
“But someone there must be,” cried Hulda, beginning to cry, “who can tell me what to do, and which way to go. For I am sure that the old man was a fairy, and if so, no living man can help Othmar, but only he who did the mischief can undo it.”

===Chinese===
“可总该有一个人啊，”胡尔达喊道，哭了起来，“能告诉我该怎么办、该往哪儿走。因为我确信那老头儿是个仙子，既然如此，世上活人谁都救不了奥特玛，只有闯了这祸的人自己才能解开它。”

===Original===
The old priest looked at her sadly and shook his head. “My child,” he said, “this is a foolish talk about fairies and sorcerers, I know nothing of such things. It is only untaught folk and fools who give heed to such matters.”

===Chinese===
老神父忧伤地看着她，摇了摇头。“孩子，”他说，“仙子呀妖术师呀，这些都是蠢话，这样的事我一概不知。只有没受过教化的愚人和傻瓜，才会把这种事放在心上。”

===Original===
“To untaught folk and fools then I must go, for surely they can help me more than the wise,” she cried. So she left the schoolhouse, and started again through the little village street. The first person she met was a baker going home after taking round his bread, and she stopped him and asked him who was the most ignorant and foolish person in those parts.

===Chinese===
“那我就去找没受过教化的愚人和傻瓜，”她喊道，“他们准能比智者帮我更多。”于是她离开学堂，又沿着小村街往前走。她遇见的第一个人是个面包师，送完了面包正要回家。她拦住他，问他这一带谁最无知、最傻。

===Original===
The baker stood and stared at her, and seemed to be half angry. At last he said, “I am sure I don’t know anything about fools. You had better go on to the cake-maker, who lives a mile up the hill. He is, to my mind, the biggest fool in these parts.” And tossing his basket about and seeming to be much offended, he went his way. Hulda went on for a mile up the hill, and there she found a little group of cottages, and in their midst was a shop with an open oven, and she could see its owner busy making cakes and sweets. Hulda went in and bought a cake, and as she sat and ate it, she asked the man timidly if he knew many of the people in that neighbourhood, and if any were very ignorant and foolish.

===Chinese===
面包师站着直瞪她，似乎有几分恼了。末了他说：“傻瓜的事我一概不知。你最好再往前走，去找做糕点的，他住在往山上去约一公里半的地方。依我看，他是这一带头一号的大傻瓜。”他把篮子一甩，像是大大地生了气，径自走了。胡尔达往山上走了约一公里半，来到一小片村舍中间，有一家带敞口烤炉的铺子，看得见店主正忙着做糕点糖果。胡尔达进去买了一块糕，坐着吃的时候，怯生生地问他认不认识这一带的许多人，其中有没有非常无知、非常傻的。

===Original===
“Indeed,” cried the man, “you may well ask that. Why, a more silly, ignorant set of folk I never knew, quite different from the people in my native town, but that is miles away.”

===Chinese===
“可不是，”那人喊道，“你算问着了。嘿，这样一群又蠢又无知的家伙，我从没见过，跟我老家的人全不一样——不过那隔着好几里地呢。”

===Original===
“And who do you think the silliest then?” asked Hulda.

===Chinese===
“那么你觉得谁最傻呢？”胡尔达问。

===Original===
“Why, for sure ’tis hard to say,” said the man, scratching his head. “They’re such a poor silly lot, right away from the Mayor down to Tommy the fool.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，这可难说，”那人挠着头说，“这帮人蠢得一个赛一个，从镇长（the Mayor）一路往下，直到傻瓜汤米（Tommy the fool）。”

===Original===
“And who is Tommy the fool?” asked Hulda eagerly.

===Chinese===
“傻瓜汤米是谁？”胡尔达急切地问。

===Original===
“ ’Tis a poor natural-born idiot who lives with his mother in the little cabin on the side of the common. He spends all his time trying to catch a bird, and he never has caught one, and never will.”

===Chinese===
“是个天生的可怜白痴，跟他娘住在荒地边上的一间小破屋里。他一天到晚就想逮住一只鸟，可从来一只也没逮着过，往后也逮不着。”
===Original===
“Thank you for telling me about him,” said Hulda, rising to go away. “Maybe if he is really a fool he could answer my questions as they say,” and she went on again with a lighter heart. At last she came to the common on which the fool lived with his mother. When she approached the little cabin, she saw someone dancing about in front of an oak, dressed up with the feathers of birds and fowls, which looked as if they had been picked up from the ground. He was a young man of about eighteen, and he had a cheerful face, but anyone looking at him could see at once he was an idiot. He was dancing round the tree and pointing up to the birds, and calling them to come down to him. Hulda came up and stood quite close and watched him, as he ran round smiling and giggling. Then she said, “Please can you tell me where I shall find a little man, a dwarf who drives a donkey covered with pipes and fiddles?”

===Chinese===
“谢谢你告诉我他的事，”胡尔达说着起身要走，“要是他真是个傻瓜，说不定倒能像人们说的那样，解答我的问题呢。”她又往前走去，心里轻快了些。她终于来到那片荒地，傻瓜同他娘就住在那里。她走近小屋时，看见一个人在一棵橡树前手舞足蹈，浑身插着鸟羽鸡毛，那羽毛瞧着像是从地上捡来的。他是个十八岁上下的青年，一张快活脸，可谁一看便知是个白痴。他绕着树又蹦又跳，指着树上的鸟儿，唤它们下来。胡尔达走上前去，站在近旁看他咧着嘴、嘻嘻笑着团团转。然后她说：“请问，你能告诉我上哪儿能找到一个小个子、一个牵驴的矮人吗？那驴身上驮满了牧笛和提琴。”

===Original===
The fool looked at her very gravely, but he said nothing; so then she went on to tell him how the little man had come to their village, and how he had stolen Othmar’s voice, and how she had come out to seek it. Just as she finished speaking, there rose from the ground a raven, and soared above their heads. When he saw it the fool pointed to it, and cried out, “The raven, the raven, follow the raven,” and as the raven flew, he ran after him with Hulda following in turn. They ran for a long way, the fool leaping and bounding, and pointing with his finger and crying, “The raven, the raven, do what the raven does.” Then suddenly he turned, giving a wild laugh, and began to run home again, but as he went he nodded and called to Hulda, “Follow the raven, follow it, do what the raven does.”

===Chinese===
傻瓜神情很郑重地看着她，却一言不发；于是她接着讲下去，讲那小个子怎样来到他们村子，怎样偷走了奥特玛的嗓音，她又怎样出来寻找。她的话音刚落，地里飞起一只渡鸦，在他们头顶盘旋。傻瓜一见便指着他喊：“渡鸦，渡鸦，跟着渡鸦走！”渡鸦往哪儿飞，他就跟在后面跑，胡尔达又跟着他。他们跑了很远，傻瓜连蹦带跳，用手指着，嘴里喊着：“渡鸦，渡鸦，渡鸦做什么你就做什么！”忽然他转过身，狂笑一声，撒腿往家跑了，一边跑一边朝胡尔达点头喊：“跟着渡鸦，跟着它，渡鸦做什么你就做什么。”

===Original===
Hulda felt inclined to burst into tears with disappointment, but still she ran meekly after the bird, murmuring to herself, “He said follow the raven, but what good can that do me?” But when the fool had turned back, the raven slackened his pace, and cawed and lighted on a tree, and Hulda, panting for breath, sat down under it, and looked up at it.

===Chinese===
胡尔达失望得几乎放声大哭，可她还是乖乖地跟在那鸟后面跑，一路喃喃自语：“他说跟着渡鸦走，可那对我有什么用呢？”不过傻瓜一回头走，渡鸦便放慢了速度，呱呱叫着落在一棵树上。胡尔达跑得上气不接下气，在树下坐下来，抬头望着它。

===Original===
“Poor Hulda!” it croaked, but she couldn’t understand it; “poor Hulda, come with me, and I will show you where the dwarf is.” Then it began to fly slowly on again.

===Chinese===
“可怜的胡尔达！”它呱声说道，可她听不懂；“可怜的胡尔达，跟我来，我带你到矮人那里去。”说完它又慢慢往前飞了。

===Original===
“What shall I do?” sobbed Hulda. “He was only an idiot, he knew nothing; still he told me to follow the raven, and no one else has told me anything;” so on she went, and this time the raven flew quite slowly, so that Hulda kept up with it walking. On they travelled till evening was well advanced, into all sorts of places where Hulda had never been, and through many villages. Then it began to grow dark, and the moon came out, but still they travelled on. Hulda was footsore and weary, but she would not give up, and said to herself, “It was what the fool said, ‘Follow the raven, do what the raven does!’ ”

===Chinese===
“我该怎么办呢？”胡尔达抽泣着说，“他不过是个白痴，什么都不懂；可他叫我跟着渡鸦走，别人又谁也没给过我一点主意。”于是她继续前行。这一回渡鸦飞得很慢，胡尔达走着便能跟上。他们一直走到暮色四合，走过胡尔达从未到过的各种地方，穿过许多村子。天黑了，月亮出来了，他们还在赶路。胡尔达脚底疼痛，浑身困乏，却不肯罢休，自言自语道：“傻瓜说的就是这句：‘跟着渡鸦，渡鸦做什么你就做什么！’”

===Original===
Just before dawn, they came out on to a big plain, where there were neither houses nor trees, but in the far distance you could see a long line of mountains; a little further in the centre of the plain Hulda saw a little dark mass, and straight to this the raven flew, and as Hulda approached it, she saw that it was the little dwarf, lying asleep upon the ground beside his heap of musical instruments, with the mule grazing near. “Oh, good wise fool,” cried Hulda, “now indeed you have given me the best advice. Since the raven has led me to the wicked dwarf, now indeed will I do what the raven does, whatever it be.”

===Chinese===
天快亮时，他们来到一片大平原上，那里既没有房屋也没有树木，只有极远处望得见一线山脉。平原中央稍往前一点，胡尔达看见一小团黑影，渡鸦径直朝那儿飞去。胡尔达走近一看，正是那小矮人，躺在地上熟睡，身旁堆着他的乐器，骡子在近处吃草。“哦，善良聪明的傻瓜！”胡尔达喊道，“如今你果真给了我最好的指点。渡鸦既然把我引到这狠毒的矮人跟前，如今我果真要渡鸦做什么就做什么，不管那是什么。”

===Original===
The raven flew on, and lit upon a scrubby bush, a little way from the sleeping dwarf, and Hulda followed and crouched beside it, making no noise lest she should disturb the sleeper, and hiding behind the branches so that she could not be seen. Presently the little man rose from the ground, and called out, “Come, my children, practice, practice; the dawn is here, and the sun will rise, and then we must go upon our way.”

===Chinese===
渡鸦往前飞去，落在离酣睡的矮人不远的一丛矮灌木上。胡尔达跟过去，蹲在树丛旁，不敢弄出一点声响，怕惊醒睡着的人，又躲到枝条后面，免得被人瞧见。不一会儿，小个子从地上起来，喊道：“来吧，孩子们，练习，练习；天亮了，太阳要出来了，到时我们该上路了。”

===Original===
Then Hulda saw what Othmar had seen before. The pipes raised themselves from the ground, and untwisted, and became tall, lithe men; some gradually uncurled themselves, and put forth long arms and became beautiful girls, till each instrument had taken the likeness of a human being. Then they began to dance and to sing, and Hulda watched them as Othmar had watched them, and she too felt as if she had never seen and heard anything so beautiful in her life, and she longed to rush to them, but she heard the raven croak above her, and remembered the words of the fool, “The raven, the raven, do what the raven does.” Then she saw that the raven had hopped off the tree, and was standing upon the earth in front of her, and was beginning to dig in the earth with its long beak, as if it would find a worm. “The fool said, Do what the raven does, so must I dig too,” thought she, and she began to scoop the brown earth with her hand, till she had made a hole, watching the raven all the time.

===Chinese===
胡尔达于是看见了奥特玛先前看见的景象。牧笛们从地上立起，舒展开来，变成高大柔韧的男人；有一些慢慢舒卷开来，伸出长长的手臂，变成美丽的少女，直到每一件乐器都化成了人的模样。他们开始又跳又唱，胡尔达望着他们，正如奥特玛当初望着他们一样，她也觉得自己一生从未见过、听过这样美的景象，恨不得冲到他们中间去；可她听见渡鸦在头顶呱地一叫，想起了傻瓜的话：“渡鸦，渡鸦，渡鸦做什么你就做什么。”这时她看见渡鸦已跳下树来，立在她面前的地上，正用长喙刨土，像要找一条虫子。“傻瓜说，渡鸦做什么我就做什么，那我也得刨。”她想着，便用手一下一下掏那褐色的泥土，眼睛始终盯着渡鸦，直到挖出一个坑来。

===Original===
Presently she saw that the raven had found a long worm, and held it by its throat in the air, but it did not swallow it. Hulda looked into her hole to see if there was a worm there also, and at the bottom she saw lying a long, lithe, green snake, twisted up and apparently torpid. “Surely,” thought Hulda, “if I do what the raven does, I shall take this out,” and she put her hand into the hole, and grasped the snake by its throat, though she was very much afraid of it, and then she crouched down behind the raven and the bush.

===Chinese===
不多时，她看见渡鸦找到了一条长蚯蚓，叼着它的喉咙提在半空，却不吞下去。胡尔达往自己的坑里看，看那里是不是也有一条虫，只见坑底盘着一条细长碧绿的蛇，蜷作一团，像是僵睡未醒。“当然，”胡尔达想，“如果我照渡鸦做的做，我就该把它拿出来。”她把手伸进坑里，一把攥住蛇的喉咙，虽然心里怕极了，然后蹲到渡鸦和树丛后面。

===Original===
“Come,” cried the dwarf, when they had all sung together, “now let us hear the last new voice. Othmar’s voice was as sweet as silver. Now let me hear how my youngest daughter has treated it.” Then there came to the front the youngest and fairest of the girls, and began to sing, and when she heard it, Hulda could scarcely keep from screaming, for she recognized that the tones were Othmar’s; but just as the singing sounded the sweetest, the raven with a croak opened his mouth, and dropped the worm upon the ground, and Hulda let go her hold of the bright green snake, which darted through the short grass towards the dancers.

===Chinese===
“来，”他们齐声唱罢，矮人喊道，“现在让我们听听最新的嗓子吧。奥特玛的嗓音甜得像银子。让我听听，我的小女儿把它调理得怎么样了。”于是那些少女中最年幼、最俊美的一个走到前面，唱了起来。胡尔达一听见，几乎忍不住要叫出声来，因为她听出那声调正是奥特玛的；可正当那歌唱到最甜美的时候，渡鸦呱的一声张开嘴，把蚯蚓丢在地上，胡尔达也松开了那条亮闪闪的绿蛇。蛇嗖地穿过短草，直朝跳舞的人们窜去。

===Original===
There arose from all sides the cry of “A snake, a snake!” and they seemed panic-stricken. The snake glided straight towards the singing girl, and deftly coiled itself round her ankles, while from the old man and all the others came a terrible uproar, but the snake from the girl’s ankles had slid up her body in bright green coils, and then twisted itself around her throat, and coiled tighter and tighter and tighter, till her head fell over on one side. Then Hulda heard a noise like the sighing of wind, but sweet and tender, while the dwarf and all the singers were in a hubbub and confusion.

===Chinese===
四面八方响起了喊声：“蛇，蛇！”他们似乎都吓慌了。那蛇径直滑向唱歌的少女，麻利地缠上她的脚踝；老头儿和其余所有人发出一阵可怕的喧哗。可蛇从少女脚踝往上，一圈圈亮绿的身子盘上她的身体，又缠住她的喉咙，越缠越紧、越缠越紧、越缠越紧，直到她的头歪向一边。这时胡尔达听见一阵像风叹息似的声音，却又甜又柔；矮人和所有唱歌的人都乱作一团。

===Original===
For a moment the old man stood motionless, then he rose and gave a terrible cry. Hulda trembled when she heard his voice, it sounded like nothing earthly, but ere he was silent there had risen from the ground and from the bushes near a number of little cloudy forms, black and thick, and twirling in all directions, and they twisted in and out among the singers; and as they twisted among them, they ceased to be men and women, but became musical instruments, as they had been before, all save the girl around whose neck the snake had wound, and who seemed to shrivel and shrivel in its coils till she was no more to be seen.

===Chinese===
老头儿木然站了片刻，随即站起来，发出一声可怕的喊叫。胡尔达听见他的声音直发抖，那声音不像尘世之物；可他话音未落，地上和近旁的灌木丛里已经升起一团团小小的云雾般的东西，又黑又浓，四处旋转，在唱歌的人们中间穿来穿去；它们一穿到他们中间，那些人就不再是男人和女人，而变回了原先的乐器——只有那被蛇缠住脖颈的少女例外，她在蛇的盘绕中越缩越小、越缩越小，直到再也看不见了。

===Original===
In less than a minute they were all packed again upon the mule, and the little old man was leading it quietly away, as if nothing had happened. And upon the grass lay the glittering snake, though all trace of the girl around whose neck it had twisted had gone. Hulda ran to it, and then she almost cried, for she feared that after all the girl with Othmar’s voice had escaped her. But she remembered the words of Tommy the fool, “Do as the raven does; follow the raven,” and looking up she saw that the raven was fluttering above her, with the worm it had picked up from the ground in its mouth.

===Chinese===
不到一分钟，他们又统统捆回了骡背上，小老头儿平平静静地牵着骡子走开，仿佛什么也没发生过。草地上躺着那条闪闪发光的蛇，被它缠过脖颈的少女却踪影全无了。胡尔达跑过去，险些哭出来，因为她怕到头来那个带着奥特玛嗓音的少女还是逃脱了。可她想起傻瓜汤米的话：“渡鸦做什么你就做什么；跟着渡鸦走。”抬头一看，渡鸦正在她头顶扑扇，嘴里衔着从地上拾起的那条蚯蚓。

===Original===
“Oh, dear raven,” cried Hulda, “you brought me to where the little man was, now lead me back and show me what to do next.” And mindful of the fool’s advice, she picked up the snake, and holding it firmly by the throat, turned to follow the raven, who flew ahead of her. Thus they journeyed back, over the same country through which Hulda had come before. All looked just the same, but Hulda was sorely tired, for she had now been walking many days, and she felt sad, for she did not know if after all she had gained anything, or whether she ought not to have followed the little old man, and though she had heard Othmar’s voice, she did not know how she was to get it back to him. “Never mind,” she said to herself, “the fool told me right so far, and evidently he knew all about it, so I had best keep to his advice.”

===Chinese===
“哦，亲爱的渡鸦，”胡尔达喊道，“是你把我带到小个子那里，如今再领我回去，指给我下一步该怎么办吧。”她记着傻瓜的嘱咐，拾起那条蛇，牢牢攥住它的喉咙，转身跟着渡鸦走，渡鸦在她前面飞。就这样，他们沿着胡尔达来时的路往回走。一路景色如旧，可胡尔达疲惫不堪，她已经走了许多天了；她心里也难受，因为不知道自己到底有没有收获，也不知道当初是不是不该跟着那小老头儿走；况且纵然听见了奥特玛的嗓音，她也不知道怎样才能把它还给奥特玛。“不要紧，”她对自己说，“傻瓜到目前为止都说对了，他分明什么都知道，我还是照他的话做最好。”

===Original===
The sun rose high in the sky and the day was very hot, and poor Hulda longed to lie down under the trees and sleep; moreover the snake in her hand twisted and twisted, till she could scarce hold it. Sometimes she cried from very weariness, but still the raven flew in front of her. She had bought dry bread as she came along, and when the raven stopped and hopped upon the ground, she munched it to stave off her hunger, but directly the raven began to fly she followed it, and she never let the snake from her grasp. The sun had set, and dark was all over the land ere she came to the village where the fool lived, but no fool was there to be seen. Hulda sought everywhere, but she could not find him. Then she saw that the raven had stopped and settled on the roof of the cottage where the fool lived, and, standing on one leg, had gone to sleep with its head under its wing, so Hulda lay down by the side of the door, and laying her head on a stone rested too. But first she took off her girdle and tied it firmly round the snake’s throat, and then tied it round her waist again lest she should fall asleep and the snake glide away.

===Chinese===
太阳升得老高，天气很热，可怜的胡尔达多想躺到树底下睡一觉；再说手里的蛇扭来扭去，扭得她几乎攥不住。有时她累得直哭，可渡鸦仍旧在她前面飞。来时她买了干面包，渡鸦停下来在地上跳的时候，她便啃几口面包充饥；渡鸦一飞，她立刻跟上，那只手始终没放过蛇。太阳落了，大地一片黑暗，她才走到傻瓜住的村子，却哪里也找不见傻瓜。胡尔达四处寻找，怎么也找不到。这时她看见渡鸦停下来，落在傻瓜住的小屋屋顶上，单腿立着，把头埋进翅膀里睡了。胡尔达便在门边躺下，头枕着一块石头，也歇息了。不过她先解下腰带，牢牢系在蛇的喉咙上，再把腰带重新系回自己腰间，生怕睡着了蛇滑走。

===Original===
Just when the stars were beginning to look pale, and as there were signs of the dawn in the sky, the door of the cottage opened, and out there came the fool, dressed up as Hulda had seen him before, with feathers and weeds and bits of bright rag. Hulda started up, and he laughed when he saw her. “Look,” he said, “the sun is rising; I am come out to see it.”

===Chinese===
恰在星星开始发白、天边露出黎明迹象的时候，小屋的门开了，傻瓜走了出来，还打扮得同胡尔达先前见的一样，插着羽毛、野草和几片亮布头。胡尔达一跃而起，他见了她就笑。“瞧，”他说，“太阳要出来了，我出来看它升起来。”

===Original===
“I have come back,” cried Hulda, “and I have seen them all﻿—the old man and the musician girls, and the one who stole Othmar’s voice will never use it again, for this snake has throttled her; but what am I to do now? How can I give him back his voice? What shall I do to make him speak?” And as she spoke she took the snake from her bosom and showed it to the fool. He looked at it very gravely as he always did when anything was shown to him, and looked very wise and nodded. “It is a snake,” he said; “perhaps Othmar will like the snake.”

===Chinese===
“我回来了，”胡尔达喊道，“我把他们全都见了——老头儿和那些乐师少女；偷奥特玛嗓音的那个再也不能用它了，这条蛇把她勒死了。可现在我该怎么办呢？我怎么把嗓音还给他呢？我要做什么才能让他开口说话呢？”她一面说，一面从怀里取出蛇给傻瓜看。傻瓜像别人拿东西给他看时一样，神情郑重地看着，显得很有学问，点了点头。“是条蛇，”他说，“说不定奥特玛会喜欢这条蛇。”

===Original===
Hulda begged him to tell her if he knew what she should do, but he would say no more, but began to dance and sing as she had seen him do before. Then at last Hulda burst out crying, “He is nothing but a poor idiot,” she said, “and I have been on a fool’s errand when I did as he told me, though I did see the wicked little man, and this snake did punish the singing girl, so I will take it back to Othmar that he may see I have tried. But now I believe he will be dumb forever.”

===Chinese===
胡尔达求他告诉她该怎么做，如果他知道的话；可他再不肯多说，只管像先前那样跳起舞、唱起来。胡尔达终于放声大哭。“他不过是个可怜的白痴，”她说，“我照他的话做，原是跑了一趟傻瓜的差事；不过我倒真见了那狠毒的小个子，这条蛇也果真惩治了那唱歌的少女。我要把它带回给奥特玛，好让他知道我尽力了。可如今我怕他要一辈子失声了。”

===Original===
And she took the snake and looked at it as she held it. It was very still, and seemed half torpid, though the weather was warm. She saw it was not a common snake, for it was bright brown, and green with odd markings, and it glittered oddly when the sun’s rays touched it.

===Chinese===
她捧起蛇，端详着手里的它。蛇很安静，虽然天气暖和，看上去却半睡半醒。她看出这不是一条寻常的蛇：它亮棕色中透着绿，带着奇异的花纹，阳光照到它身上时，它异样地闪闪发光。

===Original===
“I will go back now,” said Hulda; “I will go back to Othmar, and tell him I have failed, and ask him to forgive my vanity in thinking I could help him. I will go back at once and tell him all.” And overhead the raven croaked and told her to go quickly, but she did not understand what he said.

===Chinese===
“我这就回去，”胡尔达说，“我回奥特玛那儿去，告诉他我失败了，求他原谅我的自不量力，原谅我竟以为自己帮得了他。我马上回去，把一切都告诉他。”渡鸦在头顶呱呱地叫，催她快走，可她听不懂它说的话。

===Original===
So again she began to trudge on, holding the snake in her hands and toiling over fields and moors in the way that she knew led to her own little village, though by now her feet were so swollen and her legs so stiff that she almost cried for pain.

===Chinese===
于是她又蹒跚上路，双手捧着蛇，跋涉过田野和荒原，沿着她认得的、通往自己小村的路走，虽然此时她的脚肿得厉害，腿也僵了，疼得她几乎要哭出来。

===Original===
Presently she came to the village where the cake-maker lived, and as she passed his shop, she saw that he stood at the door and nodded to her as he saw her coming.

===Chinese===
不久她来到做糕点的住的村子，经过那家铺子时，看见店主站在门口，望见她来便朝她点头。

===Original===
“Good day,” he cried; “you are the young wench I saw go past awhile back.”

===Chinese===
“日安，”他喊道，“你就是前些日子打这儿走过的那个小姑娘吧。”

===Original===
“Yes, I am going home now,” said Hulda.

===Chinese===
“是，我这就要回家了。”胡尔达说。

===Original===
“And have you found your fiddles and horns that turned to men and women?” he asked. “It was a fool’s errand, I fear, you were going on; and what have you got in your hand now?”

===Chinese===
“你找到你那些变成男人女人的提琴和号角了吗？”他问，“我看你那趟本是傻瓜差事。你这手里又拿的什么呀？”

===Original===
“It is a snake,” answered Hulda, “and﻿—”

===Chinese===
“是一条蛇，”胡尔达答道，“而且——”

===Original===
“A snake!” screamed the cake-maker. “Lord love the girl, is she mad to go wandering over the country carrying a snake with her? Why, it might kill you, wench! Drop it at once!”

===Chinese===
“一条蛇！”做糕点的尖叫起来。“老天爷疼疼这姑娘吧，她莫不是疯了，带着条蛇满乡乱走？嗐，它会咬死你的，丫头！快扔了它！”

===Original===
“No, indeed, I will not drop it,” said Hulda, “for it seems to me that it is the only thing which may do Othmar good, for at any rate it has killed the girl who stole his voice, and﻿—”

===Chinese===
“不，我不扔，”胡尔达说，“因为依我看，唯有这东西也许对奥特玛有好处，毕竟它咬死了偷他嗓音的那个姑娘，而且——”

===Original===
At this the man started and called out, “Good Lord, she is clean off her head. Stolen Othmar’s voice! What can the wench mean? Why, girl, that snake might bite you, and you would be dead at once. Why on earth should you carry it because it has killed a girl?”

===Chinese===
那人吓了一跳，叫道：“老天爷，她真是完全疯了。偷了奥特玛的嗓音！这丫头胡说什么呀？姑娘啊，那蛇会咬你，你立刻就没命。它咬死过一个姑娘，你就为这个带着它满地走？”

===Original===
“I am carrying it because the fool told me to do what the raven does,” answered Hulda, “and he has still a worm in his mouth. Look.”

===Chinese===
“我带着它，是因为傻瓜叫我渡鸦做什么就做什么，”胡尔达答道，“它嘴里到现在还衔着一条虫呢。你瞧。”

===Original===
At this the man burst out laughing. “Why, what has that to do with you?” he cried; “a raven will often carry a worm for a bit. Drop this snake at once, you silly lass, or, better still, hold it firm while I crush its head with my poker.” And he seized the poker to kill it with.

===Chinese===
那人听了放声大笑。“嗐，那跟你有什么相干？”他喊道，“乌鸦叼条虫，叺一会儿就吞了，常有的事。快把这蛇扔了，傻姑娘；要不，更好些，你攥紧了，我用拨火棍砸烂它的脑袋。”说着他抓起拨火棍要打死它。

===Original===
Then Hulda was frightened lest he might steal the snake from her, or kill it by force, and she ran on; but she ran in such haste that she stumbled against the baker who was just coming out from his shop with his basket of loaves on his arm.

===Chinese===
胡尔达生怕他抢走蛇、或者硬把它弄死，拔腿就跑；可她跑得慌，一头撞在面包师身上——他正挎着一篮面包从铺子里出来。

===Original===
“Can’t you look where you are going?” he cried in anger, as he picked up the bread which had rolled into the road; and then, seeing it was Hulda, he said:﻿—“Why, who are you running away from, my girl? Are you on the lookout for more fools that you can’t see when an honest man comes along? And whatever have you got there?”

===Chinese===
“你走路不长眼睛吗？”他气冲冲地喊，一面拾起滚到路上的面包；认出是胡尔达后，又说道：“嗐，我的姑娘，你这是从谁那儿逃啊？找傻瓜找上瘾了，老实人来了都看不见啦？你那手里拿的到底是什么？”

===Original===
“It is only a snake I have found,” said Hulda, when she had asked the man’s pardon, and she tried to hide the snake in her skirt, but the baker seized her arm and made her show it to him.

===Chinese===
“不过是我捡到的一条蛇。”胡尔达说，先向那人赔了不是。她想方把蛇藏进裙子，面包师却抓住她的胳膊，逼她拿出来给他看。

===Original===
“What on earth are you carrying a live snake with you for?” he asked. “Don’t you know they are venomous beasts, and the bite of one is certain death?” And, like the cake-maker, he tried to wrench the snake from her. At this Hulda was terribly frightened.

===Chinese===
“你好端端的带着条活蛇干什么？”他问，“你不知道它们是毒虫吗？被咬一口就准死无疑？”他像做糕点的一样，也想把蛇从她手里夺走。胡尔达吓坏了。

===Original===
“If they take the snake from me,” she thought, “then my last chance is gone,” and she tried to free herself from the baker, but he seized her by the skirt and held her fast, and shouted out to others to come and help him.

===Chinese===
“要是他们把蛇夺走，”她想，“我最后的一点指望也就没了。”她想从面包师手里挣脱，他却揪住她的裙子死死不放，还大声喊人来帮忙。

===Original===
“Help, help!” he cried. “Here is a poor mad girl, and she has got hold of a poisonous adder, and she will let it loose in the village and it will bite some of our children and kill them.” And when they heard his cry the villagers all came running out of their cottages.

===Chinese===
“来人哪，来人哪！”他喊道，“这儿有个可怜的疯姑娘，手里抓着条毒蛇，她会把它放出来在村里乱窜，咬死我们的孩子的。”听见他喊，村民们都从各自屋里跑了出来。

===Original===
“Let me go, let me go,” shrieked Hulda, “it will do no harm. I will hold it tight, and I would not lose it for the wide world.”

===Chinese===
“放我走，放我走，”胡尔达尖声喊道，“它不会害人的。我会攥得紧紧的，天底下什么我也舍不得换掉它。”

===Original===
“I tell you she is mad,” roared the baker, and the cake-maker came up and said the same thing. “She wandered by here some time back, and all she wanted to know was where she could find another as mad as herself, but she will have far to go before she meets one, I reckon. We must secure her and take the snake from her, but beware how you catch it, for fear it should bite.”

===Chinese===
“我告诉你们，她疯了。”面包师吼道；做糕点的也凑上来这么说。“她前些时候在这儿打听过，一心只想再找一个跟她一样疯的；我看她要找啊，得走远着呢。咱们得把她捆住，把蛇夺下来；不过抓的时候当心，别叫它咬着。”

===Original===
And the people all gathered round her and made a great hubbub, though they were afraid to touch the snake which Hulda still held firmly in her hand. And they made such a din that the old schoolmaster came out of the schoolhouse with his pupils after him.

===Chinese===
人们把她团团围住，闹成一片，却不敢碰那条蛇——胡尔达仍紧紧攥在手里。他们闹出那么大的动静，把老塾师也从学堂里引了出来，学生们跟在后面。

===Original===
The people told him there was a poor mad girl who had got a snake, and would not let them take it from her, and he remembered Hulda as the others had done, and shook his head and said sadly, “I fear it is too true. The poor child is really mad, but we cannot wrench the snake from her lest it may turn and bite us. But it is certain that it would not be safe to let her go; so, as the children are all going home now, let us lock her into the schoolhouse here, till we can get something to kill the creature with, and then when the doctor comes, he can see if the poor girl is very bad, and what had better be done with her.”

===Chinese===
人们告诉他，有个可怜的疯姑娘抓着一条蛇，不肯让他们夺走。他像旁人一样认出了胡尔达，摇摇头，难过地说：“只怕是真的。这可怜的孩子真是疯了。可咱们不能硬夺她手里的蛇，免得它回头咬咱们。不过放她走是万万不成的；眼下孩子们正要回家，就把她锁在这学堂里，等找着什么东西把这畜生打死再说。回头大夫来了，让他瞧瞧这可怜的姑娘病得重不重，该怎么发落她。”

===Original===
Hulda turned quite white with fear, and cried out that she was not mad, and that the snake should harm no one, but they would not heed her, and pushed her into the schoolhouse, and bolted the doors on her, and there Hulda sat on the floor and cried as if her heart would break.

===Chinese===
胡尔达吓得面无人色，尖声喊着她没有疯，说那蛇不会伤害任何人；可谁也不听她的，把她推进学堂，闩上了门。胡尔达坐在地上，哭得心都要碎了。

===Original===
“Alas!” cried she, “now all hope is gone, and Othmar will be dumb forever. For what good have I carried this snake with me all this way, if now it is to be taken from me and killed?” and her tears fell on the viper as she looked at it in her hand. It was very bright green and yellow, and it kept wrinkling and twisting its skin as she grasped it, and making a loud hissing noise. As her tears were still falling she heard a croak over her head, and saw the raven perched on a window above her, and again her hopes revived.

===Chinese===
“唉！”她哭道，“如今全完了，奥特玛要一辈子失声了。我把这条蛇带了这一路，若是如今要被人夺去打死，那我图的什么呢？”她望着手中的蛇，泪水滴落在那毒蛇身上。蛇是鲜艳的黄绿色，她攥着它，它一个劲儿地皱皮扭动，发出很响的嘶嘶声。她还在落泪，忽听头顶呱的一声，看见渡鸦栖在她上方的一扇窗上，她的希望又活了过来。

===Original===
“Maybe he has come to help me,” she thought, “for I should never have found the little dwarf if it had not been for the raven.” Then as she looked up at the raven sitting in the window, she saw that it was pecking at a piece of rope that hung through the window, and Hulda thought﻿—“Surely if I could climb up to the window, I could scramble through it, and climb down the rope on to the ground. Only if they were to see me, they would catch me again, so I must wait till nightfall when there is no one there.”

===Chinese===
“许是它来帮我的，”她想，“当初要不是这渡鸦，我根本找不到那小矮人。”她抬头望着窗上的渡鸦，看见它正啄着一根从窗口垂下来的绳子。胡尔达想：“要是能爬到那扇窗前，我就能翻出去，顺着绳子落到地上。只是叫他们看见了，会再抓住我的，所以得等到天黑、四下无人的时候。”

===Original===
So she sat down again and waited till the sun had set, and she trembled at every noise lest it might be someone coming to seek her, but they left her alone, and no one came. When it was quite dark, and all the village was quiet she went to the window, and tried to climb up to it, but she found that she could not manage to get up on to the windowsill while she held the snake in her hand. Then first she thought she would wind it around her waist, but she remembered how it had tightened around the singing girl and killed her, and for some time she could not think what to do with it. At last she twisted it into a knot, and placed it in her bosom, though she trembled lest it should bite her. And when she placed it in her bosom, she saw the curl of Othmar’s hair that lay there, and she took it and tied up the snake’s jaws with it so that it might not open its mouth. “For Othmar’s hair will not break or give way,” she said; “it is like his heart, it will be true and strong till the end.”

===Chinese===
于是她又坐下来，一直等到太阳落山。她一听见响动就发抖，生怕是来找她的人；可他们由她去了，谁也没来。天全黑了，村子静下来，她走到窗前，想爬上去，却发现手里攥着蛇，没法攀上窗台。她先想把蛇缠在腰上，可想起它怎样缠紧那唱歌的少女、把她勒死，好一阵子想不出怎么处置它。最后她把蛇打了个结，揣进怀里，虽然怕它咬，直打哆嗦。她把它放进怀里时，看见了放在那里的奥特玛那绺鬈发，便取出来捆住蛇的上下颚，叫它张不开嘴。“因为奥特玛的头发不会断、也不会松，”她说，“它就像他的心，到死都忠实、都坚定。”

===Original===
Then she climbed up on to the window-ledge, and scrambled through the window, and took the piece of rope and let herself down on to the ground outside. And when she lit upon the ground, she heard the raven croaking above her, and her heart leapt for joy, and she began to run as fast as she could to get away from the village lest they might catch her again.

===Chinese===
随后她攀上窗沿，翻出窗外，抓着那截绳子溜到外面的地上。她一落地，便听见渡鸦在头顶呱呱地叫，心里欢喜得直跳，拔腿飞跑，尽快离村子远远的，生怕再被他们抓住。

===Original===
When she came again into the open country, she looked for the raven, and saw that it was flying in front of her as before, towards the distant mountains where she knew lay her home. She toiled on, for many days, but by now the summer had nearly passed away, and when she got into the high mountain land, she found that the cold winter had given signs of coming, and the trees were beginning to be bare, and there was a light white frost on the ground. It was far into the night when she arrived in the village, and the villagers were all asleep and their cottages shut. Outside the cottage where Othmar lived grew a big old ivy tree, and on this the raven perched, and underneath it Hulda lay down to wait the dawn and Othmar’s waking. She lay quiet for a bit, but when she saw a faint glimmering of light where the sun was going to rise, she felt she could be still no longer, and she sprang up and called, “Othmar, come down, I am here,” for she dreaded having to tell him before the other villagers that she had failed.

===Chinese===
她又来到旷野上，寻那渡鸦，见它照旧在她前面飞，朝着远方的群山——她知道她的家就在那山里。她一路跋涉了许多天；此时夏天已快过尽，走进高山地带时，她发现寒冷的冬天已有来的迹象：树木开始光秃，地面上结了薄薄一层白霜。她回到村里时已是深夜，村民们都睡了，家家门户紧闭。奥特玛住的小屋外长着一棵高大的老常春藤树，渡鸦便落在那上面；胡尔达在树下躺下，等天亮，等奥特玛醒来。她静静躺了一会儿，可一见太阳将要升起的地方泛起微微一线光，她便再也躺不住了，一跃而起，喊道：“奥特玛，下来吧，我在这儿。”因为她怕当着全村人的面告诉他，自己失败了。

===Original===
In a few moments the door of the cottage opened and Othmar came out, and ran to greet her, but she kept afar.

===Chinese===
不一刻，小屋的门开了，奥特玛跑出来迎接她，她却远远站住。

===Original===
“Othmar,” she cried, “I have done you no good, save that I have punished the wicked girl who took your voice. This snake killed her, so she will never sing as you did again. See.” And she held out the snake to him; it was curled round and still tied up with his hair, and as the sun began to shine it glittered brightly.

===Chinese===
“奥特玛，”她喊道，“我没帮上你的忙，只除了一件事：我惩治了抢走你嗓音的狠毒姑娘。这条蛇把她咬死了，她再也不能像你那样唱了。瞧。”她把蛇递给他看；蛇蜷成一团，仍用他的头发捆着，太阳一开始照耀，它便亮亮地闪光。

===Original===
“But I have done you no good, indeed rather harm,” Hulda went on, “for I have made you hope where there was no hope, and you have waited and expected that I should bring you back what you had lost, and I have not done it, and now I shall never hear you say my name ‘Hulda’ again,” and she wept so bitterly that the tears fell from her face, and dropped upon the snake which still she grasped. Othmar held out his hand, and tried to take her hand that he might kiss it, and as he did so, he touched the snake’s long tail, and it began to writhe and twist, and glisten more and more as the sun shone on it. And as he raised her hand to his mouth, Othmar tried to say her name “Hulda” with his poor dumb lips that could make no sound, and he breathed it on the snake, and it seemed as if the snake vibrated with the name, and suddenly it swelled and swelled, and shone still more brightly, and its mouth grew wide and burst Othmar’s hair which had bound it, and widened out till it was not a snake any more, but a curled golden trumpet, curled up as the snake had been, and like that which had been changed into the singing girl who stole Othmar’s voice. “Take it, Othmar, and blow,” cried Hulda, and he put it to his lips and cried “Hulda!” and Hulda heard her name echoing back in a burst of music from all around. At its sound the birds awoke in all the trees, and began their morning chorus, and the village folk ran to their windows to see what the trumpet’s peal had been, and saw Othmar standing with Hulda in his arms, and at their feet the bright trumpet which he had dropped. It lay on the ground, but as Othmar began to speak and to say, “Hulda, Hulda, you have brought it back, you have given me my voice again,” the trumpet broke into many pieces, and with every word crumbled, till there was nothing left but a little heap of shining golden sand, and from under it there glided out a dark green snake with yellow markings, and it slid away into the bushes and disappeared.

===Chinese===
“可我到底没帮上你的忙，说起来倒像害了你，”胡尔达接着说，“因为我叫你在没有希望的地方生了希望，你一直盼着、等着，等我把你失去的带回来，我却没有做到；如今我再也不会听见你说我的名字‘胡尔达’了。”她哭得那么伤心，泪水从脸上滚落，滴在她仍攥着的那条蛇身上。奥特玛伸出手，想拉过她的手来吻一吻；他这样做时，碰到了蛇的长尾巴，蛇便扭动、绞转起来，阳光照在上面，越闪越亮。他把她的手举到唇边，奥特玛用他那发不出声音的可怜的哑唇，竭力想说她的名字“胡尔达”，他把这名字呵在蛇身上；那蛇仿佛随着这名字颤动起来，忽然膨胀、膨胀，闪出更亮的光，嘴越张越大，绷断了捆着它的奥特玛的头发，越张越宽，直到不再是一条蛇，而是一支蜷曲的金号角，蜷成蛇原先盘着的模样，正像先前变成偷嗓少女的那一支。“拿起来，奥特玛，吹吧！”胡尔达喊道。他把它举到唇上，喊出一声“胡尔达！”胡尔达听见自己的名字化作一片乐音，从四面八方回响而来。这声音一响，四下树里的鸟儿都醒了，开始它们的晨曲；村民们奔到窗前，看那号角声是打哪里来的，只见奥特玛把胡尔达搂在怀里，脚边躺着他掉落的亮闪闪的号角。号角躺在地上；当奥特玛开口说起话来——“胡尔达，胡尔达，你把它带回来了，你把我的嗓音还给我了”——号角便碎成许多片，每说一个字便碎一层，直到什么都不剩，只留下一小堆闪亮的金沙；金沙底下滑出一条带黄斑的深绿蛇，钻进灌木丛，不见了。

===Original===
Then all the villagers rejoiced, and Hulda wept with happiness. And Othmar married Hulda, and his voice never left him again; but when long years after folk would tell him his voice was sweet and far more beautiful than the birds, he would say, “But it is not really my voice, it is my wife’s, Hulda’s, for I should have been dumb forever if she had not sought it and brought it back to me.”

===Chinese===
于是全村人都欢喜起来，胡尔达喜极而泣。奥特玛娶了胡尔达，他的嗓音再没有离开过他；可许多年后，每当有人夸他嗓音甜美、远胜过鸟儿，他总说：“这其实不是我的嗓音，是我妻子的，是胡尔达的；若不是她去寻了来、带了回来，我本要一辈子失声的。”

## Nanina’s Sheep / 纳尼娜的羊

===Original===
Once there lived a young girl called Nanina, who kept sheep for an old farmer. One day he said to her, “Nanina, I’m going away to buy pigs at a market far off, and I shall be away one whole month, so be sure and take good care of the flock, and remember, there are six sheep and eight lambs, and I must find them safe when I return. And mind, Nanina, that whatever you do, you don’t go near the old palace on the other side of the hill, for it is filled with wicked fairies who might do you an ill turn.” Nanina promised, and her master started.

===Chinese===
从前有个少女名叫纳尼娜（Nanina），替一位老农夫放羊。一天，老农夫对她说：“纳尼娜，我要到很远的市场去买猪，得整整离开一个月，你千万要好生照看羊群。记住，一共是六只羊、八只羊羔，我回来时必须看到它们安然无恙。还有，纳尼娜，切记，无论你做什么，都不要靠近山那边的旧宫殿（old palace），那里面住满了邪恶的仙子，说不定会害你遭殃。”纳尼娜答应了，主人便动身走了。

===Original===
The first day all went well, and she drove the flock in safely at night; but the next day she found it dull sitting on the hillside watching the lambs at play, and wondered why her master had told her always to keep on that side, and away from the old palace on the other.

===Chinese===
第一天一切顺利，夜里她把羊群安然赶回圈中；可是第二天，她坐在山坡上看羊羔嬉戏，只觉得无聊起来，心里纳闷：主人为什么叮嘱她总待在这一边，离山那边的旧宫殿远远的。

===Original===
“If it is filled with fairies,” quoth she, “it won’t hurt me just to look at it; I should like to see a fairy.” So she drove her flock to the other side of the hill, and sat looking at the old palace that was half in ruins, but was said to be lit up quite brightly every night after it was dark.

===Chinese===
“就算宫里住满了仙子，”她自语道，“只看上一眼也碍不着什么；我倒真想见见仙子呢。”于是她把羊群赶到山的另一边，坐下来望着那座旧宫殿。宫殿已半是断壁残垣，可据说每天夜幕降临之后，里面都会亮起一片通明的灯火。

===Original===
“I wonder if it really is lit up,” said Nanina, “I should like to see.” So she waited on that side of the hill till the sun went down, and then she saw a bright light appearing in one of the palace windows. As she stood and watched, the front door opened, and out there came a shepherd boy followed by a flock of black goats. Nanina stared at him, for she had never seen anyone so beautiful before. He was dressed in glittering green, and wore a soft brown hat trimmed with leaves under which his curls hung down. In one hand he held a crook and in the other a pipe, and as he drew near, he began to play the pipe and dance merrily, while the goats behind him skipped and danced too. Nanina had never seen such goats; they were jet black, with locks curling and thick and soft as silk. As she listened open-mouthed to the music of the pipe, she heard it speak words in its playing:—

===Chinese===
“不知道是不是真的会亮，”纳尼娜说，“我真想瞧一瞧。”于是她在山的这一边一直等到太阳落山，果然看见宫殿的一扇窗里透出明亮的光来。她正站着观望，宫殿的正门开了，走出来一个牧羊少年，身后跟着一群黑山羊。纳尼娜直勾勾地望着他，因为她从没见过这么俊美的人。他穿着闪闪发光的绿衣裳，戴一顶缀着叶子的棕色软帽，帽沿下垂着一绺绺鬈发。他一只手握着牧杖，另一只手拿着牧笛，走近时便吹起笛子，欢快地跳起舞来，身后的山羊也跟着蹦跳起舞。这样的山羊纳尼娜从未见过：浑身墨黑，卷毛又浓又厚，柔软如丝。她张着嘴听那笛声，听着听着，竟听见笛音里吐出话来：——

===Original===
“When the young birds sing,

And the young plants spring,

Then dance we so merrily together, oh.”

===Chinese===
“当雏鸟啼唱，

当新苗抽芽，

我们便这样欢欢喜喜共舞一场，噢。”

===Original===
The shepherd boy danced lightly to where she stood, and louder and louder sounded the pipe, and still it said—

===Chinese===
牧羊少年轻盈地舞到她跟前，笛声越奏越响，那笛子还在唱——

===Original===
“When the young birds sing,

And the young plants spring,

Then dance we so merrily together, oh.”

===Chinese===
“当雏鸟啼唱，

当新苗抽芽，

我们便这样欢欢喜喜共舞一场，噢。”

===Original===
Nanina gaped to see the goats dance and spring in time to the music, and so cheering it was, that she felt her own feet beginning to move with it. The shepherd made her a low bow and offered her his hand, and she placed hers in it, and off they started together. Nanina’s feet felt as light as if they had been made of cork, and she laughed with glee as she bounded on; and as she danced with the shepherd, so her flock began to move too, and thus they went, followed by the black goats and sheep all skipping merrily. “If my flock follow me there can be no harm,” thought Nanina, and on they kept in time to the wonderful tune—

===Chinese===
纳尼娜看得目瞪口呆，只见山羊们随着乐声又蹦又跳，那景象如此欢快，连她自己的一双脚也开始不由自主地动了起来。牧羊少年向她深深一躬，伸出手来，她便把手放进他的手心，两人一同舞了出去。纳尼娜只觉得双脚轻飘飘的，仿佛是软木做的，她一路雀跃，欢喜得直笑；她随着少年起舞，她的羊群也跟着动了起来，就这样一路走去，黑山羊在前，羊群在后，全都欢蹦乱跳。“我的羊群既然跟着我，就不会有什么祸事。”纳尼娜想道。于是他们伴着那支奇妙的曲子继续前行——

===Original===
“When the young birds sing,

And the young plants spring,

Then dance we so merrily together, oh.”

===Chinese===
“当雏鸟啼唱，

当新苗抽芽，

我们便这样欢欢喜喜共舞一场，噢。”

===Original===
Whither they went she knew not, she thought of nothing but the joy of dancing to the wonderful music; but suddenly, just ere sunrise, the shepherd stopped, and dropped her hand and gave one long slow note on the pipe, at which the goats gathered round him, and before she knew where they were going, they had disappeared into the palace. Then she was in a terrible fright, for she saw the sun beginning to rise, and found the whole night had passed, when she thought she had only been ten minutes. She counted her sheep, and, alas! there was one lamb missing.

===Chinese===
他们到了哪里，她自己也不知道，满心只想着随那奇妙音乐起舞的欢乐。可是突然，就在日出之前，牧羊少年停了下来，放开她的手，用笛子吹出一个悠长缓慢的音。山羊们闻声聚拢到他身边，她还没弄清他们要往哪里去，他们便统统隐入宫殿里去了。这下她吓坏了：她看见太阳就要升起，才发现整整一夜已经过去，而她只当过了十分钟。她数了数羊，唉！少了一只羊羔。

===Original===
She sought everywhere for it, but no trace of it was to be seen. Then she drove all the others back to the farm and watched them, falling half asleep, for she was weary with the dancing. But when evening came, and she had slept some time, she said to herself, “Surely the best plan would be to go back to the old palace, and see if I can see the shepherd and the black goats again.” So just about sunset she returned to the palace, and again the door opened, and the beautiful shepherd boy came out with the black goats following. But when he began to play on his pipe, and the goats to dance, Nanina forgot all about the lost lamb and danced with him as before. Again they danced till morning, and then he left her suddenly, and she found that another lamb had disappeared. Then she wept and lamented, and declared that the next night she would only watch the shepherd and nothing would make her dance; and again the next night the same thing happened; when once she heard the pipe, Nanina could not keep still, and another lamb was lost. This went on to the end of a fortnight, when there was only one of the flock left. Then she was terribly frightened, for her master would soon return, and she did not know what she should say to him. But still she went back and sat by the old palace, and when the shepherd came out, and she heard the music, she could not refrain from dancing, and in the morning the last lamb had gone!

===Chinese===
她四处寻找，却不见一点踪影。她只好把其余的羊赶回农庄守着，跳了一夜舞，实在困乏，守着守着便打起盹来。可到了傍晚，睡了一阵之后，她自言自语道：“最好的法子，一定是再回旧宫殿去，看看能不能再见着那牧羊少年和黑山羊。”于是日落时分，她又回到宫殿。门又开了，俊美的牧羊少年又带着黑山羊走了出来。可他一开始吹笛、山羊一开始跳舞，纳尼娜便把丢羊羔的事忘得一干二净，又像先前那样同他跳起舞来。他们又一直跳到天亮，他倏地丢下她走了，她这才发现又少了一只羊羔。她哭了，哀哀悲叹，赌咒说下一夜只看着牧羊少年，无论如何也不再跳舞；可第二天夜里还是老样子——笛声一起，纳尼娜就再也站不住，又丢了一只羊羔。这样一连过了半个月，羊群里只剩下最后一只了。她怕得要命，因为主人很快就要回来了，她不知道该对主人说什么好。可她还是回到旧宫殿旁坐着，牧羊少年一出来，她一听见那音乐，便又忍不住跳起舞来；到了早晨，最后一只羊羔也不见了！

===Original===
All the day Nanina wandered about and cried, but no sheep were to be found. At last, when she was quite weary, she sat down beneath a beech tree near the palace, and leaned her head against its trunk sobbing. Then she saw that someone had torn down the lowest branches of the tree and they were hanging down broken. She raised them and tied them up, so that they would grow together, and as she did so she heard a shadowy voice whisper, “Thank you, Nanina; Nanina, don’t dance.” She looked about but there was nobody there, and again she heard a whisper, “Nanina, don’t dance.” The voice came from the beech tree, and among the leaves she saw a small twisted face looking at her. “Thank you, Nanina, for saving my bough,” said the tree, “and if you mind me, you shall get all your sheep back again.”

===Chinese===
这一整天，纳尼娜到处走，到处哭，却一只羊也找不到。最后，她走得精疲力竭，在宫殿附近一棵山毛榉树下坐了下来，靠着树干呜呜地哭。忽然她看见有人把树最低处的枝条折断了，枝条断垂下来。她把它们扶起来捆扎好，让它们能够重新长合。正当她这样做时，她听见一个幽幽的声音低语：“谢谢你，纳尼娜；纳尼娜，别跳舞。”她四下张望，却不见一个人影，接着又听见那低语：“纳尼娜，别跳舞。”声音是从山毛榉树里传出来的，她在枝叶间看见一张小小的、扭曲的脸正望着她。“谢谢你救了我的枝条，纳尼娜，”树说，“只要你听我的话，你的羊就会全都回到你身边。”

===Original===
“My sheep,” cried Nanina. “Only tell me, and I will do anything.”

===Chinese===
“我的羊！”纳尼娜喊道，“只要你告诉我，叫我做什么都行。”

===Original===
“Then you must not dance. Every time you refuse to dance with the fairy, one of your flock will be returned.”

===Chinese===
“那你千万别跳舞。每回你拒绝同那仙子跳舞，你的羊群就会有一只回到你身边。”

===Original===
“But how can I refuse to dance?” cried Nanina, “for as I hear the pipe beginning, my feet begin to move of themselves, it is no use my trying,” and she cried aloud.

===Chinese===
“可我怎么拒绝得了呢？”纳尼娜喊道，“笛声一起，我的脚就自己动起来，我怎么忍也忍不住啊。”说着放声大哭。

===Original===
“Bury your feet in the earth like my roots,” whispered back the voice. “Dig a hole deep down, and I will hold your feet so that you shall not move them, only you must bear the pain, and not mind if you walk lame afterwards, for I shall hold them very tight, and it will hurt you.”

===Chinese===
“把你的脚像我的根一样埋进土里，”那声音低低答道，“挖一个深深的坑，我会抱住你的脚，叫你动弹不得；只是你必得忍住疼痛，日后走路瘸了也别放在心上，因为我会把它们攥得很紧很紧，那会很疼的。”

===Original===
“Hurt me as you please,” cried Nanina, “and I shan’t mind. If only I can get back my sheep I will bear any pain.” So she knelt beneath the tree, and dug a deep hole in the ground among its roots, and then she placed her feet among the loose earth, and she felt something moving near them which tightened around and drew them far down into the ground, and held them as if they were bound with cords. She saw the lights in the windows of the palace, and the door opened. “Hold me, hold me fast,” she cried, “for when I hear the music I shall begin to dance.” The tree said nothing, but she felt its roots tightening so that she could not move. The door of the palace opened as before, and the beautiful shepherd, followed by his goats and her sheep, came out, and she heard once more the sound of the wonderful pipe, and he danced straight up to the tree beneath which she stood, and held out his hand to her. Nanina felt as if her feet were beginning to move under the earth, but the roots of the tree held them so firmly that she could not stir one inch. Still the shepherd danced before her, and as she saw him springing in front, with the flocks behind him following him, she grew quite wild to dance, and tried her hardest to break her feet free from the roots which held them, but in vain, though she almost screamed with the pain they cost her. For hours the shepherd danced in front of her, till, as before, the pipe sounded forth one long note, and he disappeared, but this time not all the flock went with him, for beside her was left one of her own little lambs, and when she saw it she cried for joy. She felt the roots releasing their hold of her feet, and she drew them out of the earth, and they were all blue and bruised where they had been held. She drove home the lamb and fastened it into the sheep-pen, but her feet were so stiff and swelled that she limped as she walked. Next night she went back to the beech tree, and again slipped her feet into its roots, and felt them twist around them; but this time the poor feet were so sore that she cried when they touched them. Again the fairy appeared, and again she heard the pipe, and her longing to dance was worse than ever, but the roots clutched her and would not let her stir. When the pipe ceased and the fairy disappeared, another of her lambs was left with her, and she drove it home as she had done the first, but she had to go very slowly on account of her crushed feet.

===Chinese===
“你要怎么疼我都可以，”纳尼娜喊道，“我不怕。只要能找回我的羊，多疼我都受得住。”于是她跪在树下，在树根间挖了一个深坑，把双脚埋进松土里。她觉得脚边有什么东西在动，随即缠了上来，把她的脚紧紧箍住，直往地底下拽，就像被绳索捆住一般。她望见宫殿的窗口亮起了灯，大门开了。“抱住我，紧紧抱住我，”她喊道，“我一听见那音乐就要跳舞了。”树一言不发，只觉得树根越收越紧，叫她动弹不得。宫殿的门像先前一样开了，俊美的牧羊少年领着他的山羊和她的羊群走了出来，她又听见了那支奇妙的笛声。少年径直舞到她所站的树下，向她伸出手来。纳尼娜觉得双脚仿佛要在土里动起来，可树根把它们攥得那么紧，她连一寸也挪不动。少年还在她面前跳着，她望着他在前头腾跃，羊群在后面紧紧相随，心里的舞兴越发狂热，拼尽全力想把脚从树根里挣脱出来，却是徒然，疼得她几乎叫出声来。少年在她面前一连跳了好几个钟头，直到笛子像先前那样吹出一个悠长的音，他便隐去了。可这一次，羊群并没有全跟着他走——她身边留下了一只她自己的小羊羔，她一见便喜极而泣。她觉得树根渐渐松开了她的脚，她把脚从土里拔出来，被攥过的地方又青又紫，满是淤伤。她把羊羔赶回家关进羊圈，可她的脚又僵又肿，走起路来一瘸一拐。第二天夜里，她又回到山毛榉树下，把脚重新伸进树根间，觉出树根缠绕上来；可这一回，那双可怜的脚已经疼得一碰便疼得她直哭。仙子又出现了，她又听见了笛声，想跳舞的念头比以往任何时候都强烈，可树根死死攥住她，不许她动弹半分。笛声一停，仙子隐去，又一只羊羔留在了她身边。她像头一回那样把它赶回家去，只是双脚被夹得厉害，只能走得极慢极慢。

===Original===
The same thing happened the next night and the next, till all the flock had returned save one, and Nanina’s feet were so bad that she could scarcely hobble, for they were crushed and bleeding, and she wondered whether she would walk lame all the days of her life.

===Chinese===
第二天夜里、第三天夜里都是如此，直到整群羊只剩下最后一只没有回来。纳尼娜的脚已经伤得几乎挪不动步，又破又肿，血迹斑斑，她不知道自己是不是这辈子都要瘸着腿走路了。

===Original===
On the last evening she limped down to the tree almost crying with pain. When she sat down by its trunk she heard the soft sighing voice saying, “Never mind, Nanina; tonight is the last, and though it will hurt you the most, it will soon be past.” So she slipped her feet into the earth once more, though she shrank as they touched it, and directly the sun had set, the lights appeared in the palace windows, and out came the shepherd with all his black goats and her one white sheep following him. He looked more beautiful than ever, for he had a crown set with jewels, and was dressed in scarlet and gold, but when the pipe began to play it was not merry dance-music it made, but long sad notes, like a funeral march; yet Nanina’s feet would have moved in spite of herself, and she would have marched in time to them, had not the roots tightened like cords and held her down. Tears of pain ran down her cheeks, and she sobbed, and instead of the joyous words what the music said was—

===Chinese===
最后一天傍晚，她疼得几乎要哭，一步一瘸地挪到树下。她在树干旁坐下，听见那轻柔的、叹息般的声音说道：“别难过，纳尼娜；今夜是最后一夜了，虽然这一回最疼，可很快就过去了。”于是她又把双脚埋进土里，尽管脚一碰到土她便缩了一下。太阳刚一落山，宫殿窗口便亮起灯来，牧羊少年走了出来，领着他所有的黑山羊，还有她仅剩的一只白羊。他比以往任何时候都俊美，头戴宝石冠冕，一身的猩红与金黄。可这一回笛子吹响的，不再是欢快的舞曲，而是悠长哀伤的调子，像一支送葬进行曲；尽管如此，纳尼娜的脚仍要不由自主地动起来，要跟着那节拍踏步，幸亏树根像绳索一样收紧，把她死死按住。疼痛的泪水顺着脸颊淌下来，她抽抽噎噎地哭着，那音乐里唱的不再是欢快的言语，而是——

===Original===
“Join us, Nanina, dance again,

One last dance will ease your pain.”

===Chinese===
“来吧纳尼娜，与我们共舞，再舞一场，

最后一舞，便能消你伤痛。”

===Original===
Presently the music grew quicker, and her longing to move with it grew stronger. She swayed herself about, and cried and screamed as the fairy and flock danced, now solemnly and slowly, now joyously and wildly. Just when she felt that she could bear it no longer there came one long low note on the pipe, and with a mighty crash like thunder the shepherd and the goats disappeared, and not only had they gone, but the walls of the old palace had fallen, and nothing was left of it but a heap of stones. Beside her on the grass was the last of her lost sheep. “Goodbye, Nanina,” said the voice from the beech tree; “now you have all your flock again,” and she felt the roots loosen round her feet, but when she looked at them she found that her legs were wounded and bleeding, where she had dashed them about in trying to dance. She knelt down and smoothed over the earth where it was torn up among the trees, and she put her arms round the trunk and kissed and thanked it for having helped her, but the voice did not speak again. Then she drove home the last sheep, but she had to go on her hands and knees, for her feet were too bad to walk.

===Chinese===
不久，乐声渐渐急促起来，她想随之起舞的渴望也越发强烈。她左右摇晃，哭喊尖叫，看那仙子领着羊群舞蹈，一时缓慢庄严，一时欢快狂野。就在她觉得再也忍受不住的那一刻，笛子里传出一个低沉悠长的音，随着雷鸣般的一声巨响，牧羊少年和山羊们倏然不见了；不但他们不见了，连旧宫殿的墙也塌了，只剩下一堆乱石。她身旁的草地上，躺着最后一只丢失的羊。“再见了，纳尼娜，”山毛榉树里的声音说，“你的羊群又齐全啦。”她觉得树根在脚边松开了。可她低头一看，才发觉两条腿又破又肿，血迹斑斑——那是她挣扎着想跳舞时撞出来的。她跪下来，把树间翻起的泥土抚平，又伸出双臂抱住树干，亲吻它，感谢它的帮助，可那声音再没有响起。然后她把最后一只羊赶回家去，只能手脚并用地爬着走，因为她的脚已经疼得无法行走。

===Original===
Next day when the farmer came home, he was well pleased that she had kept his flock safe, but he would fain know how she had got such sore feet that for long she must walk lame. “Of a truth, master,” she quoth, “it was in saving the lambs when they got into dangerous places.”

===Chinese===
第二天，老农夫回到家，见羊群一只不少、平安无事，十分欢喜，却很想知道她的脚怎么伤成这样，害她得瘸上好一阵子。“不瞒您说，主人，”她答道，“是羊羔们闯到险处，我去救它们时伤的。”

===Original===
Underneath the beech tree, where Nanina’s feet had bled among the earth, there sprang up pretty little scarlet flowers, and whenever she passed and saw them she remembered how she had been punished for disobeying her master, and made up her mind never to do so again.

===Chinese===
在山毛榉树下，纳尼娜的双脚曾经滴血入土的地方，开出了一朵朵娇艳的猩红小花。每当她路过看见这些花，便想起自己是怎样因为不听主人的话而受罚，从此下定决心再也不违逆他了。

## The Gipsy’s Cup / 吉普赛人的杯子

===Original===
In a little village there lived a young potter, who made his living by making all sorts of earthenware. He took the clay, and made it into shapes on the wheel, and then baked his cups and jars in a kiln. He made big jugs and little jugs, and basins and cups and saucers, and indeed every sort of pot or jar that could be wanted. He was very fond of his work, and was always thinking of how to make new shapes, or colour his jars with pretty colours. It was a very tiny village he lived in, and he worked at throwing his pots on his wheel by the roadside, but people came from many other villages and towns to buy his ware. Once a year there was a big fair, held in the town near, and just before it, the potter was always very busy making new pots and jugs to sell there. A few nights before the fair was to be held, he was hard at work, trying to finish a number of little bowls, so he sat at his wheel late in the evening after the sun was set. All day long the road had been gay with folk coming to the fair, some were in carts, and some were on foot, and there were a number of gipsies in caravans, bringing all sorts of goods to sell. Most of them went through the village and on to a big common a little further on, where they got out of their carts and put up tents, to sleep in while the fair went on.
