The potter was so busy with his little basins on his turning wheel that he did not hear the sound of footsteps, and when he looked up, he was surprised to see a young gipsy girl standing near, watching him. She was quite young, and had big black eyes, and rosy round cheeks, and her black hair was twisted up in little red beads and chains. She was dressed in some very gay stuff, and round her neck was a gold necklace, and on her fingers and arms were rings and bracelets.

===Chinese===
从前，在一座小小的村庄里住着一个年轻的陶工（potter），他以制作各式陶器为生。他取来黏土，在陶轮（wheel）上把它们塑成各种形状，再把杯子和罐子放进窑（kiln）里烧制。他做大水罐，也做小水罐，还做盆、杯子和碟子，凡是用得着的坛坛罐罐，他样样都做。他非常热爱自己的手艺，总在琢磨怎样塑出新的样式，或是给罐子染上好看的颜色。他住的村子极小，他就在路边支起陶轮拉坯，可是附近许多村镇的人都来找他买陶器。每年一次，邻近的镇上要办一场大集市，集市开张之前，陶工总是忙着赶制新盆新罐，好拿去卖。集市开张前几天的一个夜里，他正埋头赶工，想做完一批小碗，因此太阳落山之后，他还坐在陶轮前干到很晚。一整天里，路上都是赶集的人，热闹非凡：有的坐马车，有的步行，还有不少吉普赛人驾着大篷车，载着各式各样要卖的货物。他们大多穿过村子，到前面不远处一大片公共草地去，在那里下了车、搭起帐篷，集市期间就睡在里头。陶工只顾埋头在转动的陶轮上做他的小盆，竟没听见脚步声；待他抬起头来，吃了一惊，只见一个吉普赛女郎（gipsy）站在近旁，正望着他。她很年轻，长着一双黑色的大眼睛，圆圆的脸颊红扑扑的，黑头发里编着红色的小珠子和链子。她穿着一身十分鲜亮的衣裳，脖子上挂着一串金项链，手指上、手臂上都戴着戒指和镯子。

===Original===
“That should be a fine cup,” said the girl, “since you keep your eyes on it and can look at nothing else.”

===Chinese===
“那只杯子准是只顶好的杯子，”少女说，“不然你怎么眼睛只盯着它，旁的什么都顾不上看呢。”

===Original===
“I keep my eyes for my work, that I may do it well,” said the potter, “for I live by my work, and neither by stealing nor begging.”

===Chinese===
“我的眼睛是留给活计的，这样才能做得好，”陶工说，“我是靠手艺吃饭的，可不靠偷，也不靠讨。”

===Original===
“But I fancy many others can do your work as well, or better than you,” answered the gipsy. “What can your cups do when they are finished? I don’t hear you say anything to them, so I should think they would be stupid cups—only fit to drink out of.”

===Chinese===
“可我瞧许多人都能把你这活计做得一样好，兴许比你还强呢，”吉普赛女郎答道，“你这些杯子做好了又能干什么？我没听见你对它们说过一句活，看来准是些呆杯子——只配拿来喝水。”

===Original===
“And what else should they be for?” asked the potter angrily. “What do you mean by saying that you don’t hear me saying anything to my cups? I don’t think you know what you are talking about. It is nonsense, and you are talking nonsense.”

===Chinese===
“不然还要它们干什么？”陶工气冲冲地问，“你说没听见我对杯子说话，这话是什么意思？我看你自己都不知道自己在讲什么。简直是胡说八道，你就是在胡说八道。”

===Original===
“My grandfather used to make pots on a wheel,” said the gipsy, and she laughed low, and showed her white teeth in the moonlight; “ah! but he knew how to do them, and he had charms to say to them when he threw them. And one of his cups would make you wise if you drank out of it, and another would give you your true love’s heart if she drank from it, and another would make you forget everything—yes, even your true love, and all your mirth and all your sorrow, and I think that was the best cup of all;” and again the gipsy laughed in the moonlight, and sang a little song to herself as she sat herself down before the potter.

===Chinese===
“我祖父从前也在陶轮上做罐子，”吉普赛女郎说着低低一笑，月光下露出雪白的牙齿，“啊！可他懂得怎么做，他拉坯的时候还对它们念咒。他做的杯子，有一只，你喝了里头的东西就会变得聪明；有一只，你的真心爱人喝了，就会把整颗心给你；还有一只，能叫你忘掉一切——是啊，连你的真心爱人都忘掉，忘掉你所有的欢喜和所有的悲伤。我看哪，那才是顶好的一只杯子。”吉普赛女郎又对着月光笑了，在陶工面前坐下来，自顾自地哼起一支小歌。

===Original===
“Now this is real child’s talk,” said the potter very impatiently. “ ’Tis easy to say your grandfather knew how to do all this, but why should I believe you? and because your grandfather may have been able to throw a bowl upon the wheel, that doesn’t make you know anything about the craft, or how it is done.”

===Chinese===
“这可真是小孩子话了，”陶工很不耐烦地说，“你说你祖父会这一切，嘴上说说倒容易；可我凭什么信你？就算你祖父会在陶轮上拉一只碗，也不等于你懂这门手艺，懂得它是怎么做出来的。”

===Original===
“Nay, but he taught me too,” said the gipsy. “Give me a piece of your clay, and let me come to your wheel and you shall see.”

===Chinese===
“才不呢，他也教过我，”吉普赛女郎说，“给我一块黏土，让我到你的陶轮前来，你瞧着就是。”

===Original===
At first the potter thought she was talking nonsense, but to his great surprise she took hold of the clay in her little brown hands, and moulded and modelled it with the greatest skill. Then she placed it on the wheel and threw a little jug, and he wondered to see how deft she was.

===Chinese===
起初陶工只当她在胡说，可叫他大为吃惊的是，她竟用那双棕色的小手抓起黏土，以极为精巧的手法揉捏塑形起来。随后她把它放到陶轮上，拉出一只小水罐，他看着她那样灵巧，不由得暗暗称奇。

===Original===
“Now I will make you a little bowl,” she said, and then she made jugs and pots and jars, far more quickly and skilfully than the potter could have done. “And now I will colour them too,” she cried. “See, I shall catch the colour from the moon, and tomorrow you can put them into your kiln and bake them, and you may be sure that you have never had such pots there before.” Then she put her little brown hands out into the moonlight, and they were covered with rings which glittered and shone, but as she held up her palms to the moon’s rays, it seemed to the potter as if they too were full of some strange glittering liquid. “And now,” she said, “see, I will put it on to your pots, and I should think I had taught you that I know more about your trade than you do yourself.” And she took the pots in her hands and rubbed her palms over them, and she traced patterns on them with her fingers.

===Chinese===
“这回我给你做只小碗，”她说。接着她做出罐子、盆和坛子，比陶工自己做得又快又好得多。“现在我还要给它们上色，”她喊道，“瞧着，我要从月亮那儿把颜色接来；明天你把它们放进窑里烧，保管你从前从没在窑里烧出过这样的陶器。”于是她把那双棕色的小手伸进月光里，手上戴满戒指，闪闪发亮；当她把手掌举向月光时，陶工仿佛觉得那两只掌心里也盛满了某种奇异的、亮晶晶的液体。“现在，”她说，“瞧着，我要把它涂到你的陶器上。这下总该叫你明白：论你这一行，我懂得比你自己还多。”她把陶器拿在手里，用手掌在器身上摩挲，又用手指在上面描出花纹。

===Original===
The potter looked at her and felt almost angry, but she only laughed in his face.

===Chinese===
陶工望着她，几乎要动气了，她却只冲着他的脸笑。

===Original===
“And now one last thing,” she cried, “and that is, that I will make you a cup that has a spell in it, and it shall be a present for you to remember me by. It will be very plain, and there will be no gay colours in it, but when you give it to your true love to drink from, if once you have drunk from it yourself, you will have all her heart, but beware that she doesn’t take a second draught. For though the first draught that she drinks will be drunk to love, the second draught will be drunk to hate, and though she have loved you more than all else on earth, all her love will turn to hate when she drinks again. And as you are so ignorant how to make bowls and cups, you will not know how to fashion one so as to win back her love again.”

===Chinese===
“还有最后一件事，”她喊道，“我要给你做一只带咒的杯子，就当是件礼物，好叫你记着我。它会做得非常朴素，上头没有一点鲜亮的颜色；可当你把它递给你的真心爱人喝时——只要你先喝过——她就会把整颗心都给你。只是千万当心，别叫她喝第二口。因为她喝的头一口是为爱而饮，第二口却要为恨而饮；哪怕她先前爱你胜过世上一切，再喝一口时，她全部的爱都会化成恨。而你既然这样不会做碗做杯，也就不会知道该怎样再造出一只来，把她的爱重新赢回来。”

===Original===
The potter stared in silence, while the gipsy took another bit of clay and placed it upon the wheel, and then she bent her head, which glittered with beads and coins, low over it, and placing her rosy lips close to the mouth of the cup, sang some words into it, while she moulded it with her hands, and turned the wheel with her foot. It was in some strange language that the potter had never heard before.

===Chinese===
陶工默默瞪着眼，只见吉普赛女郎又拿起一块黏土放到陶轮上，然后低下她那缀满珠子钱币、亮闪闪的头，把玫瑰色的嘴唇凑近杯口，一边用手捏塑、用脚转动陶轮，一边对着杯子唱起一些词句来。那是一种陶工从未听过的奇异语言。

===Original===
“Goodbye,” she said presently. “Now, there, that is for you, and be sure you do not sell the little brown cup, but keep it and give it to your true love to drink out of; but only one draught, for if there are two maybe you will need the gipsy’s help again.” Then she laughed, and nodding her head over her shoulder, tripped lightly away in the moonlight while the potter stared after her.

===Chinese===
“再会了，”过了一会儿她说，“喏，这个给你。记住，千万别把这只小棕杯（the little brown cup）卖了，要留着它，给你的真心爱人喝；可只能喝一口，因为若喝了第二口，说不定你还得再求吉普赛人帮忙呢。”说罢她一笑，回头点了点头，踏着月光轻盈地去了，陶工只管怔怔望着她的背影。

===Original===
At first he thought he had been asleep, but there around him stood the little rows of jugs and pots which the gipsy had made, and truly they were beautifully done. He took them up, and turned them over in his hand, and wondered at their shape and workmanship.

===Chinese===
起初他以为自己睡着了，可是他四周摆着一排排吉普赛女郎做的小罐小盆，的确做得漂亮极了。他把它们拿起来，在手里翻来覆去地看，惊叹它们的造型和手工。

===Original===
“Tomorrow,” he said, “I will put them into the kiln, and see how they come out. She certainly was a clever wench, and knew her work; but as for her talk about having coloured them, that was all nonsense, and as for breathing spells and charms into the cups, why it is like baby’s talk.”

===Chinese===
“明天，”他说，“我把它们放进窑里，看看烧出来是什么样。她真是个伶俐的姑娘，手艺不含糊；至于她说给它们上过色，那全是胡扯；往杯子里吹咒念符——嗐，那简直像娃娃话。”

===Original===
But next day when the pots were baked, the potter was even more surprised, for they had the most wonderful colours that he had ever seen: silver, blue, grey and yellow, in all sorts of patterns, all save the little brown cup, which was the last the gipsy had made. But when he looked at it the potter felt a little uncomfortable, and began to wonder if it really did contain the charm as she had declared.

===Chinese===
可是第二天陶器烧好之后，陶工愈发吃惊了：那些陶器上有着他生平未见过的最美妙的颜色——银白、蓝、灰、黄，配成各式花纹；唯独那只小棕杯例外，那是吉普赛女郎最后做的。可陶工一看到它，心里就有点发毛，开始寻思它是不是真像她说的那样，里头藏着咒。

===Original===
When the fair began, the potter placed all the gipsy’s wares on a stall with his own, and marked them with very high prices, but had he asked three times as much he could have got it, for there were some rich folk from the big houses who came to the fair, and they at once bought them all up, declaring that such pots and jugs they had never seen. At this the potter was well pleased, and found that he had made more money than he had earned in many a long month past; but when people wanted him to make them more like them, he was obliged to shake his head, and say, “That he was very sorry, but he had had them coloured from afar, and he did not know where he could now have them done.” Of the gipsy he saw nothing more, though he looked for her everywhere during the three days in which the fair lasted, but she was not to be seen, and when the fair was over, and the other people were packing their carts and vans to go on their way, he saw very many gipsies, and supposed that she had gone with some of them, without giving him the chance of speaking to her again.

===Chinese===
集市开张后，陶工把吉普赛女郎做的陶器同他自己的一起摆上摊子，标了很高的价钱；可就算他要三倍的价，人家也照样肯掏，因为集市上来了一些大户人家的阔绰人，一眼就把这些陶器全买光了，直说这样的盆盆罐罐从未见过。陶工见了大为欢喜，一算挣的钱，比过去好几个月挣的加起来还多。可当人们要他再做些一样的出来时，他只好摇摇头说：“实在对不住，这些是从老远的地方上的色，如今我上哪儿也寻不着再做的地方了。”集市一共开了三天，他到处找那吉普赛女郎，却再没见到她一点影儿。集市散了，别人都收拾车马准备赶路，他望见许多吉普赛人，猜想她准是随哪一伙走了，没给他再说话的机会。

===Original===
Years went by, and the potter never heard anything more of the gipsy, indeed he would have thought it had all been a dream if it had not been for the little brown pot standing on the shelf. Sometimes he took it up, and looked at it, and wondered when he saw how well and cleverly it was made. He still laughed when he remembered what the gipsy had said about leaving a charm in it, for though he himself had drunk out of it many times, he never thought it had brought any spell on him.

===Chinese===
一年年过去，陶工再没听到吉普赛女郎的任何消息；若不是架上还立着那只小棕杯，他真要以为那一切不过是一场梦。他有时把它取下来端详，看它做得那样精巧妥帖，心里直纳闷。想起吉普赛女郎说往里面留了咒，他还是发笑——虽说他自己用它喝过许多回，却从没觉出它给他带来过什么法术。

===Original===
One year when the fair was being held, the potter was at his place as usual with his stall covered with pots, and there came and placed herself beside him at the next stall a woman with some spinning-wheels. Her stall was covered with fine linen cloths woven in pretty patterns, and so fine and well wrought were they, that many people wanted to buy them. With her were her two daughters, and one sat at the spinning-wheel and spun the flax, and the other had a hand-loom and wove it when it was spun to show the good folk how the cloths were made. Both were pretty girls, but the girl who had the hand-loom had the sweetest face the potter had ever seen. Her eyes were very blue, and her hair was like golden corn, and when she smiled, it was as if the sun shone. The potter watched her as she sat weaving, and could not keep his eyes from her or attend properly to his own pots, or to the people who wanted to buy them. Every day he watched the young girl at her work, for the fair lasted for a week, and the more he looked at her the more he wanted to look, till at last he said to himself that somehow or other he must get her for his wife; so when the fair was done he begged her to marry him, and to remain with him, and he said he would always work for her, and she should want for nothing. The mother was a poor widow, and she and her daughters made their bread by going about the country spinning and weaving, and she would have been quite willing that the potter should marry her daughter, but the girl only laughed, and said that she scarcely knew the potter, but when she came back again the next year to the fair, she would give him his answer. So the widow and her two daughters went away, and no sign of them was left with the potter, save a lock of golden hair, which he had begged from the daughter.

===Chinese===
又一年集市开张的时候，陶工照旧守着自己的摊子，摊上摆满陶器；紧挨着他摊位的地方，来了一个带着几架纺车的女人。她的摊上铺着织成漂亮花纹的细麻布，那些布又细又精，许多人都想买。跟她一起的是她的两个女儿：一个坐在纺车前纺亚麻，一个架起手织机，把纺好的线织成布，让好人们瞧瞧这些布是怎么织出来的。两个姑娘都生得俊，可那个掌织机的姑娘，生着陶工生平所见最甜的一张脸。她的眼睛极蓝，头发像金黄的麦子，她一笑，仿佛天上的太阳都亮了。陶工望着她坐在那儿织布，眼睛怎么也挪不开，自己的陶器也顾不上照应，连来买陶器的人也怠慢了。集市要开一个礼拜，他天天看着那少女做活，越看越想看，末了他对自己说，无论如何也得设法娶她做妻子。于是集市一散，他便求她嫁给他，留在他身边，说他愿意一辈子为她操劳，让她什么也不缺。那母亲是个穷寡妇，带着两个女儿走乡串镇靠纺纱织布过活，她倒十分愿意陶工娶她的女儿；可那姑娘只是笑，说她跟陶工还几乎不相识，等明年再来赶集时，给他答复。于是寡妇带着两个女儿走了，没给陶工留下什么念想，只有一绺金发，是他向那姑娘讨来的。

===Original===
The year passed away, but to the potter it seemed the longest year he had ever lived. He pined for the time to come when the fair should be held, and the widow and her daughters should return. As the time drew near he got down the brown cup, and looked at it again and again. “Nay,” he said, “what harm could it do? the gipsy said it would give me my true love’s heart if she drank out of it after I had drunk, and I have drunk out of it many a time. I don’t believe it, but all the same it would be no harm for her to drink from it.”

===Chinese===
这一年过去了，可在陶工看来，这是他平生最长的一年。他苦苦盼着集市开张的日子，盼着寡妇和她女儿们回来。日子临近，他把小棕杯取下来，看了一遍又一遍。“嗐，”他说，“它能有什么害处？吉普赛人说，只要我先喝过，她再喝，它就能把真心爱人的心给我；我用它喝过多少回了。我是不信的，不过反正她用它喝一口，也没什么坏处。”

===Original===
And so when the fair was opened, he took the brown cup down with him, and stood it upon the stall with his other ware. The spinning woman and her two daughters came back with their fine cloths, and their wheel and their loom, and when he saw the golden-haired girl, he loved her still more than before, for he thought her eyes were bluer and her smile was brighter. He watched her all the time as she sat weaving, but said nothing, but when the fair was over, and they were packing their goods to go on their way, he pressed the maid for her answer. Still she hesitated, and then the potter took the little brown cup off his stall, and poured into it some choice wine, and said to her,

===Chinese===
于是集市一开张，他就带着小棕杯去了，把它同别的货色一起摆在摊子上。纺布的女人带着两个女儿回来了，带着她们的好布、纺车和织机。他一见那金发姑娘，比先前更爱她了，只觉得她的眼睛更蓝，笑起来更明媚。她坐在那儿织布，他一直望着她，什么也没说；集市散了，她们收拾货物要上路时，他催那姑娘给他答复。她还是犹豫，于是陶工从摊上取下那只小棕杯，往里倒了些上好的酒，对她说，

===Original===
“If then you wish to go away, and never see me again, I pray you drink one draught, in remembrance of the happy days we have had together.”

===Chinese===
“既然你想走，从此再见不着我，那么求你喝上一口吧，就当纪念我们在一起度过的那些快活日子。”

===Original===
The young girl took the cup, but no sooner had she tasted it than she put it down and turned her eyes on the potter, and said in a low voice,

===Chinese===
那少女接过杯子，才尝了一口就把它放下，眼睛转向陶工，低声说，

===Original===
“I will stay with you always, if you want me, and will be a true wife to you, and love you better than anything on earth.”

===Chinese===
“只要你愿意，我就永远陪着你，做你忠实的妻子，爱你胜过世上一切。”

===Original===
So the potter married her, and she went to live in his little cottage.

===Chinese===
于是陶工娶了她，她便搬进他的小屋住下了。

===Original===
Time passed, and the potter and his young wife lived together very happily, and every day he thought her fairer and sweeter. And they had a little baby girl with blue eyes like its mother’s, and the potter thought himself the happiest man on earth, and the little brown pot stood on the shelf, and the potter looked at it, and still he would not believe about the charm, for he said to himself, “My wife loved me for my own sake, and not for any silly charm or nonsense.”

===Chinese===
光阴流逝，陶工和年轻妻子过得非常和美，他天天觉得她更美、更可亲。他们生了一个小女孩，一双蓝眼睛像妈妈。陶工自认为是世上最幸福的人。那只小棕杯仍立在架上，陶工看着它，还是不肯信那咒，他对自己说：“我妻子爱的是我这个人，不是什么傻咒语、鬼名堂。”

===Original===
So for a time all things went well, but there came a day when the potter had to go to a neighbouring town and leave his wife at home alone all day. When he was gone she sat by the window with her little child, and presently there came up outside a dark, rough-looking man, with a wicked face, and he looked at her as she sat rocking the cradle, and thought she was the most beautiful woman he had ever seen on earth. When he looked at her the potter’s wife was frightened, but when he told her he was very hungry, and begged her for food and drink, she rose, for her heart was tender, and she fetched him bread and meat, and spread them on the table before him. So the rough man came into the cottage and sat at the table, and ate the potter’s bread and meat, and drank his wine. “And who is your husband, and where is he?” he said. “I am sure he is a lucky man to have such a wife and such a home.”

===Chinese===
就这样，有一阵子事事顺遂。可是有一天，陶工要到邻镇去，只得留妻子一人在家。他走后，她抱着孩子坐在窗边，不一会儿外头来了一个皮肤黝黑、面目粗野的男人，一脸凶相。他望见她坐在那儿摇摇篮，心想这是他生平在世上见过的最美的女人。他那么盯着她看，陶工之妻心里害怕起来；可他说自己饿得很，向她讨点吃的喝的，她心肠软，便起身给他拿了面包和肉，摆在桌上。于是那粗野的男人进了屋，坐在桌边，吃了陶工的面包和肉，喝了他的酒。“你丈夫是谁？上哪儿去了？”他说，“娶了这样的妻子、有这样的家，他可真是个有福气的人。”

===Original===
“Yes, truly,” said the potter’s wife. “We are very happy, and we love each other dearly, and we really have nothing else to wish for.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，真的，”陶工之妻说，“我们很幸福，彼此深深相爱，实在没有别的可求了。”

===Original===
Then the gipsy man said, “But your dress is plain, and your rooms are bare; now, were you the wife of some wealthy man, he would give you pearls and diamonds for your neck, and beautiful silks and satins.”

===Chinese===
那吉普赛男人却说：“可你的衣裳素净，屋子里空荡荡的；你要是嫁了个有钱人，他会给你珍珠钻石戴在脖子上，给你漂亮的绫罗绸缎。”

===Original===
“No, but I don’t want them,” said the potter’s wife smiling. “My husband works very hard, and he gives me all he can, and I am quite content with it.”

===Chinese===
“不，我不稀罕那些，”陶工之妻微笑着说，“我丈夫干活很卖力，能把给的都给了我，我很知足了。”

===Original===
“And you say he is a potter; then what sort of things does he make?” asked the gipsy man as he cast his eyes about the room, and they lit upon the little brown jug standing upon the shelf. “And did he make the little bowl there?”

===Chinese===
“你说他是个陶工，那他都做些什么东西？”吉普赛男人问着，眼睛在屋里四下扫视，落在了架子上立着的那只小棕杯上。“那只小碗也是他做的吗？”

===Original===
“I don’t know,” said his wife, and she took it down and turned it about in her hand. “I suppose so, but he has told me it was very old.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道，”他妻子说着把它取下来，在手里翻看，“想必是吧，不过他跟我说过，它很有些年头了。”

===Original===
The gipsy man seized it eagerly, and poured wine in it, and looked inside it, and then he laughed, and stooping his head over it, said a few words, and then laughed again.

===Chinese===
吉普赛男人急切地一把夺过去，往里倒了酒，朝杯子里面看了看，随即笑了；他低下头对着杯子说了几句话，又笑了。

===Original===
“I have seen cups like this before,” he said. “And they are worth a mint of money, though you would not think it. And have you never drunk out of it? Has it not been used?”

===Chinese===
“这样的杯子我从前见过，”他说，“值一大笔钱呢，你别看不出来。你没用它喝过酒吗？它没用过吗？”

===Original===
“I don’t drink from it,” said the potter’s wife, “but I believe I did so once, and that was on the day when I promised my husband I would be his wife.”

===Chinese===
“我不用它喝，”陶工之妻说，“不过我记得喝过一回，就是在我答应丈夫要做他妻子的那一天。”

===Original===
Then the gipsy laughed again and again. “See,” he said, “I am going a long way off, perhaps to die by cold and hunger by the roadside, while you and your husband are cosy and warm. You set small store by this cup, but it may be that in foreign countries I could sell it for what would keep me for many a long day. Give it to me, I pray you, that I may take it with me.”

===Chinese===
吉普赛人笑了一阵又一阵。“你瞧，”他说，“我要到很远的地方去了，说不定会冻死饿死在路边，你和你丈夫却舒舒服服、暖暖和和。你不把这杯子当回事，可在外国，兴许我能把它卖个好价钱，够我活上好些日子。求你把它给我吧，让我带上它。”

===Original===
The potter’s wife hesitated and trembled. She was afraid of the man, and she thought he had a hard, bad face, but she did not want to seem unkind.

===Chinese===
陶工之妻迟疑着，直发抖。她怕这个男人，觉得他一脸横肉、面目不善，可她又不愿显得刻薄。

===Original===
“Well, take it,” she said; “but why should you want it?”

===Chinese===
“好吧，拿去，”她说，“可你要它做什么呢？”

===Original===
Then the gipsy man came and caught her by the arm. “Now,” he said, “you are the fairest woman I have ever seen, and I am going away, and shall never see you again. So I beg you wish me Godspeed, and drink my health out of the little brown cup you have given me. And if your lips have touched it, it will be the dearest thing I have on earth!”

===Chinese===
于是吉普赛男人走上前来，一把抓住她的胳膊。“听我说，”他说，“你是我见过的最美的女人。我要走了，再也见不着你了。所以求你祝我一路平安，用你给我的这只小棕杯喝一口，祝我健康。你的嘴唇碰过的杯子，将是我在这世上最珍爱的东西！”

===Original===
Then the potter’s wife was still more frightened, and trembled more than before. But the man looked so dark and threatening, that she did not like to refuse him, and she took the cup in her hand.

===Chinese===
陶工之妻愈发害怕，抖得比先前更厉害。可那男人满脸阴沉凶横，她不敢回绝，只得把杯子拿在手里。

===Original===
“And then you will go on your way,” she said.

===Chinese===
“然后你就上路？”她说。

===Original===
“And then I shall go on my way,” cried the gipsy. “And you will wait here till your husband comes, whom you love more than anything else on this earth.”

===Chinese===
“然后我就上路，”吉普赛人嚷道，“你就在这儿等你丈夫回来——你爱他，不是胜过这世上一切吗。”

===Original===
Then the potter’s wife bent her head and tasted the wine out of the cup, and wished the gipsy happiness. And when she had done so he laughed again, long and low, till her heart sank with fear, and he picked up the cup and put it into his bundle, and went his way. Then the potter’s wife sat down by the cradle, and almost cried, she knew not why, and the whole room seemed cold, and when she looked out at the sunshine it looked dark, and she bent over the baby in the cradle with her tears falling.

===Chinese===
于是陶工之妻低下头，就着杯子尝了一口酒，祝吉普赛人幸福。她刚喝完，他又低低地笑了好久，笑得她心头发凉、直往下沉。他拾起杯子，塞进包袱，上路去了。陶工之妻坐到摇篮边，几乎要哭出来，自己也说不出为什么；满屋子像是冷了下来，她望望外面的阳光，阳光也显得灰暗。她俯身对着摇篮里的婴儿，眼泪直往下掉。

===Original===
“Alack!” she cried, “why doesn’t my husband come home? Where is he gone? How cruel it is to leave me all alone here, so that any rough man may come into the house. In truth I don’t think he can love me much, since all he thinks of is to go away and leave me; and as for me, surely I could have had many a better husband, and one who should have loved me more. How foolish I was to marry him.”

===Chinese===
“唉！”她喊道，“我丈夫怎么还不回家？他上哪儿去了？把我一个人撂在这儿，多狠心啊，随便什么粗野男人都能闯进屋里来。说真的，我看他不见得有多爱我，他一心想的只是出门、离开我；至于我，我本来满可以嫁个好得多的丈夫，嫁个更爱我的人。我嫁给他，真是糊涂啊。”

===Original===
Thus she sat and lamented all day, and in the evening, when the potter drove up to his door and cried out “Wife, wife,” she wouldn’t go out to receive him. And when he came in to their little sitting-room, he found her with tears in her eyes, sitting lamenting and complaining. When he went up to her to take her in his arms and kiss her, she turned away from him and would not let him touch her, and the potter, who had never seen his wife cross or angry, knew that there must be something wrong. She must be ill, he thought; tomorrow or the next day she will be well again. So he urged her to rest well, and took no notice of her angry words; but the next day, and the next, there was no change, and things were growing from bad to worse. For now the wife wouldn’t speak to him at all, and when she came nigh him she looked at him with anger, and would not even suffer him to touch the hem of her dress. Then the potter began to think of the little brown cup, and he looked up at the shelf and saw that it was not there, and he began to feel very much alarmed.

===Chinese===
她就这样坐着哀叹了一整天。傍晚陶工赶车到家门口，喊道：“老婆，老婆！”她也不出去迎接。他走进他们的小客厅，见她眼里含着泪，坐着又哀又怨。他走上前去要搂她亲吻，她把身子扭开，不许他碰。陶工从没见过妻子使性子、动怒，知道必是出了什么岔子。她准是病了，他想，明天或者后天就会好的。于是他只劝她好生歇息，不去理会她的气话。可是第二天、第三天，毫无起色，事情越来越糟。如今妻子索性不跟他说话了，一走近他就怒目而视，连衣角也不许他碰一下。这时陶工想起那只小棕杯，抬头望架子，见它不在了，心里顿时慌了。

===Original===
“Why,” he said, “what has become of my little old brown cup that used to stand up on the shelf?”

===Chinese===
“咦，”他说，“我那只常搁在架子上的小棕杯，哪儿去了？”

===Original===
“I gave it to a gipsy man,” she answered scornfully. “He seemed to like it, and I didn’t see that I was obliged to keep all the rubbish that you had in the house.”

===Chinese===
“我给了一个吉普赛男人，”她轻蔑地答道，“他好像挺喜欢它。再说，我不见得非得把你堆在屋里的那些破烂全都留着不可。”

===Original===
Then the potter groaned within himself and said,

===Chinese===
陶工心里暗暗叫苦，说，

===Original===
“But did you just take it off the shelf and give it to him, and did he ask you for it? Why did he want it?”

===Chinese===
“你就这么把它从架子上拿下来给了他？是他跟你要的吗？他要它做什么？”

===Original===
“Of course he asked for it,” said the wife very angrily, “and I just gave it him when I had drunk his health out of it, as he wished me to.”

===Chinese===
“当然是跟我要的，”妻子气冲冲地说，“照他的心愿，我用那杯子祝了他健康，喝完就给他了。”

===Original===
Then the potter was stricken with deadly fear, and remembered the words of the gipsy. “The first draught she will drink to your love, and the second draught she will drink to your hate,” and he knew in his heart that the words were true, and that the cup carried with it a charm.

===Chinese===
陶工顿时怕得要命，想起了吉普赛女郎的话：“她喝的头一口是为你的爱而饮，第二口要为你的恨而饮。”他心里明白，这话是真的，这杯子里带着咒。

===Original===
He sat and thought and thought, and waited many days, hoping that his wife would change, and love him as before, but she remained cold and hard. Then the potter packed a wallet full of clothes, and put some money in his pocket, and he went to his wife and said, “Wife, there is a man somewhere who has done me a great wrong, and perhaps he did it unwittingly. I am going out to find him, and to make him right it, and though you do not love me, you will bide here quietly with your baby till I come back. And I do not know if that will be in months or in years.” Then the potter’s wife fell a-crying.

===Chinese===
他坐着想了又想，又等了许多天，盼着妻子回心转意，像从前那样爱他，可她依旧又冷又硬。于是陶工打了一个行囊，装满衣裳，口袋里揣了些钱，走到妻子跟前说：“老婆，有个人害苦了我，也许他不是有意的。我要出去找他，叫他把这事弥补过来。虽然你不爱我了，你也安安静静带着孩子在这儿住到我回来。至于什么时候回来，是几个月还是几年，我自己也不知道。”陶工之妻哭了起来。

===Original===
“I do not love you, nay, I hate you, and shall be glad when you have gone, but perhaps it may be because I am a wicked woman; and I do not know what has come over me, that now I want to fly away from you, when I used to think that I had the best husband on all this earth.” The potter sighed bitterly, but he kissed her cheeks, which felt as cold as ice, and then he said goodbye to his baby, and started on his way with the tears filling his eyes.

===Chinese===
“我不爱你，不，我恨你，你走了我才高兴呢。不过，也许是因为我是个坏女人吧。我也不知道自己是怎么了：从前我以为自己嫁的是天下最好的丈夫，如今却一心想从你身边逃开。”陶工苦苦叹了口气，还是吻了吻她的脸颊——那脸颊冷得像冰——然后向婴儿道了别，眼里噙着泪上路去了。

===Original===
When the potter had gone the wife cried sorely, but still she was glad that she had not to see him, and for some time she lived with her baby happily enough. She kept the house, and mended and swept and cleaned as before, and thought little of the potter or where he had gone; but by and by all her money began to be spent, and she knew that unless the potter returned she would soon be very poor, and the winter was coming on, and she feared cold and hunger for her little one. So she went into a garret where she kept her old weaving loom, and she brought it out, and she bought flax, and sat down to weave just as she used to, when she went round the country with her sister who spun the flax. And she found that she could still weave her cloths very skilfully, and she began to sell them to the passers by, and in this way she earned her bread.

===Chinese===
陶工走后，妻子哭得很伤心，可她毕竟乐得不用见他。有一阵子她带着婴儿过得还算快活。她照旧操持家务，缝缝补补，扫地抹灰，不大想陶工，也不想想他去了哪里。可是渐渐地钱花光了，她知道陶工若不回来，她们很快就要穷透了；冬天又快到了，她怕孩子挨冻受饿。于是她上阁楼里搬出搁着的旧织机，买了亚麻，坐下来织布，就像从前她跟着纺亚麻的姐姐走乡串镇时那样。她发觉自己的布仍织得很巧，便开始卖给过路人，靠这个挣饭吃。

===Original===
The winter set in very cold and hard, and the potter’s wife felt very sad. “But perhaps,” she said, “it is thinking of the poor things who are starving around with no homes,” for she never thought of her husband at all. And the flax began to be very dear, and she had difficulty in buying it. “Instead of doing all these cheap clothes,” she said, “it would be better to get very fine flax, and do a very very fine cloth; it will be the finest cloth I have ever woven, and I will sell it to some very rich lady.” So she bought the finest flax that money could bring her, and when she had woven a little bit of it, she sat and looked at it in her room, and she saw a tress of her own golden hair lying upon it, and she thought how beautiful it looked. Then she said—

===Chinese===
冬天来了，又冷又严酷，陶工之妻心里很不好过。“不过，”她说，“兴许是想着四下里那些没屋没灶、忍饥挨饿的可怜人吧。”——她压根没想起过丈夫。亚麻越来越贵，她买起来很吃力。“与其净织这些粗布，”她说，“不如买顶细的亚麻，织一块顶细顶细的布；那将是我织过的最好的布，我要把它卖给哪位顶阔气的太太。”于是她买来钱能买到的最好的亚麻，织了一小段之后，坐在屋里端详，看见自己一绺金发落在布上，觉得那样子真好看。于是她说——

===Original===
“There is no one now who loves me or my hair, so I will weave it into a cloth with this very fine flax, and I must sell it for a very large sum of money, or else I shall have nothing left to go on with.” But she couldn’t think of any pattern in which the hair looked well with the fine linen flax, till at last she hit on one in which there was a cup with a heart on the top of it. The cup she made of the gold hair, and the heart also. She worked at it for many long days, and when she had finished it she looked at it, and was very much pleased, and said indeed it was the most beautiful cloth she had ever made; and now she must make haste and take it in to the town and sell it for a great deal of money, or she and her child would begin to do badly for food and fire.

===Chinese===
“如今没有人爱我，也没有人爱我的头发了，那我就把它同这顶细的亚麻一起织进布里；我一定得把这布卖出好大一笔钱，不然我就没有本钱往下过日子了。”可她想不出一个花样，能让头发配上细亚麻显得好看，后来终于想出一个：布上织一只杯子，杯顶上端着一颗心。杯子用金发织成，心也是。她织了许多许多天，织成之后端详一番，十分满意，说这真是她织过的最美的布；如今得赶紧进城去，把它卖个大价钱，不然她和孩子的吃食和柴火就要断了。

===Original===
The snow was lying heavily upon the ground, as the potter’s wife stood by the window looking at her cloth, when there crawled up outside the window a poor gipsy woman leading a little boy by the hand. She had big black eyes and a brown face, but her cheeks were so thin that the colour scarcely showed in them, and the potter himself would have had much ado to recognize her as the gipsy girl who made the cup years before; and her clothes all hung upon her in rags, and her little boy was crying bitterly with the cold. She knocked against the window with her poor thin hands. “Take me in,” she cried, “and have pity on me, for I can go no further.” Then the potter’s wife opened the door, and the gipsy woman entered the room with her little boy by her side, and crouched by the fire.

===Chinese===
地上积着厚厚的雪。陶工之妻正站在窗前看她的布，窗外爬来一个可怜的吉普赛女人，手里牵着一个小男孩。她长着黑色的大眼睛、棕色的脸，可两颊瘦得几乎看不出血色，就连陶工本人也得费好大的劲，才认得出她就是多年前做那只杯子的吉普赛女郎；她的衣裳破破烂烂挂在身上，小男孩冻得直哭。她用那双瘦骨嶙峋的手敲着窗。“让我进去吧，”她喊道，“可怜可怜我，我再也走不动了。”于是陶工之妻开了门，吉普赛女人牵着小男孩走进屋，蜷在炉火边。

===Original===
“Where is the potter who lived here?” said the gipsy. “It is long years ago since I saw him, and now I have come back to pray that he would give me food, for I am starving.”

===Chinese===
“住在这儿的那个陶工呢？”吉普赛女郎说，“我已有好多年没见他了。如今我回来求他给我点吃的，我快饿死了。”

===Original===
“No,” said the wife, “I know not where he is, for he is my husband, and he has left me, and right glad of it am I; but if you will stay here I will give you food and drink and attend to you, for, poor woman, you seem to me to be very ill; so stay here and I will attend to you till you are well enough to go your way.”

===Chinese===
“唉，”妻子说，“我不知道他在哪儿。他是我丈夫，可他把我丢下了，我还巴不得呢。不过你若肯留下，我就给你吃喝，照看你；可怜的人，我看你病得不轻，就住下吧，我照看你，等你好了再上路。”

===Original===
“There is only one way that I shall ever go,” said the gipsy, and she looked into the fire with her big black eyes, “and that is the road which leads to the churchyard. But if he was your husband, why do you say that you are glad he is away? Is he not kind to you?”

===Chinese===
“我要走的路只有一条了，”吉普赛女郎说，一双黑色的大眼睛望着炉火，“就是通往教堂墓地的那条。他既是你丈夫，你为什么说他走了你倒高兴？他待你不好吗？”

===Original===
“He was very kind to me,” said the potter’s wife, “he gave me everything I wanted, and money and to spare, but for all that I could not love him, and I am glad he has gone, and left me alone with my baby girl.”

===Chinese===
“他待我好得很，”陶工之妻说，“我要什么他给什么，钱也宽裕；可尽管这样，我爱不起来。他走了我倒高兴，留下我一个人带着小女儿。”

===Original===
“You are a foolish woman,” said the gipsy. “If you had a husband who loved you and worked for you well, you should have loved him and cherished him. My husband beat me, and was cruel to me, and stole all I had. And now that I am dying, he has deserted me to die as I may.”

===Chinese===
“你是个傻女人，”吉普赛女郎说，“有这样爱你、为你操劳的丈夫，你就该爱他、疼他。我丈夫打我，虐待我，把我的一切都偷光。如今我要死了，他倒把我甩下，任我自生自灭。”

===Original===
Then the potter’s wife brought her food and bid her lie down, and dried her rags of clothes, and she wrapped the little boy in her own clothes, and gave him food and put him to sleep; and as she lay, the gipsy woman watched her with her great black eyes, and at last she said, “Have you a brown cup here, a little rough brown cup? did your husband give it to you?”

===Chinese===
于是陶工之妻端来吃的，安置她躺下，把她破烂的衣裳烘干，又用自己的衣裳把小男孩裹起来，喂他吃了饭，哄他睡了。吉普赛女郎躺着，用她黑色的大眼睛望着她，末了说道：“你这儿有只棕杯吗，一只粗糙的小棕杯？是你丈夫给你的？”

===Original===
The potter’s wife stared with astonishment. “How did you know I had a little rough brown cup?” she said. “There was such a one, and it stood upon the shelf, but I have given it away. I gave it to a poor gipsy man who begged it of me; he wanted it so badly that I couldn’t refuse, and he made me drink his health in it ere he took it away.”

===Chinese===
陶工之妻惊得直瞪眼。“你怎么知道我有只粗糙的小棕杯？”她说，“是有那么一只，一直搁在架子上，可我给掉了。我给了一个讨要它的穷吉普赛男人；他那么想要，我不好回绝；他临走还叫我用它祝了他健康。”

===Original===
Then the gipsy woman raised her head, and her eyes looked blacker and her cheeks blacker.

===Chinese===
吉普赛女人抬起头来，眼睛显得更黑，脸颊也更暗了。

===Original===
“And what was the gipsy man like?” she cried. “Had you drunk from the cup before? Can you remember?”

===Chinese===
“那吉普赛男人长什么样？”她喊道，“你先前用那杯子喝过吗？还记得吗？”

===Original===
“I remember well,” said the woman. “I drank from it on the day when I promised I would marry my husband, and I drank from it once again when I wished the gipsy Godspeed, and soon after that, my husband left me, for I could not bear to have him near me.”

===Chinese===
“记得清楚，”那女人说，“我答应嫁给丈夫的那天，用它喝过；后来祝那吉普赛人一路平安，又喝过一回。打那以后不久，我丈夫就离开了我，因为他在跟前我就受不了。”

===Original===
Then the gipsy cried out aloud, and said something in a language which the woman did not understand, and beat her hands.

===Chinese===
吉普赛女郎放声喊了出来，说了几句那女人听不懂的话，直拍双手。

===Original===
“I think it was my husband,” she said. “Alack a day! tomorrow night I shall die, and who will take care of my little boy, and see that he does not starve? for his father would beat and ill-treat him if he found him.” Then the potter’s wife kneeled down beside the gipsy woman, and kissed her on the forehead.

===Chinese===
“我想那准是我丈夫，”她说，“天哪！明天夜里我就要死了，谁来照看我的小男孩，别让他饿死呢？他爹要是找着他，是要打他、糟践他的。”于是陶工之妻跪在吉普赛女人身边，吻了吻她的额头。

===Original===
“Be at peace,” she said. “If it be that you must die, die with a quiet heart, for I will keep your little boy. What is enough for two is enough for three, and he shall call my little girl sister and me mother.”

===Chinese===
“安心吧，”她说，“你若真难免一死，就安安心心地去吧，你的小男孩我留下。够两个人吃的，就够三个人吃；他会管我的小女儿叫妹妹，管我叫妈妈。”

===Original===
The gipsy said nothing, but she looked at the potter’s wife for long, and then she said, “And my clothes are all in rags, and I have no garment in which you can wrap me for my grave.”

===Chinese===
吉普赛女郎没作声，只久久望着陶工之妻，然后说：“我的衣裳全破了，我没有一件能裹身入殓的衣裳。”

===Original===
Then the potter’s wife began to cry. “Be at peace,” she said, “for I have a fine cloth made of flax and my own hair, and in it you can lie clad like a princess.”

===Chinese===
陶工之妻哭了起来。“安心吧，”她说，“我有一块细布，是亚麻和我自己的头发织的，你裹上它入土，就像公主一样。”

===Original===
Then again the gipsy woman cried out words that the potter’s wife did not understand, and again she beat her breast and lamented. But as evening drew nigh she turned to the potter’s wife, and told her all the truth about the charms in the cup, and wept for the evil she had done her, who was so good and kind.

===Chinese===
吉普赛女人又喊出一些陶工之妻听不懂的话，捶着胸口哀叹。可是天色将晚时，她转向陶工之妻，把杯中咒语的真相原原本本告诉了她，为自己带给这个善良仁厚的好人的祸害，哭了。

===Original===
The potter’s wife sat by her all that day, and into the dark hours of the night, but when it was drawing nigh to twelve o’clock the gipsy woman sat up, and stretched out her arms. “The wheel,” she cried, “bring me your husband’s wheel, and give me a piece of clay, that while there is yet time I may throw my last cup, and you may drink from it before the dawn and undo the harm I have worked.”

===Chinese===
陶工之妻在她身边守了一整天，一直守到夜深。快到十二点时，吉普赛女人坐起身，伸出双臂。“陶轮，”她喊道，“把你丈夫的陶轮给我搬来，再给我一块黏土，趁还有工夫，让我拉我最后一只杯子；天亮前你可以用它喝，把我造下的孽解掉。”

===Original===
The potter’s wife wondered much, but she feared to disobey her, and she went out into her husband’s workshop, and she brought in his wheel and a piece of clay which stood there, and placed them beside the gipsy. The gipsy was so weak that she could scarcely sit up, but when she saw the wheel she staggered to her feet, and took the clay in her thin little brown hands, and moulded it as she had done years before; and then she set it on the wheel, and set the wheel spinning, and formed it into a little brown bowl, and bent her head over it, and whispered into it.

===Chinese===
陶工之妻大为诧异，却不敢不依，便走进丈夫的工棚，搬来他的陶轮和放在那儿的一块黏土，摆在吉普赛女郎身边。她虚弱得几乎坐不住，可一看见陶轮，便挣扎着站起来，用她瘦小的棕色双手抓起黏土，像多年前那样捏塑起来；随后把它放上陶轮，拨得陶轮转起来，塑成一只棕色的小碗，低下头去，对着它低低耳语。

===Original===
“Now drink,” she cried, “although the clay is still wet. Pour water into it and drink from my little bowl, and wish me Godspeed as you did to my husband. And then dress me in white and gold like a princess, for I must start upon my journey. But keep my little boy always, and if my husband comes to search for me, give him my ring, but tell him that he shall never find me more.”

===Chinese===
“现在喝吧，”她喊道，“泥还湿着也不碍事。倒点水进去，用我这小碗喝了，像待我丈夫那样，祝我一路平安。然后给我穿上金白两色的衣裳，像公主一样，因为我该动身上路了。只是要永远留着我的小男孩；若是我丈夫来寻我，把这枚戒指给他，告诉他，他永远也找不着我了。”

===Original===
The potter’s wife poured some water into the little clay cup, and stooped her face and drank it, that the woman might be content, and when she had done so, the gipsy folded her hands and lay back and died. But when she had tasted the water out of the wet clay, the potter’s wife thought of her husband, and she called his name, and cried to him to come and help her with the poor gipsy woman. And then she thought of how long it was since he had been with her, and she began to cry, and wept bitterly as she leant over the dead woman.

===Chinese===
陶工之妻往那小小的泥杯里倒了些水，俯下脸去喝了，好叫那女人心安。她刚喝完，吉普赛女郎便合起双手，向后一仰，死了。可那湿泥杯里的水一沾唇，陶工之妻就想起了丈夫，她唤着他的名字，哭喊他回来帮着照应这可怜的吉普赛女人。接着她想到他离开她已这么久这么久，便哭了起来，伏在那死去的女人身上，悲悲切切地哭。

===Original===
“Oh, where is he gone? why did I drive him from me?” she said. “Have I been mad? Truly the poor gipsy spoke rightly, that if a woman has a husband who loves her and works for her, she should cherish him with all her might. Alas, alas! and now my husband is out in the wide world, and I am alone here with no one to help me; until this poor woman told me, I never knew how wrong I was.” Then she looked at the gipsy woman lying in all her rags, and she remembered her promise to her, and she took the fine linen cloth in which was woven the gold heart and the gilt cup, and she clothed her in it as if she were a princess, and the next day the poor woman was buried, and no one knew from whence she came nor to whom she belonged.

===Chinese===
“唉，他上哪儿去了？我怎么把他从我身边赶走的？”她说，“我是疯了吗？那可怜的吉普赛女人说得真对：女人若有爱她、为她操劳的丈夫，就该竭尽全力疼他。唉，唉！如今我丈夫在茫茫世界的外头，我一个人在这里，没人帮衬；若不是这可怜的女人告诉我，我永远不会知道自己错得多深。”随后她望着躺在破衣烂衫里的吉普赛女人，想起自己的许诺，便取来那块织着金心和描金杯子的细麻布，把她裹好，仿佛给她穿上了公主的衣裳。第二天，这可怜的女人下了葬，没有人知道她从哪里来，也没有人知道她是谁家的人。
===Original===
Then the potter’s wife sat down, and grieved bitterly, for she didn’t know what it would be best to do to find her husband again, and tell him that she loved him as at first. At first she thought she would go out and seek for him in the wide, wide world, but then she remembered how he bid her wait where she was till he came back, and she knew she ought to do what he had told her; but as now she had three to keep instead of two, she feared they would be very poor, and as she had buried the gipsy in the fine gold and white cloth woven with her hair, she had not got it to sell, and she had not any money left wherewith to buy more fine thread to weave. The gipsy’s little boy was a pretty boy, with dark eyes like his mother’s, and when she looked at him she said they would all three starve together, but she would keep him, as she had promised his mother, rather than turn him out into the cold streets. So she washed him, and mended his rags as best she might, and then she began to seek everywhere for something she might weave to sell, and keep them from starving. She wandered round the garden, and in and out of the house, and the gipsy boy, who was a clever, bright lad, went with her.

===Chinese===
随后陶工之妻坐下来，悲伤地哭泣，因为她不知怎样才能寻回丈夫，告诉他她还像当初一样爱他。起初她想走到宽广宽广的世界里去寻他，可随即想起他吩咐她原地等他回来，她知道该听他的话；可如今她要养活的是三口而不是两口，只怕要穷得很；她又把吉普赛人裹在那块用自己头发织的金白细布里下了葬，那块布没法卖了，也没有余钱再买细线来织。吉普赛人的小男孩是个俊孩子，一双黑眼睛像他母亲；她望着他，心里说：三个人就一起挨饿吧，可她答应过他母亲，宁可如此，也绝不把他赶到冰冷的街上去。于是她给他洗了澡，尽力缝补他的破衣裳，然后开始四处寻找可以织来卖的东西，好叫他们不挨饿。她在园子里转来转去，在屋里屋外找，吉普赛少年（gipsy boy）是个机灵乖觉的孩子，跟在她身边。

===Original===
“What are you searching for?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“您在找什么呀？”他问。

===Original===
“I am searching for thread, that I may weave into some kind of cloth and sell,” she said. “Otherwise we shall surely starve, for I have no money left to buy it with, and nothing more left to sell.”

===Chinese===
“我在找线，好织成什么布卖掉，”她说，“不然我们准得挨饿；我没钱买了，也没东西可卖了。”

===Original===
“I will go and get you something to weave,” said the boy, and he ran out into the road, and looked up and down it to see what would come past. Presently there came up a big cart laden with straw, and on the top of the cart lay one man, while another drove. The horses went slowly, and the gipsy boy followed them, and began to beg.

===Chinese===
“我去给您找点能织的东西来，”少年说着跑到大路上，上下游张望，看有什么过来。不一会儿过来一辆装满稻草的大车，一个人躺在车顶上，另一个赶着车。马走得很慢，吉普赛少年跟在后面讨要。

===Original===
“Run off, little chap,” cried the man at the top, “I have no money to give to beggars.”

===Chinese===
“走开吧，小家伙，”车顶上的男人喊道，“我没钱打发叫花子。”

===Original===
“But I don’t ask you for money,” cried the boy, “but of your charity give me a handful of straw.”

===Chinese===
“我不是要钱，”少年喊道，“行行好，给我一把稻草吧。”

===Original===
“And what do you want with a handful of straw?” asked the carter, as the boy still went on begging.

===Chinese===
“要一把稻草干什么？”赶车的问，少年还是一个劲儿地讨。

===Original===
“Why, see!” cried the lad, “I am all in rags, but if my mother had a handful of straw she can weave me a coat, and I shall be quite warm,” at which the men both laughed, and declared that the idea of a coat of straw was very funny, but the driver said, “Well, give some to the little chap. I expect he comes from a lot of lying gipsies further on, and they want it for their animals, still it won’t do any harm to give him a few wisps,” and so they flung down a bundle, and the boy picked it up, and ran back with it to the potter’s wife.

===Chinese===
“嗐，您瞧！”少年喊道，“我浑身破破烂烂的，我妈妈要有一把稻草，就能给我织件外套，我就暖和了。”两个男人听了都笑，说稻草外套这主意真逗；可赶车的说：“喏，给小家伙一些吧。我估摸他是前头那一大帮子满嘴谎话的吉普赛人家的，想要回去喂牲口；不过给他几把也没什么。”于是他们扔下一小捆，少年拾起来，抱着跑回陶工之妻那儿。

===Original===
“See what I have brought you,” he cried. “Now make that into a mat, and I will take it out and sell it, and bring you back the money.”

===Chinese===
“瞧我给您带什么来了！”他喊道，“把它织成垫子吧，我拿去卖，把钱带回来给您。”

===Original===
The potter’s wife was amazed by his cleverness, but she knew that the gipsies had to live by their wits, and that teaches them to be sharp, so she sat down, and tried to weave the straw into a mat, as the gipsy boy had said.

===Chinese===
陶工之妻惊异于他的机灵，不过她知道吉普赛人得靠心计过活，那把他们磨得精明；于是她坐下来，照吉普赛少年说的，试着把稻草织成垫子。

===Original===
At first she found it very hard to use, for it was coarse and brittle, and she thought she could make nothing of it. The lad sat beside her, and cut it into even lengths for her, and chose out the good pieces, and at last betwixt them it was done, and it looked quite a smart little mat, and the boy took it on his back and ran away with it to the village.

===Chinese===
起初她觉得这材料很难使，又粗又脆，心想根本织不成什么。少年坐在她旁边，替她把草剪成一样长短，挑出好的一段段；末了两人合力织成了，看上去是顶齐整的一块小垫子。少年把它背在背上，一溜烟跑到村子里去了。

===Original===
“A mat, a mat,” he cried, “who wants to buy a good straw mat to wipe their feet on when they are dirty, or for the cat to sit on by the fire, or to put over the fowl-house and keep it warm?”

===Chinese===
“垫子，垫子，”他喊道，“谁要买块好草垫？脚脏了踏一踏，猫儿烤火坐一坐，或者盖在鸡窝上保暖——谁来买呀？”

===Original===
At first all the people he met laughed at him, and said nobody wished to buy a mat at all. Then he turned into the alehouse. There were some men smoking and keeping themselves warm by the fire, and when the host saw him, and the mat over his shoulder, he said it was quite a well-made thing, and he would have it to lay down by his doorway for in-comers to tread on; and then one and another looked at it, and the boy told them where it came from, and said he could bring them plenty more straw mats and carpets, all as good or better, and so well worked that they would last almost forever; and presently one and another began to say that they would buy them, and when he had taken his money, the gipsy boy ran home well content.

===Chinese===
起初他碰到的人都笑他，说根本没人想买垫子。后来他拐进酒馆。里面有几个男人抽着烟在炉边取暖；店主一见他，见他肩上背着垫子，说这东西做得蛮像样，要买下来铺在门口给进来的人踩。接着这个看看、那个瞧瞧，少年告诉他们垫子的来历，说他还能拿来许多草垫草毯，一样好或者更好，织得那样结实，几乎能用到永远。不一会儿便这个也要买、那个也要买；少年收了钱，心满意足地跑回家去。

===Original===
So the potter’s wife sat all day weaving straw mats, and presently she got to do them so well, that from far and near the people sent to buy them of her. Then after a time she put patterns into them, made with red, and black, and white straws, but do what she could, the patterns always came out in the shape of a cup, and still she wept and grieved all day long. Then the gipsy lad said to her—

===Chinese===
就这样，陶工之妻整天坐着织草垫，不久织得极好，远远近近的人都来向她买。又过了一阵，她往垫子里织进花样，用红的、黑的、白的草；可无论她怎么织，那花样总出来一个杯子的形状，她还是整天流泪伤心。于是吉普赛少年对她说——

===Original===
“What are you crying for now? You have plenty to eat and drink. Tell me why you are crying, and I will help you if I can, because you took my mother into your house to die, and buried her in your fine cloth like a princess.”

===Chinese===
“这会儿您哭什么呀？吃的喝的都不愁了。告诉我您为什么哭，能帮的我一定帮，因为您把我母亲接进家里送终，还用您那块细布把她像公主一样下葬。”

===Original===
“I cry because my husband has gone a long way off,” said the potter’s wife, “and he doesn’t know that I love him, and he will never come back to me, for when he went away I hated him.”

===Chinese===
“我哭，是因为我丈夫到很远的地方去了，”陶工之妻说，“他不知道我爱他，他再也不会回到我身边了，因为他走的时候，我还恨着他。”

===Original===
“He will never know it if you don’t try to tell him,” said the gipsy boy. “You should tell it to everyone you meet, to all the birds of the air, and the wild animals too. That is what my mother told me to do, if I wanted to send news abroad. You should say it even to the winds, and write it in the sand, and on the earth, and on the leaves of the trees in case they blow about, for she said all things could pass on a secret, though none can keep one. And why don’t you weave it into your mats too? For the people who buy them take them far and near, and maybe he will see one, and know that you want him to come home again.”

===Chinese===
“您不去想法子告诉他，他就永远不会知道，”吉普赛少年说，“您该逢人就讲，讲给天上所有的鸟儿，讲给野地里的走兽。我母亲说，要把消息传出去，就这么办。连风您也该对它说，还要写在沙上、写在泥地上、写在树叶上，免得风吹散了漏掉；她说万物都能把秘密传下去，虽然没一个能守住秘密。您怎么不把它也织进垫子里呢？买垫子的人把它们带得远远近近，说不定他看见一块，就知道您盼他回家了。”

===Original===
Then the potter’s wife tried to weave her secret into her mats, and beside the pattern of the cup she wove a little verse—

===Chinese===
于是陶工之妻试着把自己的心事织进垫子，在杯子花样的旁边，织了一小节诗——

===Original===
“From the gipsy’s cup I drank for love,

From the gipsy’s cup I drank for hate,

But when she gave me a cup again

My love had gone and I drank too late.”

===Chinese===
“我曾从吉普赛人的杯中为爱而饮，

我曾从吉普赛人的杯中为恨而饮，

可当她再递给我一只杯，

我的爱已去，我饮得太迟。”

===Original===
“Now,” cried the gipsy boy, “your husband may see it, and perhaps he will come home, and all will be well with us.”

===Chinese===
“这下好了，”吉普赛少年喊道，“您丈夫兴许会看见，也许就回家了，咱们就都好过了。”

===Original===
But still the potter journeyed far into the world, and wherever he went he asked if any gipsy had been near there; and if there happened to be a gipsy camp in the neighbourhood, he went to it at once, and asked for a gipsy woman with red beads and gold chains in her hair, or for a gipsy man who carried a brown cup with him. But though he saw hundreds of gipsies, yet he never again saw the girl who had thrown the cup, and none of the men knew anything about the man, nor could tell him anything about the little brown bowl. Then he went to the shops in the big towns where jars and bowls are sold, and asked for a cup that had a spell in it, for he thought if they sold such a one, they might know how to help him to undo the work of the gipsy’s bowl, but everywhere the people only laughed at him.

===Chinese===
可陶工仍在世上远行，每到一处，就打听附近可来过吉普赛人；那一带若恰有吉普赛人的营地，他便立刻找上门去，寻问一个头发里编着红珠子金链子的吉普赛女人，或是寻问一个随身带着棕杯的吉普赛男人。可他虽见过成百上千的吉普赛人，却再没见过拉出那只杯子的女郎；男人们谁也不知道那个男人，也说不出那只小棕碗的来历。于是他又到大城镇里卖坛卖碗的铺子去，求购一只带咒的杯子，心想他们若卖过这样的杯子，兴许知道怎么帮他破掉那吉普赛碗的法术；可走到哪儿，人们都只拿他取笑。

===Original===
So he went through strange countries, seeing strange things, but none of them gave him any pleasure, since he was always thinking of his wife at home. Then he returned to his native land, and pondered whether he should go back to his own cottage, but his heart failed him, and he kept far from the little village where it stood.

===Chinese===
他就这样走过一个个陌生的国度，见了许多新奇的事物，可没有一样能给他半点快乐，因为他心里总想着家里的妻子。后来他回到故土，盘算着要不要回自己的小屋去，可又鼓不起勇气，始终远远躲着小屋所在的那个小村子。

===Original===
“It would be little use to go home,” he said, “for if my wife is not glad to see me, it is no home to me; and she will not be glad to see me till I can find the gipsy and know how the charm can be broken.”

===Chinese===
“回家也没什么用，”他说，“妻子若见着我不高兴，那就不是我的家；找不到那吉普赛人、不知道咒怎么破，她见了我高兴不起来。”

===Original===
One night he went into a booth where there were a number of men drinking, and amongst them there was one who looked like a gipsy, a dark, savage-looking fellow who was talking loud, and boasting much of all he had done. The potter sat and listened to their talk, and presently they began quarrelling, and talking about who was the most beautiful woman in the world. The gipsy cried out that he knew the most beautiful, and that she had given him a parting gift and wished him Godspeed, and now he was going back to her, for he knew now the way to make her love him, and he meant to wed her and have her for his wife.

===Chinese===
一天夜里，他走进一家棚屋酒肆，里面有许多男人在喝酒，其中有一个看模样是吉普赛人，黑黑的、一脸凶悍，正大声说话，大吹自己干过的事。陶工坐着听他们讲话，不多时他们争吵起来，争的是世上谁家的女人最美。那吉普赛人嚷道，他认得天下最美的女人，她送过他临别的礼物，祝过他一路平安；如今他要回到她身边去，因为他已经知道叫她爱他的法子，他要娶她，让她做自己的妻子。

===Original===
Upon this the others laughed and jeered, and said, was it likely that such a beautiful woman would care for such a rough, ill-favoured fellow as he, and declared they didn’t think much of her beauty if she was willing to marry him and to be his wife.

===Chinese===
众人听了又笑又挖苦，说那样美的女人，岂会看上他这样粗鲁丑陋的家伙；还说她若肯嫁他做妻子，她的美貌也不见得怎么样。

===Original===
Then another man standing near said that he knew where lived the most beautiful woman on the face of the earth, though he did not believe that she would ever be wife of his, still all the same it would be hard to beat her for loveliness; and she was a clever worker too, for she it was who worked the mats that lay under his feet in the cart he drove. Upon this they all began to wrangle, and their words grew high.

===Chinese===
这时站在旁边的另一个男人说，他知道天下第一美的女人住在哪儿；虽说他不信那女人会做他的妻子，可论俊俏，谁也难胜过她；而且她还是个巧手，他赶的那辆车脚下垫的垫子，就是她织的。这一来众人吵得更凶，火气越来越大。

===Original===
“And if the beautiful woman loved you so,” cried one man to the gipsy, “how could you come away and leave her?”

===Chinese===
“那美人既然那么爱你，”一个人冲着吉普赛人喊，“你怎么舍得走开，把她丢下？”

===Original===
The gipsy laughed. “She didn’t love me then,” he said, “but she will now, for I am taking her a charm which will make her love me more than anyone on earth. She has only to drink out of the cup I carry here, and she will be mine for life.”

===Chinese===
吉普赛人笑了。“那时她还不爱我，”他说，“如今就不同了，因为我给她带着一件法宝，能叫她爱我胜过世上任何人。她只要用我这儿带的这只杯子喝上一口，就一辈子是我的人了。”

===Original===
Upon this they all laughed, and derided him still more.

===Chinese===
众人听了越发笑他、奚落他。

===Original===
“Then let everyone believe that what I say is true,” cried the gipsy, and from his bosom he took out a small brown bowl and waved it in the air, “and here is the cup to prove it.” And the potter’s heart almost stood still, for he recognized the cup which the gipsy girl had made for him years before.

===Chinese===
“那就叫大伙儿都信我说的是真话！”吉普赛人喊道，从怀里掏出一只棕色的小碗，在空中一挥，“这就是凭证，杯子在这儿！”陶工的心几乎停止了跳动——他认出来了，这正是吉普赛女郎多年前给他做的那只杯子。

===Original===
The other man laughed scornfully. “That proves nothing,” he said. “I might take the mat out from the cart and ask it to say if I spoke the truth; but mats and cups have no tongues to speak with, though my mat can say more than your cup, for there is a rhyme on it with a pattern of a cup; moreover, the rhyme is about a gipsy too.”

===Chinese===
另一个男人讥诮地笑了。“这证明不了什么，”他说，“我也可以从车里把垫子拿出来，问它我说的是不是真话。垫子和杯子都没有舌头讲话；不过我的垫子比你的杯子能说，上面织着一首押韵的诗，还带着杯子的花样；再说，那诗里讲的也是个吉普赛人。”

===Original===
“Let us see it,” cried they all.

===Chinese===
“拿来瞧瞧！”众人都喊。

===Original===
Then the man went out to his cart and fetched in a white and brown straw mat, covered with a pattern made of cups, and he read the rhyme which was written upon it—

===Chinese===
于是那人出去，从车上取来一块白棕相间的草垫，上面满是杯子的花样，他念了织在上面的那首诗——

===Original===
“From the gipsy’s cup I drank for love,

From the gipsy’s cup I drank for hate,

And when she gave me that cup again

My love was gone and I drank too late.”

===Chinese===
“我曾从吉普赛人的杯中为爱而饮，

我曾从吉普赛人的杯中为恨而饮，

当她再递给我那只杯，

我的爱已逝，我饮得太迟。”

===Original===
On hearing this the potter jumped up, and dashed into their midst, and seized the cup.

===Chinese===
一听这话，陶工霍地跳起来，冲进人堆，一把夺过杯子。

===Original===
“The gipsy speaks truth,” he cried, “when he says she is the most beautiful woman in the world, but he speaks false when he says that she will ever love him; for he has stolen that cup, and I shall take it from him, and if he tries to stop me, why then I will fight him, and let everyone see who is the better fellow of the two.”

===Chinese===
“吉普赛人说她天下最美，这话不假，”他喊道，“可他说她会爱他，这话是假的！那杯子是他偷来的，我要从他那把它拿回来；他若敢拦我，哼，我就跟他打一架，让大伙儿瞧瞧，咱俩哪个才算堂堂男子汉。”

===Original===
But when the gipsy had seen the rhyme upon the mat, he stood and stared as if he were made of stone, and said no word to the potter, and indeed scarcely seemed to notice that he had taken away the cup from him. Then the potter turned to the man who owned the mat and said, “If you will sell me your mat I will pay you handsomely for it, and I beg you to tell me who made it, and where you got it, for I would like to buy some more like it.”

===Chinese===
可那吉普赛人见了垫子上的诗，直挺挺站着发怔，像变成了石头，对陶工一言不发；陶工把杯子从他手里拿走，他似乎也浑然不觉。于是陶工转向垫子的主人说：“你肯把垫子卖给我，我出大价钱；还求你告诉我，这是谁织的，你从哪儿弄来的，我想再多买几块这样的。”

===Original===
The traveller was much astonished, but he told the potter that it was made by a woman who lived in a village a little way off, and she sat by her doorway and wove mats, with a gipsy boy to help her; and she was the loveliest woman he had ever seen on earth, with eyes just like blue cornflowers and hair like golden corn. Then the potter took his bowl and the mat and started to go home, but the gipsy slunk out of the room and went into the night, and nobody noticed him.

===Chinese===
那赶车人十分诧异，不过还是告诉陶工，这垫子是住在不远处一个村子里的女人织的；她坐在自家门口织垫子，有个吉普赛少年给她打下手；她是他生平在世上见过最标致的女人，眼睛蓝得像矢车菊，头发像金黄的麦子。于是陶工带上杯子和垫子，动身回家；那吉普赛人却溜出屋去，没进了黑夜里，谁也没有留意。

===Original===
Meantime the potter’s wife continued to grieve and lament, for in spite of her taking the gipsy boy’s advice, and telling all things that she loved her husband and wished him back, he did not come back to her; and though she wove her rhyme into every mat that she made, she despaired of the potter’s ever seeing one. The only thing which seemed to console her, was the little brown clay cup that the gipsy woman had thrown for her, before she died. As it had never been baked in the oven, the clay was dry and hard and cracked, and it was a sorry thing to look at, but still the potter’s wife kept it beside her, and would drink out of nothing else, and from time to time she kissed it, and laid her cheek against it.

===Chinese===
这期间，陶工之妻仍旧悲叹伤心；虽说她听了吉普赛少年的主意，逢着万物便诉说自己爱丈夫、盼他回来，他却没有回来；她虽把那首诗织进织的每一块垫子，也几乎不指望陶工会见到一块。唯一能给她安慰的，是吉普赛女人临死前给她拉的那只棕色小泥杯。它从没进窑烧过，泥又干又硬，裂着缝，样子实在寒碜；可陶工之妻仍把它带在身边，喝水只用它，还不时吻它，把脸颊贴在上面。

===Original===
The gipsy boy said to her—

===Chinese===
吉普赛少年对她说——

===Original===
“If I were you I should watch for my husband all day. I would weave my mats in the doorway, and look up the road both ways, from morn till night, otherwise your husband will come back and go past the cottage and you will never know.” So she took her loom and sat by the roadway, and watched, and looked over the hill and to right and left for whoever might come. And often the gipsy boy would watch too, and look from the other side of the cottage while the potter’s wife sat in the front. One day the gipsy boy ran round to her and said, “There is someone coming up the road who will come here, but it is not your husband. It is my father, and he will want to take me away, and he will beat me as he did my mother. And if he gets hold of the cup that my mother made for you, he knows all her charms, and he can undo what she did, and perhaps can throw some evil spell on us all, so that your husband will never return again. So the best thing will be for you to give me the cup and let me hide myself with it, and then you must tell him that you do not know where I am, and if he asks, tell him that the cup is gone; and when he is gone I will come back again, but promise that you will not give me up to him.”

===Chinese===
“我要是您，就整天守着等丈夫。我会坐在门口织垫子，从早到晚望着大路两头，不然您丈夫回来了，打小屋门口走过去，您还不知道呢。”于是她搬了织机，坐在路边守着，望着山那边、望着左右，看有谁来。吉普赛少年也常常帮她望——陶工之妻坐在屋前，他就从屋子另一头张望。有一天，吉普赛少年跑到她跟前说：“路上来了个人，要到这儿来，可不是您丈夫。是我父亲，他要把我带走，还要像打我母亲那样打我。他要是拿到我母亲给您做的那只杯子——她的咒他全会，能把她的法术解掉，兴许还能给咱们大伙儿下个恶咒，叫您丈夫永远回不来。所以顶好您把杯子给我，让我带着它藏起来；然后您就对他说，不知道我上哪儿去了；他要是问，就说杯子也没了；等他走了我就回来。您得答应我，绝不把我交给他。”

===Original===
So the potter’s wife promised that she would never give up the little boy, and she bid him take the cup and run quickly and hide himself, and then she took her little girl by the hand and sat and waited for the gipsy man to come, though she trembled with fear, and wished him far away.

===Chinese===
于是陶工之妻答应绝不交出小男孩，叫他拿了杯子快去藏好；随后她牵着小女儿的手坐下，等着那吉普赛男人来，虽然她怕得发抖，一心盼着他走得远远的。

===Original===
Presently the gipsy man came up to the front of the cottage where the potter’s wife sat, and bid her good day.

===Chinese===
不一会儿，吉普赛男人走到小屋前陶工之妻坐着的地方，向她道了日安。

===Original===
“I was here before,” he said. “And you gave me something to eat and drink. Is your husband come back, for he was away then?”

===Chinese===
“我从前来过这儿，”他说，“你还给过我吃喝。你丈夫回来了吗？那时候他不在家。”

===Original===
“My husband is away still,” she said. “But soon I hope he will be here.”

===Chinese===
“我丈夫还在外头，”她说，“不过我盼着他快回来了。”

===Original===
Then the gipsy took up one of her mats which lay on the ground beside her, and looked at it.

===Chinese===
吉普赛人拾起她身边地上的一块垫子，看了看。

===Original===
“You are clever with your loom,” he said; “but what do you mean by the little verse you put on all these mats?”

===Chinese===
“你织机上的功夫不错，”他说，“可你这些垫子上织的小诗，是什么意思？”

===Original===
“It is a little verse which can but be rightly read by one person,” she answered; “and if he sees it, it will not matter whether others understand it or no.”

===Chinese===
“那是一首小诗，只有一个人能真正读懂，”她答道，“他若看见了，旁人懂不懂都不要紧。”

===Original===
“And have you been here all alone since I came by?” asked the gipsy; “have no other gipsies been past? for I want to join some of my own people, and perhaps you can tell me which way they are gone.”

===Chinese===
“打我上次路过，你一直一个人在这儿？”吉普赛人问，“没别的吉普赛人打这儿过吗？我想找我的同族人，兴许你能告诉我他们往哪边去了。”

===Original===
“One came not so long ago,” answered the potter’s wife, “but she was so tired with tramping far that she could go no further. So she has stayed, and rests in the churchyard. She was a gipsy woman with red beads and coins in her hair. And I kept her and let her die in peace, and wrapped her in a cloth of white and gold.”

===Chinese===
“不久前来过一个，”陶工之妻答道，“可她走了太多路，累得再也走不动了。她就留了下来，如今在教堂墓地里安息。她是个头发里编着红珠子和钱币的吉普赛女人。我收留了她，让她安安静静地死去，又用一块金白两色的布裹了她。”

===Original===
“And did she do nothing while she rested here?” asked the gipsy man. “Did she make you no present to pay you for your trouble?”

===Chinese===
“她在这儿歇着的时候，什么也没做吗？”吉普赛男人问，“没送你点什么，谢你的辛苦吗？”

===Original===
“She made me a present which paid me for my trouble well,” said the woman, “though it was only a little cup of clay that was grey and wet. And she gave me this ring, and bid me give it to her husband if he came by here, and tell him that it was useless for him to seek her further.”

===Chinese===
“她送了我一件谢礼，把我的辛苦报答得足足的，”那女人说，“虽不过是一只灰扑扑、湿漉漉的小泥杯。她还给了我这枚戒指，嘱咐我，她丈夫若打这儿过，就交给他，告诉他，再找她也是白找。”

===Original===
The gipsy man looked at the ring she held out to him, and he turned pale, and knit his brows.

===Chinese===
吉普赛男人看着她递过来的戒指，脸发了白，眉头拧了起来。

===Original===
“And where is that cup?” he asked; “and where is her little boy? For I will take him with me into the world.”

===Chinese===
“那杯子呢？”他问，“她的小男孩呢？我要带他到世上去。”

===Original===
“I don’t know where he is gone,” said the potter’s wife; “as for the cup, he took it with him when he went.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道他去哪儿了，”陶工之妻说，“杯子嘛，他走的时候带走了。”

===Original===
Meantime the gipsy boy had hidden in a haystack quite close to the cottage, from where he could see the roadways all round, and he looked to right and left for who should pass, for he was still half afraid that his father might come and search for him, and take him away by force. As he lay and watched he saw a man coming over the hill, who looked spent and tired, as if he had walked far. He seemed to know the path well, and he came straight to the cottage, but he did not come in, but waited near as if he wanted to see who was there. Then the gipsy boy said to himself—

===Chinese===
这时，吉普赛少年藏在离小屋很近的一个草垛里，从那儿能望见四下的路口。他左张右望，看有谁经过，因为他心里还有几分怕父亲来搜他，硬把他拖走。他正躺着望，看见一个人翻过山来，看那模样疲惫不堪，像是走了很远的路。那人好像很熟这条路，径直朝小屋走来，却不进屋，只在近旁停住，像要先看看里头有谁。于是吉普赛少年心里想——

===Original===
“Perhaps this is the potter himself, whom she has been looking for all this time.” So he slid down and ran to the man and began to pretend to beg.

===Chinese===
“说不定这就是陶工本人，就是她找了这么久的那个人。”于是他溜下草垛，跑到那人跟前，装作讨乞。

===Original===
The man looked at him and said—

===Chinese===
那人望着他说——

===Original===
“You are a gipsy’s child. Where do you come from? Are you living under a hedge, or do you come from a gipsy’s camp near?”

===Chinese===
“你是个吉普赛人的孩子。打哪儿来？是住在树篱下，还是从近处的吉普赛营地来？”

===Original===
“It is true I am a gipsy’s child,” answered the boy, “but I am living under no hedge, but in that little cottage, for the woman who lives there keeps me for love of my mother, who helped her when she was in trouble.”

===Chinese===
“我确实是吉普赛人的孩子，”少年答道，“可我不住在树篱下，我就住那座小屋里；住在那儿的太太因为爱我母亲才留下我，我母亲在她落难的时候帮过她。”

===Original===
“And what did your mother do for the woman?” asked the man, who was no other than the potter. “It must have been a great service, that she should be willing to take you and keep you.”

===Chinese===
“你母亲为那女人做了什么？”那人问——他不是别人，正是陶工。“那必定是天大的恩情，她才肯收留你、养着你。”

===Original===
“She saved her from an evil charm that had been cast upon her,” answered the boy, “and taught her to love her husband again, and she waits his return now and longs for him to come. Therefore she promised to keep me with her, but now I dare not go into the cottage, because my father, who is a gipsy, is there, and I am afraid lest he may take me away with him.”

===Chinese===
“她救了她，把她从加在她身上的恶咒里解了出来，”少年答道，“教她重新爱自己的丈夫；她如今正等他回来，一心盼着他回家。所以她答应把我留在身边；可现在我不敢进屋去，因为我父亲——他是个吉普赛人——在那儿，我怕他把我一起带走。”

===Original===
When the potter heard that the gipsy man was there he would have run straight into the cottage, but the boy implored him to listen first and hear what he was saying. So they crept round to the side of the cottage, and they heard the gipsy man growing angry, and threatening the potter’s wife, that if she did not tell him where his boy had gone he would seize her by the hair and wring her throat, in spite of her being so fair a woman. At this the potter waited no longer, but burst into the cottage, and seized the gipsy and hurled him out of the house with all his might; but he and his wife never looked to see if he was hurt or no, for they looked at nothing but each other and the little child that the potter’s wife held by the hand. And the gipsy man went away, and they never heard of him again.

===Chinese===
陶工一听那吉普赛男人在屋里，恨不得立刻冲进去，可少年央求他先听一听那人正在说什么。于是他们蹑手蹑脚绕到屋侧，听见吉普赛男人正在发狠，威吓陶工之妻，说她若不说出孩子去了哪儿，就揪住她的头发、拧断她的喉咙——也不管她生得多俊。陶工再也忍不住，破门而入，一把揪住吉普赛人，使出浑身的力气把他掼出屋去；可两口子谁也没去看他摔伤了没有，他们眼里只有彼此，和陶工之妻牵在手边的小女儿。那吉普赛男人走了，从此再没有听到他的消息。

===Original===
Then the potter’s wife showed her husband the gipsy boy, and told him of her promise to his mother, and of all he had done for her, and begged him that he would let her keep him with them. And the potter promised that she should, and said that when he grew up to be a man he would teach him his trade, and make him a potter like himself. So they all lived happily together, and the gipsy boy learned to make cups and bowls, and was very clever at doing them, but they were cups and bowls that carried no charms with them, and so could do no one any harm that drank from them.

===Chinese===
随后，陶工之妻把吉普赛少年指给丈夫看，讲了她对他母亲的许诺，讲了他为她做的一切，求丈夫答应让她把他留在身边。陶工答应了，还说等他长大成人，要把自己的一身手艺教给他，把他调教成一个和自己一样的陶工。于是他们全都快快活活地生活在一起。吉普赛少年学会了做杯做碗，做得十分灵巧；只是他做的杯杯碗碗，再也不带什么咒，谁喝了也不会受害了。

## The Ploughman and the Gnome / 农夫与地精

===Original===
A young ploughman was following his plough in a field one morning when suddenly the horses stopped, and do what he would he could not make them stir. Then he tried to push the plough himself, but he could not move it one hair’s-breadth. He stooped down to see what could be stopping it, when a deep voice cried, “Stop, I am coming up.” The voice was so loud that the ploughman shook with fear, but though he looked all around him, he could see no one from whom it could come. But presently it spoke again (only this time it was a little lower), and called out, “Have patience, and I shall be up in a moment.” The ploughman quaked in every limb, and stood quite still, and the voice began again (but this time it was no louder than most folks’), and it said—“If you will only not be in such a hurry, I will tell you what I want. Look in front of your horse’s right foot, and pick me up.”

===Chinese===
一天早晨，一个年轻的农夫正赶着犁在田里犁地，马儿忽然停住了，他怎么吆喝，马也一步不肯挪。他又亲自去推犁，可那犁连一丝一毫也推不动。他弯下腰去看是什么东西挡住了犁，忽听一个低沉的声音喊道：“站住，我要上来了。”那声音响得吓人，农夫浑身发抖，可他环顾四周，却看不出这声音能是从谁那里发出来的。不一会儿，那声音又说话了（只是这一回低了些），喊道：“耐心点儿，我这就上来。”农夫四肢打颤，一动也不敢动。那声音又开口了（可这一回，已不比寻常人说话更响），说道：“只要你别这么性急，我就告诉你我要什么。往你马儿右蹄前面看，把我捡起来。”

===Original===
He bent down and looked on the ground, and there in the earth, just in front of his horse’s right foot, he saw what he thought was a little black lizard. He touched it very cautiously, and started back with surprise when the voice spoke again, and he found it came from this tiny creature.

===Chinese===
他弯下身往地上看，就在马儿右蹄前面的泥土里，他看见一样东西，还当是一条黑色的小蜥蜴。他小心翼翼地碰了碰它，那声音又响了起来，他惊得直往后缩——原来声音竟是从这小小的东西里发出来的。

===Original===
“Yes,” it said, “that is quite right. You can pick me up in your hand if you like, but I think I must grow a little bigger, as I am really uncomfortably small,” and while he held it on the palm of his hand, the ploughman saw that it was beginning to grow larger, and it swelled so fast that in a few seconds it was near a foot high, and he had to take both hands to hold it. Then he saw that it was not a lizard, but a little black woman with a face that looked as though it were made of india-rubber, and ugly little black hands.

===Chinese===
“是啊，”它说，“这就对了。你若乐意，可以把我搁在手心里；不过我想我得长大一点儿，我实在小得难受。”农夫把它托在掌心上，只见它开始长大，而且胀得飞快，不消几秒钟就有将近一尺高了，他得用两只手才捧得住。这时他看清了，那不是蜥蜴，而是一个小小的黑女人，一张脸像是橡胶做的，还生着一双丑丑的黑色小手。

===Original===
“There, that will do,” said the strange little gnome. “That is a nice useful size. Oh dear, how tiring growing is! I don’t think I’ll be any bigger just yet. Now be sure you don’t drop me, and handle me very carefully, for I do not like to be roughly touched. I have not slept nearly as long as I meant to. I wanted a hundred years’ nap, and it cannot be more than fifty, but now that I am awake I think I will keep so for a bit. You seem to be rather a nice civil young man. How would you like to take me for a lodger?”

===Chinese===
“好了，这样正好，”那古怪的小地精（gnome）说，“这大小又合适又管用。唉呀，长大可真累人！我想这会儿不必再变大了。你千万拿稳了，别把我摔着，捧的时候要轻轻的，我不喜欢叫人粗手粗脚地摆弄。我这一觉睡得远没有打算的那么长。我本想睡上一百年，如今看来顶多睡了五十年；不过既然醒了，我打算醒上一阵子。你瞧着倒是个挺和气、懂礼数的年轻人。你愿不愿意留我做个房客？”

===Original===
“A lodger!” gasped the ploughman. “Why, what should I do with you?”

===Chinese===
“房客！”农夫倒抽一口气，“哎呀，留你做什么用呢？”

===Original===
“I should give no trouble,” said the gnome. “But are there any women in your house?”

===Chinese===
“我不会添一点儿麻烦，”地精说，“只是你家里有女人吗？”

===Original===
“No,” said the ploughman, “for I have no wife, and I am too poor to keep a servant.”

===Chinese===
“没有，”农夫说，“我没娶妻，又穷得雇不起仆人。”

===Original===
“So much the better,” said the gnome. “For though I am a woman myself, I detest women, and only get on with men.”

===Chinese===
“那就更好了，”地精说，“我虽说自己是个女人，却最厌烦女人，只同男人合得来。”

===Original===
“You a woman!” cried the ploughman, and he laughed outright.

===Chinese===
“你是个女人！”农夫嚷起来，放声大笑。

===Original===
“Of course I am a woman,” said the creature. “Come, say quickly, do you like to have me for a lodger or not? Of course you will have to agree to my terms.”

===Chinese===
“我当然是女人，”那东西说，“来，快说，你到底愿不愿意留我做房客？自然，你得答应我的条件。”

===Original===
“And what are your terms?” asked the ploughman.

===Chinese===
“你的条件是什么？”农夫问。

===Original===
“Only this. Whatever comes into the house, you must always give the best of it to me. I will choose where I shall live for myself, when I see the house, but of all the food you have, you must save the best and give it to me. Not much of it, but the very best pieces. As you are a man I cannot wear your clothes, but you can give me some of their material, and of everything else that comes into the house of any sort, tobacco, or carpets, or furniture, I must have some of the best. And at meals I must always be helped first. If you agree to this, I may stay with you for a very long time.”

===Chinese===
“只有这一条：凡是进这个家门的东西，顶好的那份总得给我。等见了你的屋子，我自己挑住哪儿；你所有的吃食里，也得把最好的留下给我。不用多，可得是最精美的几口。你是男人，我穿不得你的衣裳，可你可以给我些衣料；至于旁的进家门的东西，不论什么，烟草也好，地毯也好，家具也好，样样我都得拿一份最好的。吃饭的时候，回回都得先给我上。你若应下这些，我兴许会在你家住上很久很久。”

===Original===
“Oh, oh,” said the ploughman, “and pray what shall I get by it? It seems to me as if you wanted to get the best of everything and give nothing in return.”

===Chinese===
“哦，哦，”农夫说，“那请问，我能落着什么好处呢？照我看，倒像你什么好的都想占了，却一点儿回报也不给。”

===Original===
“On the contrary,” replied the gnome, “I shall give a very great deal. For as long as I remain in your house, all things will go well with you. You are a poor man now, but you will soon be a rich one. If you sow seeds they will give twice as much crop as other people’s. All your animals will do well, and in a little time, instead of being a poor ploughman, you will be the richest farmer in the countryside.”

===Chinese===
“恰恰相反，”地精答道，“我会给你非常多。只要我住在你家，事事都会顺顺当当。你如今是个穷人，可很快就阔了。你撒下种子，收成会比别人家的多上一倍。你的牲口只只兴旺，要不了一会儿，你就不是穷农夫，而是方圆一带最富的富农了。”

===Original===
“Well,” quoth the ploughman, “I don’t mind trying. I think it would rather amuse me to take you to my cottage; but if you don’t keep your part of the bargain, and I don’t find things are going very well with me, I warn you I’ll pretty soon turn you out.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，”农夫说，“试试倒也不妨。把你带回我的小屋，想来还挺有意思。可你要是不守约，我的日子也不见起色，那我先说下，我很快就把你撵出去。”

===Original===
“Agreed,” said the gnome; “but remember, if you fail in your compact with me, I shall go by myself. Now carry me home and let me choose where I will live.”

===Chinese===
“一言为定，”地精说，“不过记住，要是你先失了约，我就自己走了。现在带我回家，让我挑挑我住哪儿。”

===Original===
The ploughman carried the odd little figure back to his cottage, gaping with astonishment; there he put it down on the kitchen-table in the little kitchen. It looked all round it, and twisted about its little black head.

===Chinese===
农夫把那古怪的小人儿抱回自己的小屋，惊讶得直合不拢嘴；到了家，他把它放在小厨房的饭桌上。它四下张望，黑色的小脑袋转来转去。

===Original===
“That will do nicely,” it said at last; “there is a little hole in that corner, down which I can go, and near that hole you must place all your daintiest bits, and remember that I must always be helped first at all your meals.” And without a word it leaped from the table, and scuttled away down a big hole that the rats had made, and was no more to be seen.

===Chinese===
“这地方倒不错，”它终于说，“那个墙角有个小洞，我能钻下去；你要把顶精细的吃食都搁在洞口边上。记住，每顿饭，都得先给我上。”话音才落，它一句话不说就从桌上跳下去，钻进一个耗子掏出的大洞里，再不见踪影了。

===Original===
But when in the evening the ploughman came in to eat his meal, before he began it he took the very best bit of meat and the nicest of the vegetable, and laid them down near the hole. Then he watched eagerly to see what would happen, but while he looked there they remained. Suddenly, however, the door shut with a bang, and he turned his head for a moment to see what caused it, and when he looked back the food had disappeared. Every day it was much the same. He put some of the best food on the table down near the hole, but as long as he watched there it remained, but when he took his eyes off for a moment it had disappeared. In the same way when he had new clothes, he took a choice bit of material and laid it near the hole, and it vanished also. And of whatever came into the house he took some of the best and did the same with it.

===Chinese===
可到了傍晚，农夫进屋吃饭，动筷之前，他挑了顶好的一块肉、最水灵的一样菜，搁在洞口附近。他眼巴巴守着看有什么动静，可只要他望着，那些吃食就一动不动。忽然，门“砰”地一声关上了，他扭头看了一瞬，再回头时，吃食已经没了。天天都是这样：他把顶好的饭菜放在桌上洞口边，只要他盯着，东西就还搁在那儿；可他眼睛一错开，东西就不见了。添新衣裳时也一样，他拣一块好料子放在洞口，它照样消失。凡是进了家门的物件，他都拣最好的取一份，照这么办理。

===Original===
Meantime things began to improve very much with him. He had only a little bit of land round his cottage, but this year the vegetables and fruit he had planted there grew so well that he had a large quantity to send to market, and he sold them for such good prices, that soon he was able to get more land and buy his own animals, and in a little while had a farm of his own, and had grown to be quite a rich man, while all his neighbours said his luck was extraordinary. Meantime he saw or heard nothing more of the little black gnome, and except when he put the food and other thing’s near the hole almost forgot all about her.

===Chinese===
这期间，他的光景一天天好起来。他原只有小屋周围一小片地，可这一年他种的瓜菜果木长得极好，收了满满一大茬运去集市，卖了好价钱，不久便添置了更多田地，买了自己的牲口，不多时竟有了自家的农庄，成了个相当殷实的人，左邻右舍都说他的运气真是少见。这期间，那个小黑地精再没露过面，也没一点儿声息，除了往洞口放吃食和旁的东西的时候，他几乎把她忘了个干净。

===Original===
Time passed, and the time came when the ploughman began to think he would like to take a wife; he made up his mind to marry a very pretty girl in the next village, who was said to be the prettiest girl in all the neighbourhood. Many of the young men would have liked to marry her, but the ploughman was a handsome, cheery young fellow, and she preferred him to all of the others, and so they were married, and she came home to live at the farm. The evening after their wedding they had a fine fat fowl for supper, and the ploughman before he helped his wife cut off the choicest slice from the breast and took it as usual to the hole.

===Chinese===
光阴流转，农夫动了娶亲的念头；他看中了邻村一位非常标致的姑娘，据说她是方圆左近最美的女子。多少年轻后生都想娶她，可农夫生得俊朗、性子爽快，她谁都看不上，偏偏中意他，于是两人成了亲，她便来农庄安了家。成亲那天晚上，两口子晚饭吃一只肥美的鸡，农夫还没给妻子切肉，先从鸡胸上片下最精美的一片，照老规矩送到洞口去了。

===Original===
“Husband,” cried the wife, “have you gone mad that you should give the best of the food to the rats and the mice?”

===Chinese===
“当家的，”妻子嚷道，“你莫不是疯了，把最好的吃食拿去喂耗子？”

===Original===
“I am not mad at all,” said the ploughman, “but my grandfather loved nothing in the world so well as rats and mice, and he made me promise before he died that they should always be well cared for in my house, and have of the best.”

===Chinese===
“我一点儿没疯，”农夫说，“只是我祖父在世时，天底下什么都不爱，就爱耗子，临终前还要我起誓：在我家里，一定得好生供养它们，顿顿给最好的。”

===Original===
“Then if you are not mad,” replied the wife, “I think your grandfather was! It is only the best poison that is good for rats and mice, and they shall have it soon, now that I am in the house.” But the ploughman caressed his wife and begged her to let him keep his promise to his grandfather, and the wife held her peace, not liking to seem bad-tempered on her wedding-day. After a bit she got used to her husband putting down little bits of food, as he said, for the rats and mice, and though she always declared she was going to poison them, she did not try to do so, as her husband seemed grieved when she talked about it.

===Chinese===
“你要是没疯，”妻子答道，“那我看你祖父才疯了！对付耗子，只有顶好的耗药才顶事。如今我进了这个门，很快就叫它们尝尝。”可农夫又是哄又是求，求妻子容他守对祖父的诺言，妻子也就不再言语——大喜的日子，她不愿显得脾气坏。过了些日子，她渐渐习惯了丈夫往洞口搁些吃食——照他的说法，是喂耗子的；虽说她总嚷着要下药毒死它们，却始终没动手，因为一提这事，丈夫便面露愁容。

===Original===
Thus things went on very happily for some months, when the wife began to think that her clothes were getting very old, and that she must have some new ones. So she took plenty of money and went into the neighbouring town, and came home with new dresses, and hats, and bonnets, and very pretty she looked in them, and her husband was very much pleased with them. But that evening after his wife was gone to bed, as the ploughman was finishing his pipe in the kitchen, he suddenly heard a deep voice from the hole, which called out just as it had done months before, “Stop, I am coming up.”

===Chinese===
就这样，两口子顺顺和和过了好几个月。后来妻子渐渐觉得衣裳旧了，该添几件新的。于是她带上充足的银钱，去邻镇走了一趟，买回新裙子、新帽子、新软帽，穿戴起来甚是好看，丈夫也满心欢喜。可那天晚上，妻子睡下之后，农夫在厨房里抽完最后一斗烟，忽然听见洞里传来一个低沉的声音，同几个月前一模一样地喊道：“站住，我要上来了。”

===Original===
For an instant the ploughman quaked with fear, then he saw something no bigger than a black beetle creeping through the hole, and it came in front of his chair, and he heard the voice, which was not so loud this time, say—

===Chinese===
刹那间，农夫吓得发抖；随后他看见一个不比黑甲虫大多少的东西从洞里爬出来，爬到他椅子跟前，他听见那声音——这一回不那么响了——说道：

===Original===
“That will do, now I am going to begin to grow a little,” and it began to grow, and grow, and grow, till it was about eight inches high, and the ploughman saw it was the little black woman. “There,” she said, speaking quite quietly, “that is a nice useful size, that will do. Now I have something to say to you, and you will have to attend very carefully. I consider that you are breaking your compact. In the first place, you married without asking my leave, and, as I told you, I don’t like women in the house, but I will say nothing about that, as we had not spoken about it before, but how can you explain about all the fine clothes that your wife fetched home today? She has taken them to her room and not given one to me!”

===Chinese===
“好了，现在我要开始长大一点儿了。”它便越长越大，越长越大，长到约莫八寸来高，农夫看清了，正是那个小黑女人。“瞧，”她平平静静地说，“这大小又合适又管用，正好。现在我有话对你说，你须仔仔细细听着。我认为你正在毁约。头一件，你娶妻也不问过我一声；我早说过，我不喜欢家里有女人，不过这事我们先前没讲过，我就不追究了。可你妻子今天买回家的那些漂亮衣裳，你又怎么解释？她全拿进自己房里去了，一件也没给我！”

===Original===
“Nay,” cried the ploughman, “they are my wife’s clothes, not mine.”

===Chinese===
“哪里，”农夫嚷道，“那是我妻子的衣裳，不是我的。”

===Original===
“Nonsense,” said the gnome, “you gave her the money for them. Now understand that whatever she buys for herself in the future, she must buy the same for me. Two of everything: dresses, hats, gloves, whatever she has, I must have too, and be sure that mine are quite as good as hers.”

===Chinese===
“胡说，”地精说，“买衣裳的钱是你给她的。你给我听好：往后她给自己买什么，就得照样给我买一份。样样都得成双：裙子、帽子、手套，她有什么，我就得有什么，还要记住，我的那份得同她的一样好。”

===Original===
“But how am I to manage that?” cried the ploughman; “how can I explain it to her without telling her that you are there?”

===Chinese===
“可这叫我怎么办理？”农夫嚷道，“不告诉她你在那儿，我怎么跟她说得清呢？”

===Original===
“That is your business,” said the gnome. “All I say is that I must have the things if I am to remain in your house. You can tell her what you please. So now you know, and see that you do as I tell you,” and suddenly the little figure shrunk up till it was about the size of a black beetle, and then disappeared down the hole without another word.

===Chinese===
“那是你的事，”地精说，“我只说一句：要想我留下，东西我就必须要。你随你怎么跟她讲都行。话我搁在这儿了，照我说的办。”说罢，那小小的身影倏地缩下去，缩得只有黑甲虫大小，一声不响地钻进洞里不见了。

===Original===
The ploughman rubbed his head, and wondered what he could do. He did not at all want to tell his wife about the little gnome, for he was sure she would not like it, but at the same time he did not want the gnome to leave his house and take away his luck.

===Chinese===
