农夫挠着头，不知如何是好。他实在不想把小地精的事告诉妻子，他知道她准不乐意；可他同样不想叫地精离开家，把他的好运气一并带走。

===Original===
A few days after, his wife told him she was going to the shoemaker’s to buy herself some smart new shoes, and the ploughman thought of the gnome, and knew he must do as she had told him. So he said to his wife, “Wife, when you get those shoes for yourself, I wish you would get a pair just like them for my cousin who has written to me to ask me for a present. I should like to send her some nice boots and shoes as she is very poor, so I shall be very much obliged if you will get two pairs of whatever you may get for yourself that I may send her one.”

===Chinese===
过了几天，妻子告诉丈夫，她要去鞋匠铺给自己买几双漂亮的新鞋。农夫想起了地精，知道必须照她吩咐的办。于是他对妻子说：“娘子，你给自己买鞋的时候，劳驾照样子替我表妹也买一双——她写信来托我送她件礼物。她日子过得清苦，我想给她几双好靴子好鞋。所以不论你给自己买什么，都请买上两双，我好送她一双，那就多谢你了。”

===Original===
The wife wondered very much, for she did not know the ploughman had any cousin; however, she went into the town, and brought home two pairs of smart red shoes with bows on the top.

===Chinese===
妻子好生纳闷，她从不知道丈夫还有什么表妹；不过她还是进了城，买回两双漂亮的红鞋，鞋面上缀着蝴蝶结。

===Original===
When she had gone to bed at night, the ploughman took one pair and laid it by the hole in the same place where he had put the food, and it disappeared just as the food did without his seeing where it went. “Now,” thought he, “when she sees I am quite honest, perhaps the ugly little gnome will be content, and let us go on in peace.”

===Chinese===
夜里妻子睡下后，农夫拿起一双，放在洞口放吃食的老地方，它便像吃食一样消失得无影无踪，他也没瞧见它去了哪儿。“这下好了，”他心想，“等她见我如此诚实守信，这丑陋的小地精兴许就满足了，容我们太太平平过日子。”

===Original===
So time went on, and the ploughman and his wife lived very happily and quietly, till one evening a pedlar came round with a tray holding all sorts of pretty things to sell. The ploughman’s wife went to the door, and looked at the things: then she bought a pretty comb for her hair, but she would not show it to her husband, as she meant to wear it before him as a surprise next day.

===Chinese===
日子就这样过去，农夫两口子和和美美、安安静静地过活。直到一天傍晚，来了个货郎，托着满满一盘各色漂亮玩意儿沿门兜售。农夫妻子上前看货，给自己买了一支好看的发梳；她不肯拿给丈夫瞧，打算第二天戴在头上，给他一个惊喜。

===Original===
But that evening after his wife had gone to bed, as the ploughman sat finishing his pipe by the fire, he heard the voice from the hole calling as loudly as ever, “Stop! I am coming up.” Again the ploughman quaked with fear, and then he saw coming through the hole something no bigger than a black beetle, and again the voice said in a lower tone, “Now I will begin to grow a little,” and presently the tiny black thing had swelled into the ugly little black woman with the face like india-rubber.

===Chinese===
可那天晚上，妻子睡下之后，农夫坐在炉边抽完最后一斗烟，又听见洞里的声音扯开嗓门喊道：“站住！我要上来了。”农夫又吓得发抖，随后看见一个不比黑甲虫大的东西从洞里钻出来，那声音又放低了些说：“现在我要长大一点儿了。”顷刻间，那小小的黑东西已胀成了那个面如橡胶的丑陋小黑女人。

===Original===
“Listen to me,” she said, “and know that I am beginning to feel very angry. You are beginning to cheat me. Today your wife bought herself a brand-new comb from a pedlar at the door, and never got one for me. Tomorrow evening I must have that comb. I don’t care how you get it, but have it I must.”

===Chinese===
“你听着，”她说，“要知道我已经开始动怒了。你开始糊弄我了。今天你妻子在门口从货郎手里给自己买了一支崭新的发梳，就没给我买。明晚之前，我必须拿到那把梳子。我不管你用什么法子，反正我必须要到。”

===Original===
The ploughman scratched his head and was sore perplexed. “What on earth am I to do?” he cried, “for my wife will think me very cruel if I take away all the pretty little things she buys for herself.”

===Chinese===
农夫挠着头，一筹莫展。“我到底怎么办才好？”他嚷道，“妻子自己买的这些漂亮小玩意儿，若叫我都拿走，她只当我狠心死了。”

===Original===
“I can’t help that,” answered the gnome. “I have got to have that comb by this time tomorrow night, and I warn you if you begin to deceive me, just as if I were an ordinary human being, I shall pretty soon take myself off,” and with that the gnome disappeared through the hole in an instant.

===Chinese===
“那我管不着，”地精答道，“明晚这时辰之前，我必须拿到那把梳子。我警告你，你要是存心糊弄我，把我当寻常人来哄骗，我很快就走。”说罢，地精一眨眼便钻进洞里不见了。

===Original===
Next morning at breakfast the wife came down with the new comb in her hair, and said to her husband, “See, husband, I bought this of the pedlar yesterday, and he tells me they are quite the newest fashion, and all the great ladies in town are wearing them.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨吃早饭时，妻子戴着那支新发梳走下楼来，对丈夫说：“瞧，当家的，这是我昨天从货郎手里买的。他说这是顶新的式样，城里的阔太太们人人都戴呢。”

===Original===
“Well,” quoth the ploughman, “such a fashion may be all very well for the great ladies who have scarce any hair of their own, but, for my own part, I had rather see your beautiful hair just as it is without any adornment.”

===Chinese===
“哦，”农夫说，“这种时新样式，搁在那些自己没剩几根头发的阔太太头上倒也罢了；要依我说，宁看你那一头好头发，什么都不戴才最好看。”

===Original===
At this the wife pouted, and was very cross. “ ’Tis too bad of you to say that. I thought you would like your wife to wear all the new fashions, and be smart like other folks.”

===Chinese===
听了这话，妻子撅起嘴，好不气恼。“你这话说得可太不该了。我还当你喜欢妻子赶时新、跟旁人一样打扮得俏丽呢。”

===Original===
“Nay,” cried the ploughman, “my wife is much prettier than other folks, and she looks prettiest of all when she has little to adorn her. If any of these great ladies had hair like yours you may be sure they would pretty soon throw away any combs or caps or pins, so that nothing but their hair should be seen.”

===Chinese===
“哪里的话，”农夫嚷道，“我的妻子本就比旁人标致，越是少戴首饰，越是好看。那些阔太太但凡有你这样的头发，保管很快就把梳子呀、帽子呀、簪子呀统统扔了，单叫人看她们的头发。”

===Original===
When her husband was gone, the wife went to her glass and looked at herself, and took out the comb and then put it in, and tried it every way. “ ’Tis true, for sure,” said she, “my hair is very beautiful, and maybe it looks best done up as I used to wear it, still it seems a pity not to use the comb when I have bought it.” So when her husband came back, she said to him, “I believe you are right, husband, and it suits me better not to have anything in my hair, but maybe if you are wanting to send a present to that cousin of yours, you would like to send her that comb. It would save buying anything fresh.”

===Chinese===
丈夫走后，妻子对着镜子左照右照，把梳子取下来又插上去，怎么摆弄都试了一遍。“这话倒真是不错，”她说，“我的头发确实好看，也许照我原先的梳法更好；只是买了梳子不用，又怪可惜的。”于是丈夫回来时，她对他说：“当家的，我想你说得对，我头上什么都不戴更相宜。不过，你若正想送你那位表妹礼物，兴许乐意把这支梳子送她，也省得再破费买新的了。”

===Original===
On this the ploughman laughed to himself, but he thanked his wife very much and put the comb in his pocket. In the evening after the wife had gone to bed, the ploughman took it, and put it down by the hole, and then went on smoking his pipe without waiting to see if it disappeared. But in a few minutes he heard the voice crying, “Stop! I am coming up,” and saw again the gnome come through the hole and then begin to grow as before. “Now this is too bad,” cried the ploughman. “What can you want now? Here I have just given you the comb you wanted, and nothing else new has come into the house.” “On the contrary,” answered the gnome, “I consider that you have brought a great many new things into the house since I came to live here, and I mean now to have my choice of some of them, since I do not find that you are honest enough to offer them to me. To begin with, I want your wife’s hair. I have been trying mine with that comb, and I find I can’t make it do at all, and so I mean to have your wife’s.”

===Chinese===
农夫听了暗自发笑，嘴里却千恩万谢，把梳子揣进口袋。晚上妻子睡下后，他取出梳子，搁在洞口边，接着自顾抽烟，也不等看它消失。可不消几分钟，他又听见那声音喊：“站住！我要上来了。”只见地精又从洞里钻出来，像先前一样开始长大。“这也欺人太甚了，”农夫嚷道，“这回你还要什么？我才把你想要的梳子给了你，家里再没添过别的新东西。”“恰恰相反，”地精答道，“我算着打我住进这儿，你家里添了许多新东西；如今我瞧你也未必肯诚心诚意献给我，那我就自己动手挑几样。头一件，我要你妻子的头发。我拿那梳子试过我自己的头发，怎么也梳不来，所以我打算要你妻子的。”

===Original===
“My wife’s!” gasped the ploughman. “You must be mad!”

===Chinese===
“我妻子的头发！”农夫倒抽一口气，“你准是疯了！”

===Original===
“Mad or not mad,” replied the gnome, “I mean to have it, and, moreover, it is my due. You married without consulting me, and if I kept you to your bargain, I should have a great deal that I have not got. Certainly your wife has the best head of hair in the house, so you must cut it off near her head and bring it all to me.”

===Chinese===
“疯也罢，不疯也罢，”地精答道，“我反正要定了，况且这本是我分内应得的。你娶妻不曾同我商量；当初要是照约行事，我该得的东西还多着呢。你妻子无疑有着这一家最好的头发，所以你得贴着她的头皮铰下来，一并给我送来。”

===Original===
“But whatever shall I say to my wife?” cried the ploughman in distress.

===Chinese===
“可我怎么跟妻子交代呢？”农夫愁苦地嚷道。

===Original===
“That is your lookout, not mine,” said the gnome. “Anyhow you have got to give it to me. But as the thought of it seems to annoy you I will give you a week to get it in.”

===Chinese===
“那是你的事，与我无干，”地精说，“横竖你得把它给我。不过瞧你这般烦恼，我就宽限你一个礼拜。”

===Original===
The ploughman sat and thought and thought, and very sad did he feel at thinking of all his wife’s beautiful hair being given away to the little gnome.

===Chinese===
农夫坐着想了又想，一想到妻子那满头秀发竟要送给小地精，心里就说不出的难过。

===Original===
Next day he took his horse and cart, and told his wife he had to go for a long drive on business to a big town, a long way off. It was quite the biggest town in that neighbourhood, and many very fine people lived there. At first the wife wanted to go too, but her husband said it was too far and she would be too tired, as he could not be back till very late at night.

===Chinese===
第二天，他套上马车，对妻子说有要事得出一趟远门，去一座很远的大城。那是方圆一带顶大的城，住着许多体面人物。起初妻子也要同去，丈夫说路太远，她该累着了，他不到深夜回不来。

===Original===
Next morning, when they sat at breakfast, he told his wife all he had heard and seen in the big town, and then he added, “And all the very fine ladies there have now the funniest fashion.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，两口子坐下吃早饭，他把大城里的所见所闻一五一十讲给妻子听，末了又添上一句：“那城里顶体面的太太们，如今兴起一种顶好笑的打扮。”

===Original===
“And what is that?” asked his wife; “pray tell me, for I love to hear the new fashions.”

===Chinese===
“是什么呀？”妻子问，“快说给我听，我最爱听这些新鲜时样。”

===Original===
“Why,” said the ploughman, “ ’tis with their hair. Instead of wearing it long, they have it cut quite close all round their heads, because they say it looks smarter now.”

===Chinese===
“嘿，”农夫说，“就出在头发上。她们不留长发，把脑袋四周铰得精短，据说是这样才显得俏丽精神。”

===Original===
“Well, I do call that a silly fashion,” said the wife; “they can’t have had much hair to consent to have had it cut off.”

===Chinese===
“嗬，这我可要说是个傻样式，”妻子说，“她们准是头发本来就不多，才舍得铰了去。”

===Original===
“No, indeed,” said her husband, “and yet with some of them, they look very smart and pretty with their little curly heads.”

===Chinese===
“倒也未必，”丈夫说，“她们有些人剪成一头小卷毛，看着还挺俊俏利落的。”

===Original===
“Much like boys, I should think,” said the wife scornfully.

===Chinese===
“我看跟毛头小子差不多。”妻子不屑地说。

===Original===
“No, not quite that, either,” said the ploughman, “more like the pictures of angels in the old churches, and they say it is the great thing for it to curl up all round the head, and when it does that of itself, they are very proud of it.”

===Chinese===
“不，也不尽然，”农夫说，“倒更象老教堂里画像上天使的模样。据说顶要紧的是头发四边自己卷起来；要是能自己卷起来，她们就得意得不得了。”

===Original===
“Well, then, some of them might be very proud of mine,” said the wife, “for it’s as curly as may be, and if I were to cut it short would be all in tiny curls.”

===Chinese===
“那么，她们要见了我这头发，怕不都得眼馋死了，”妻子说，“我这头发卷得不能再卷了，要是铰短了，保管满头都是小卷儿。”

===Original===
When her husband had gone to his work, the ploughman’s wife could do nothing but think of the strange new fashion of which her husband had told her. “I wonder how it would suit me,” she thought, and when he came in to dinner she said to him—

===Chinese===
丈夫下地干活去了，妻子满脑子想的都是他讲的那桩奇怪的新时样。“不知道我剪了合不合适。”她心想。丈夫回来吃晚饭时，她便对他说——

===Original===
“Husband, is it really true that all those fine ladies looked very pretty and smart with their hair short?”

===Chinese===
“当家的，那些阔太太剪了短发，当真个个又俊又俏吗？”

===Original===
“Ay, that they did,” he said; “I was quite surprised to see them, and I heard they said ’twas a wonderful saving of trouble, and that their hair could never grow untidy.”

===Chinese===
“可不，当真，”他说，“我瞧着都吃了一惊。听她们说，这样省事极了，头发再也乱不了。”

===Original===
“That is true,” said the wife, “yet I should be sorry to cut mine off.”

===Chinese===
“这话不假，”妻子说，“可要我把头发铰了，我还是舍不得。”

===Original===
“No need that you should,” said the ploughman, “and there are not many folks up here to see if we are in the fashion or not. All the same, you are sure to look prettier than the town ladies anyway, whichever way your hair is done, for your face is prettier.”

===Chinese===
“不必非铰不可，”农夫说，“咱们这地方偏僻，也没几个人来看咱们赶不赶时样。不过话说回来，你的脸蛋比城里太太们俊，头发随便怎么梳，你横竖都比她们好看。”

===Original===
But when her husband had gone away again the wife went to her glass with the scissors in her hand. “As my husband says,” she quoth, “it would be a wonderful saving of trouble, and then it would be very nice to let all the women round see that I could be in the fashion before they. I wonder how it would look?” and she snipped off a big bit. “Here goes,” she cried; “after all ’tis best to follow the fashions, whatever they are,” and she went on cutting till, when her husband came in, he found her with her hair all cut off beside her.

===Chinese===
可丈夫又走开后，妻子拿着剪刀来到镜子前。“照当家的说，铰了倒省事得很，”她自语道，“再说，能让周围的女人们瞧瞧，我比她们先赶上了时样，那才叫好呢。不知铰出来什么样？”她咔嚓铰下一大绺。“索性铰了！”她喊道，“管它什么样式，赶时样总归不会错。”她一路铰下去，待丈夫进屋，只见她坐在一地青丝旁边，头发已铰得精光。

===Original===
“There, husband,” cried she, “do I look like the smart ladies in town?”

===Chinese===
“瞧，当家的，”她嚷道，“我像城里那些俊俏太太吗？”

===Original===
“Ay, that you do,” he answered, “only ten times prettier; but as for all that beautiful hair, you must just give it to me, for it is so beautiful I would not let it be lost for anything,” and he took up all the heap of fine gold hair and tied it together with a bright ribbon.

===Chinese===
“像，太像了，”他答道，“还要俊上十倍。至于这一头好头发，你可得交给我，它这么美，无论如何不能白白糟蹋了。”说着，他把那一堆金丝般的秀发拾起来，用一根鲜亮的缎带扎成一束。

===Original===
The wife looked at herself in the glass, and thought she really looked very nice with little curls all round her head, and though the ploughman grieved over it in his heart, yet he was glad he had got her hair, and thought, “Now at last that miserable little gnome will be content, and leave me alone.” So that evening, when his wife was gone to bed, he took the bunch of hair and laid it near the hole, and it disappeared, and he knew the gnome had it.

===Chinese===
妻子照了照镜子，觉得满头小卷儿的自己着实好看。农夫心里虽然惋惜，却也庆幸头发到了手，心想：“这下那可恶的小地精总该满足了，再不来纠缠我了。”于是当天晚上，妻子睡下之后，他拿起那束头发放在洞口，头发不见了，他知道地精收下了。

===Original===
So for a time all went on quietly with the ploughman, and he hoped he should not hear more of the gnome, but one evening, after his wife had gone to bed and he was in the kitchen smoking his pipe alone, he heard the hated voice shouting, “Stop! I am coming up,” and then he saw the little black thing like a black beetle coming through the hole, and all happened just as before.

===Chinese===
此后农夫家太平了一阵子，他盼着再别听见地精的消息。可一天晚上，妻子睡下后，他独自在厨房抽烟，又听见了那个可恨的声音喊道：“站住！我要上来了。”接着就见那不比黑甲虫大的小黑东西从洞里钻出来，一切照旧。

===Original===
“Well, what do you want now?” cried the ploughman when he saw the ugly little woman in front of him. “I have given you my wife’s hair, and surely you ought to be content.”

===Chinese===
“好啊，这回你还要什么？”农夫见那丑陋的小女人立在跟前，嚷道，“我妻子的头发都给了你，你总该知足了吧。”

===Original===
“Not at all,” said the gnome, “for I have tried on her hair, and I find it does not suit my complexion. I have never seen her myself, and I don’t think you any judge, but I heard you telling her that her face was prettier than any of the town ladies. In that case you have no right to keep it for yourself. I must have your wife’s face.”

===Chinese===
“才不呢，”地精说，“我戴了她那头发，发觉同我的脸色不相衬。我自己从没见过她，你也不是什么好眼力；可我听见你对她说，她的脸比城里哪位太太都俊。既然如此，你就没道理独自留着它。我要你妻子的脸。”

===Original===
“My wife’s face!” screamed the ploughman, “I think you must be mad. How can I give you my wife’s face? And what would you do with it?”

===Chinese===
“我妻子的脸！”农夫尖叫起来，“我看你准是疯了。我怎么把妻子的脸给你？你要它做什么？”

===Original===
“Wear it,” answered the gnome; “and all you have to do is to fetch your wife in here this day week, and tell her what I wish; and I will come up and scrape off as much of her face as I want.”

===Chinese===
“戴着呗，”地精答道，“你只消七天后的今天，把你妻子领到这儿来，把我的意思告诉她；我便上来，从她脸上刮下我要的那一些。”

===Original===
“Why, it would kill her,” cried the ploughman.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，那会要了她的命！”农夫嚷道。

===Original===
“Not at all,” said the gnome, “neither would it hurt her, for she would scarcely feel my little knife; the only thing is, that when I have done, her skin will be rather black and shrivelled like my own, but as mine has been good enough for me all these years, it will surely be good enough for a common human woman. Anyhow, now you know. I must have your wife’s complexion to wear with her hair, or else I go at once. And as it will be you who have broken the compact, I shall take all your wealth with me.” And repeating in a deep voice, “Remember, this day week at twelve o’clock,” the gnome grew small, and disappeared through the hole.

===Chinese===
“根本不会，”地精说，“也伤不着她，我那把小刀，她几乎觉不出疼。只有一桩：等我刮完，她的皮肤会变得又黑又皱，跟我这张一样。可我这张皮这些年自己用着也挺够用，给一个寻常的凡人妇道人家，保管也尽够了。总之，话就这样：我必须拿你妻子的脸皮，配她那头发；不然我立刻就走。而且是你先毁的约，我会把你的全部家财一并带走。”末了，她又用低沉的声音重了一句：“记住，七天后的今天，正午十二点。”说罢便缩小了身子，钻进洞里不见了。

===Original===
Next day the ploughman was very miserable, and whenever he looked at his wife felt inclined to burst into tears. The wife, not knowing what was amiss, tried to cheer him, and asked if he were ill. But he shook his head, and told her “no,” and had not the courage to tell her the truth. Thus things went on, the ploughman growing sadder and sadder every day, till the evening before that on which the gnome had told him he must bring his wife to meet her. The ploughman was scarce able to check his sobs before his wife, and at last she came into the kitchen, and there found him crying outright.

===Chinese===
第二天，农夫愁苦不堪，一看见妻子就想放声大哭。妻子不知出了什么事，只管宽慰他，问他是不是病了。他摇摇头说“没有”，却又鼓不起勇气说实话。日子就这样熬着，农夫一天比一天悲伤，直到地精要他领妻子来见她的前一天晚上。他在妻子跟前几乎抑制不住呜咽，最后她走进厨房，正撞见他嚎啕大哭。

===Original===
When she saw this, she kneeled down by him, and said, “Husband, you surely do not think me a good wife, for a good wife shares all her husband’s troubles. Tell me what troubles you. Two heads are better than one, and perhaps I can help you.”

===Chinese===
见这情形，她在他身边跪下，说：“当家的，你一定是拿我不当好妻子了，好妻子是同丈夫分担忧愁的。告诉我你愁什么。一人计短，二人计长——兴许我还能帮你一把呢。”

===Original===
Then the ploughman told her all about the hated gnome, and how he had found it in a field, and how he had promised to give it some of the best of everything, and now how it wanted her face.

===Chinese===
于是农夫把那可恨的地精一五一十讲了：怎样在地里发现她，怎样答应凡进家门的东西都给她最好的一份，如今她又怎样要她的脸。

===Original===
At first the wife would scarcely believe it, and then she cried, “But if ’tis such a little creature why not pick it up and strangle it, or let me put my foot on it, while it is no bigger than a black beetle?”

===Chinese===
妻子起初简直不敢相信，随后嚷道：“既然是这么个小东西，趁它不比黑甲虫大的时候，何不把它捉起来掐死，或者让我一脚踩死它？”

===Original===
“Nay, do not think of such a thing,” said her husband, “for it is ill to play tricks with fairy folk, and most likely she would kill us outright.”

===Chinese===
“快别打这主意，”丈夫说，“同仙灵（fairy folk）耍花招是要遭殃的，十有八九她会当场要了咱俩的命。”

===Original===
“But part with my face I never will,” cried the wife.

===Chinese===
“可这张脸我死也不给。”妻子嚷道。

===Original===
“Then we will let her go and take with her the house and all our wealth, and be contented to live in my old cottage again, and be quite poor folk,” said her husband.

===Chinese===
“那就由她走，房子和全部家财都让她带走，咱们回我那间旧屋去住，安安分分做穷人。”丈夫说。

===Original===
On hearing this the wife burst into tears, and wept more bitterly than her husband, for she would not stop at all. It was in vain for him to try to cheer her and tell her that poor folk could be quite as happy as rich ones. She declared she could never be happy poor. Then when he said if she would let her face go, he would love her just as much or more without it, she cried that she could never be happy with a dreadful shrivelled black skin like a monkey’s. All that night she cried, and when morning came her skin was all red, and her eyes could scarce be seen, so swelled were their lids, but still she cried on all day, and her husband said nothing to comfort her, because he did not know what to say. By the time it grew dark, her face was so swelled and sore that she could not bear to touch it, and she had cried herself almost blind, but still the tears were rolling down. When the time came for the clock to strike twelve, her husband took her hand and led her to the kitchen, and there she sat with her face in her hands sobbing. Just as the clock struck, they heard the voice like thunder shouting, “Stop! I am coming up,” and the wife peeped between her fingers and saw the little thing no bigger than a black beetle come through the hole and then grow, and grow, and grow, till it was like an ugly little black woman near a foot high. And when she saw how hideous it was she thought, “Never, never will I consent to have a skin like that—not for millions of pounds.”

===Chinese===
一听这话，妻子放声大哭，哭得比丈夫还伤心，一发不可收拾。他百般劝解，说穷人也能活得同富人一样快活，全是白费口舌。她一口咬定，受穷她这辈子快活不起来。后来他说，她若肯把脸让出去，没了那张脸他也照样疼她，只会更疼；她哭喊着说，顶着一副猴子似的又皱又黑的皮，她一辈子也别想快活。她哭了一整夜，天亮时满脸通红，眼睛肿得只剩一条缝，还是哭个不停；一整天丈夫一句话也没劝，因为他不知说什么好。挨到天黑，她的脸肿得碰都碰不得，眼泪几乎哭干了，泪水还是不住往下滚。钟快敲十二点时，丈夫牵起她的手，领她到厨房，她双手捂脸坐着抽泣。钟声敲响的一刻，两人听见一个雷霆般的声音喊道：“站住！我要上来了。”妻子从指缝里偷看，只见那不比黑甲虫大的小东西钻出洞来，随即越长、越长、越长，长成一个将近一尺高的丑陋小黑女人。她一见那东西如此骇人，心想：“叫我换那样一张皮，我死也不肯——给百万千万镑也不肯。”

===Original===
The gnome did not speak to her, but said to the ploughman, “So you have brought your wife. That is a good thing, if you wish me to remain with you. So now tell her to take down her hands and let me see this face you make such a fuss about. I have my knife all ready.”

===Chinese===
地精不同她说话，只对农夫说：“你把妻子带来了，很好，这样我才肯留下。叫她放下手，让我瞧瞧这张让你夸个不停的脸。我的刀已经备好了。”

===Original===
And the ploughman saw that she had in her hand a tiny knife, which did not look as if it could hurt anyone.

===Chinese===
农夫看见她手里握着一把小小的刀，瞧那样子，简直伤不了人。

===Original===
“Wife, wife,” groaned the ploughman, “what shall we do?”

===Chinese===
“娘子，娘子，”农夫呻吟道，“咱们可怎么办哪？”

===Original===
Then the wife looked up out of her swollen eyes, and was just going to speak, when the gnome gave a shriek. “What?” she cried, “that face! Do you mean to say that is what you think so pretty, and that I am going to change my beautiful, dry, black skin for that swollen red mass? No, indeed. You must be mad. It is a good thing that I saw it in time. I shall leave the house at once.”

===Chinese===
妻子从红肿的眼睛里抬起头来，正要开口，地精忽然尖叫一声。“什么？”她喊道，“那张脸！你是说，这就是你们觉得那么俊的脸，要我拿自己这张又干又美的黑皮，去换那团红肿的东西？不，绝不。你们准是疯了。幸亏我及时看见了。我马上就离开这屋子。”

===Original===
“Nay,” cried the ploughman, “but it is you who are breaking your compact this time.”

===Chinese===
“且慢，”农夫嚷道，“这一回可是你失了约！”

===Original===
But the gnome made no reply, but scuttled down through the hole as fast as it could, and the ploughman and his wife burst out laughing for joy. And that was the last they ever saw of it, and it must have gone right away, but they knew it had left some of its luck behind it, as they both lived happily for the rest of their days.

===Chinese===
可地精不答话，只顾飞快地钻进洞里去了，农夫和妻子喜得放声大笑。那是他们最后一次见着她，她准是一去不回了；可他们知道她留下了几分好运气，因为两口子此后一辈子都过得快快活活。

## The Pool and the Tree / 池塘与树

===Original===
Once there was a tree standing in the middle of a vast wilderness, and beneath the shade of its branches was a little pool, over which they bent. The pool looked up at the tree and the tree looked down at the pool, and the two loved each other better than anything else on earth. And neither of them thought of anything else but each other, or cared who came and went in the world around them.

“But for you and the shade you give me I should have been dried up by the sun long ago,” said the pool.

“And if it were not for you and your shining face, I should never have seen myself, or have known what my boughs and blossoms were like,” answered the tree.

===Chinese===
从前，在一片广袤的荒野中央，立着一棵树，枝条的浓荫下覆着一泓小小的池塘，枝条向水面俯身弯垂。池塘仰望着树，树俯视着池塘，它们彼此相爱，胜过世上的一切。它们心里除了对方再无别念，也不去理会周遭的世界里谁来了、谁又走了。

“若不是有你，有你给我遮荫，我早叫太阳晒干了。”池塘说。

“若不是有你，有你这张莹亮的脸，我永远照不见自己的模样，也不会知道我的枝桠和花朵生得如何。”树回答道。

===Original===
Every year when the leaves and flowers had died away from the branches of the tree, and the cold winter came, the little pool froze over and remained hard and silent till the spring; but directly the sun’s rays thawed it, it again sparkled and danced as the wind blew upon it, and it began to watch its beloved friend, to see the buds and leaves reappear, and together they counted the leaves and blossoms as they came forth.

One day there rode over the moorland a couple of travellers in search of rare plants and flowers. At first they did not look at the tree, but as they were hot and tired they got off their horses, and sat under the shade of the boughs, and talked of what they had been doing. “We have not found much,” said one gloomily; “it seemed scarcely worth while to come so far for so little.”

“One may hunt for many years before one finds anything very rare,” answered the elder traveller. “Well, we have not done, and who knows but what we may yet have some luck?” As he spoke he picked up one of the fallen leaves of the tree which lay beside him, and at once he sprang to his feet, and pulled down one of the branches to examine it. Then he called to his comrade to get up, and he also closely examined the leaves and blossoms, and they talked together eagerly, and at length declared that this was the best thing they had found in all their travels. But neither the pool nor the tree heeded them, for the pool lay looking lovingly up to the tree, and the tree gazed down at the clear water of the pool, and they wanted nothing more, and by and by the travellers mounted their horses and rode away.

===Chinese===
每年，当树叶和花朵从枝头凋尽、寒冬来临时，小池塘便封冻起来，坚硬而缄默地挨到春天；可太阳的光线一将它消融，它便又波光潋滟、随风起舞，开始守望它亲爱的朋友，看芽苞和绿叶重新萌出，它们又一同数点着陆续绽开的叶子和花朵。

一天，荒原上来了两个寻访奇花异草的旅人。起初他们并没有留意这棵树，但走得又热又累，便下了马，坐在枝条的荫凉里，说起各自的见闻。“我们没找到什么，”其中一个闷闷不乐地说，“跑这么远只得了这么点东西，似乎不值当。”

“要寻到真正稀罕的东西，得先寻上许多年呢。”年长的旅人答道，“好了，我们还没有完，谁知道运气会不会就来了呢？”说话间，他随手拾起身旁一片树上落下的叶子，顿时一跃而起，拉低一根枝条细看。他又唤同伴起身，那人也细细察看了这些叶子和花朵，两人热切地议论了一番，最后宣称这是他们此行所觅得的最好的东西。可是池塘和树都没有理会他们——池塘正含情仰望着的，是树；树正凝眸俯视着的，是池塘清澈的水。它们再无所求，不多时，旅人们便上马离去。

===Original===
The summer passed and the cold winds of autumn blew.

“Soon your leaves will drop and you will fall asleep for the winter, and we must bid each other goodbye,” said the pool.

“And you too when the frost comes will be numbed to ice,” answered the tree; “but never mind, the spring will follow, and the sun will wake us both.”

===Chinese===
夏天过去了，秋风带着寒意吹了起来。

“不久你的叶子就要落尽，你就要睡去度过冬天，我们也该彼此道别了。”池塘说。

“等到霜降下来，你也会冻僵成冰。”树答道，“不过别怕，春天随后就来，太阳会把我们俩一起唤醒。”

===Original===
But long before the winter had set in, ere yet the last leaf had fallen, there came across the prairie a number of men riding on horses and mules, bringing with them a long wagon. They rode straight to the tree, and foremost among them were the two travellers who had been there before.

“Why do they come? What do they want?” cried the pool uneasily; but the tree feared nothing. The men had spades and pickaxes, and began to dig a deep ditch all round the tree’s roots, and then they dug beneath them, and at last both the pool and the tree saw that they were going to dig it up.

“What are you doing? Why are you trying to wrench up my roots and to move me?” cried the tree; “don’t you know that I shall die if you drag me from my pool which has fed and loved me all my life?” And the pool said, “Oh, what can they want? Why do they take you? The sun will come and dry me up without your shade, and I never, never shall see you again.” But the men heard nothing, and continued to dig at the root of the tree till they had loosened all the earth round it, and then they lifted it and wrapped big cloths round it and put it on their wagon and drove away with it.

===Chinese===
然而冬天还远未到来，最后一片叶子也尚未落尽，荒野上便驰来一群骑马骑骡的人，还带着一辆长长的马车。他们径直朝树而来，走在最前面的，正是先前到过的那两个旅人。

“他们来做什么？他们想要什么？”池塘不安地喊道；树却什么都不怕。那些人带着铁锹和镐头，围着树根挖起一道深沟，继而又往底下掏去，到后来，池塘和树都明白过来：他们是要把它掘走。

“你们在做什么？为什么要把我的根拔起、要把我搬走？”树喊道，“你们不知道吗？若是把我从池塘身边拖走——它滋养了我一生，也爱了我一生——我是会死的。”池塘则说：“唉，他们能想要什么呢？为什么要把你带走？没有你的荫凉，太阳一来就会把我晒干，我再也、再也见不到你了。”可是那些人什么也没听见，只顾在树根旁挖个不停，直到四周的泥土尽数松动，才把它抬起来，用大幅的布裹住，放上车运走了。

===Original===
Then for the first time the pool looked straight up at the sky without seeing the delicate tracery made by the leaves and twigs against the blue, and it called out to all things near it: “My tree, my tree, where have they taken my tree? When the hot sun comes it will dry me up, if it shines down on me without the shade of my tree.” And so loudly it mourned and lamented that the birds flying past heard it, and at last a swallow paused on the wing, and hovering near its surface, asked why it grieved so bitterly. “They have taken my tree,” cried the pool, “and I don’t know where it is; I cannot move or look to right or left, so I shall never see it again.”

“Ask the moon,” said the swallow. “The moon sees everywhere, and she will tell you. I am flying away to warmer countries, for the winter will soon be here. Goodbye, poor pool.”

At night, when the moon rose, and the pool looked up and saw its beautiful white face, it remembered the swallow’s words, and called out to ask its aid.

===Chinese===
这时，池塘生平第一次直直地仰望天空，却再也看不见枝叶在蓝天上织出的玲珑图案；它向身边的一切呼喊：“我的树，我的树，他们把我的树带到哪里去了？等炎炎烈日来临，若无我的树遮荫，它会把我晒干的。”它哀哀恸哭，声气悲切，连飞过的鸟儿都听见了。终于有一只燕子敛翅停住，在水面上方盘旋，问它为何这样伤心。“他们把我的树带走了，”池塘喊道，“我不知道它在哪里；我不能动，也不能左顾右盼，所以我再也见不到它了。”

“去问月亮吧，”燕子说，“月亮什么都看得见，她会告诉你的。我要飞往温暖的地方去了，冬天很快就要来了。再见吧，可怜的池塘。”

夜里，月亮升起来，池塘仰头望见她那皎洁美丽的脸，想起了燕子的话，便开口求她相助。

===Original===
“Find me my tree,” it prayed; “you shone through its branches and know it well, and you can see all over the world; look for my tree, and tell me where they have taken it. Perhaps they have torn it in pieces or burnt it up.”

“Nay,” cried the moon, “they have done neither, for I saw it a few hours ago when I shone near it. They have taken it many miles away and it is planted in a big garden, but it has not taken root in the earth, and its foliage is fading. The men who took it prize it heartily, and strangers come from far and near to look at it, because they say it is so rare, and there are only one or two like it in the world.”

On hearing this the pool felt itself swell with pride that the tree should be so much admired; but then it cried in anguish, “And I shall never see it again, for I can never move from here.”

===Chinese===
“替我找回我的树吧，”它祈求道，“你曾照彻它的枝桠，最熟识它；你能望遍整个世界。去找找我的树，告诉我他们把它带到哪里去了。也许他们已把它撕碎，或是烧掉了。”

“不，”月亮喊道，“都没有。几个时辰前我还照在它近旁呢。他们把它带到好几英里之外，栽在一座大花园里，可它没有在泥土里生根，枝叶正日渐凋萎。带走它的人万分珍爱它，远近的陌生人纷纷赶来看它，因为它实在太稀罕，世上这样的树只有一两棵。”

听了这话，池塘觉得自己因骄傲而涨满了——树竟这样受人赞赏；可随即它又痛苦地喊道：“可我再也见不到它了，我永远离不开这里。”

===Original===
“That is nonsense,” cried a little cloud that was sailing near; “I was once in the earth like you. Tomorrow, if the sun shines brightly, he will draw you up into the sky, and you can sail along till you find your tree.”

“Is that true?” cried the pool, and all that night it rested in peace waiting for the sun to rise. Next day there were no clouds, and when the pool saw the sun shining it cried, “Draw me up into the sky, dear Sun, that I may be a little cloud and sail all the world over, till I can find my beloved tree.”

===Chinese===
“那是傻话，”一朵正在近旁飘行的小云朵喊道，“我从前也像你一样待在地上。明天，只要太阳明亮地照耀，他就会把你引上天去，你便可以一路飘游，直到找见你的树。”

“这是真的吗？”池塘喊道。那一夜它安安稳稳地歇着，静候太阳升起。第二天万里无云，池塘见太阳照耀，便喊道：“把我引上天去吧，亲爱的太阳，让我化作一朵小云朵，飘遍整个世界，直到找见我亲爱的树。”

===Original===
When the sun heard it, he threw down hundreds of tiny golden threads which dropped over the pool, and slowly and gradually it began to change and grow thinner and lighter, and to rise through the air, till at last it had quite left the earth, and where it had lain before, there was nothing but a dry hole, but the pool itself was transformed into a tiny cloud, and was sailing above in the blue sky in the sunshine. There were many other little clouds in the sky, but our little cloud kept apart from them all. It could see far and near over a great space of country, but nowhere could it espy the tree, and again it turned to the sun for help. “Can you see?” it cried. “You who see everywhere, where is my tree?”

“You can’t see it yet,” answered the sun, “for it is away on the other side of the world, but presently the wind will begin to blow and it will blow you till you find it.”

===Chinese===
太阳听见了，便抛下千百缕细小的金线，纷纷落在池塘身上。池塘缓缓地变了，越来越轻、越来越薄，袅袅升入空中，直到整个儿离开了地面。它原先躺着的地方，只剩一个干涸的坑洼；而池塘自己，已化作一朵小小的云，正在蓝天里沐着阳光徐徐飘行。天上还有许多别的小云朵，可我们这朵小云朵谁也不挨着。它居高望远，方圆百里的原野尽收眼底，却哪里也望不见那棵树，于是又向太阳求助。“你看得见吗？”它喊道，“你这无所不见的，我的树在哪里？”

“你还看不见它，”太阳答道，“它在世界的另一头呢。不过一会儿风就要刮起来，会一路把你吹到找见它为止。”

===Original===
Then the wind arose, and the cloud sailed along swiftly, looking everywhere as it went for the tree. It could have had a merry time if it had not longed so for its friend. Everywhere was the golden sunlight shining through the bright blue sky, and the other clouds tumbled and danced in the wind and laughed for joy.

“Why do you not come and dance with us?” they cried; “why do you sail on so rapidly?”

“I cannot stay, I am seeking a lost friend,” answered the cloud, and it scudded past them, leaving them to roll over and over, and tumble about, and change their shapes, and divide and separate, and play a thousand pranks.

===Chinese===
于是风起了，云朵疾疾飘行，一路四处寻找那棵树。若不是这样苦苦思念着朋友，它本可以过得快活惬意。处处是金色的阳光穿过明净的蓝天，别的云朵在风里翻滚、跳舞，笑得欢畅。

“你为什么不来和我们一同跳舞？”它们喊道，“你为什么飘得这样急？”

“我停不得，我在寻一位失散的朋友。”云朵答道，从它们身旁掠过，任凭它们翻来滚去、挤作一团，变换着身形，散了又聚，耍着千百样的把戏。

===Original===
For many hundred miles the wind blew the little cloud, then it said, “Now I am tired and shall take you no further, but soon the west wind will come and it will take you on; goodbye.” And at once the wind stopped blowing and dropped to rest on the earth; and the cloud stood still in the sky and looked all around.

“I shall never find it,” it sighed. “It will be dead before I come.”

Presently the sun went down and the moon rose, then the west wind began to blow gently and moved the cloud slowly along.

===Chinese===
风把这朵小云朵吹了数百英里，然后说：“我累了，不能再送你了。不过西风就快来，他会接着送你。再会。”话音一落，风便停息，落到地上歇息去了；云朵停在半空，四下张望。

“我再也找不到它了，”它叹道，“不等我到，它就要死了。”

不多时，太阳落了，月亮升起来，随后西风轻轻地吹起，缓缓推着云朵前行。

===Original===
“Which way should I go, where is it?” entreated the cloud.

“I know; I will take you straight to it,” said the west wind. “The north wind has told me. I blew by the tree today; it was drooping, but when I told it that you had risen to the sky and were seeking it, it revived and tried to lift its branches. They have planted it in a great garden, and there are railings round it and no one may touch it; and there is one gardener who has nothing to do but to attend to it, and people come from far and near to look at it because it is so rare, and they have only found one or two others like it, but it longs to be back in the desert, stooping over you and seeing its face in your water.”

===Chinese===
“我该往哪儿走？它在哪里？”云朵恳求道。

“我知道，我带你径直到它那儿去。”西风说，“是北风告诉我的。今天我从树旁吹过，它正蔫蔫地垂着；可我一告诉它你已升上天空、正在寻它，它便振作起来，竭力抬起枝条。他们把它栽在一座大花园里，四周围着栏杆，谁也不许碰它；还有一位园丁，什么别的事都不做，专司照料它。人们从远近各地赶来看它，因为它太稀罕了，世上只又寻得出一两棵同类的树。可它一心只想回到荒野去，俯身向着你的怀抱，在你的水里照见自己的脸。”

===Original===
“Make haste, then,” cried the cloud, “lest before I reach it I fall to pieces with joy at the thought of seeing it.”

“How foolish you are!” said the wind. “Why should you give yourself up for a tree? You might dance about in the sky for long yet, and then you might drop into the sea and mix with the waves and rise again with them to the sky, but if you fall about the tree you will go straight into the dark earth, and perhaps you will always remain there, for at the roots of the tree they have made a deep hole and the sun cannot draw you up through the earth under the branches.”

“Then that will be what I long for,” cried the cloud. “For then I can lie in the dark where no one may see me, but I shall be close to my tree, and I can touch its roots and feed them, and when the raindrops fall from its branches they will run down to me and tell me how they look.”

===Chinese===
“那就快些吧，”云朵喊道，“只怕还没到它跟前，我一想到就要见着它，欢喜得先碎掉了。”

“你多傻呀！”风说，“何必为一棵树把自己整个儿舍掉呢？你尽可以在天上再舞上许久，然后落进大海，同波浪融为一体，再随它们升回天上。可你若在树旁落下来，就会径直沉进黑暗的泥土里，也许就永远留在那儿了——他们在树根四周挖了一个深洞，太阳没法穿透枝下的泥土再把你引上天去。”

“那正是我所盼望的呀。”云朵喊道，“那样我就能躺在黑暗里，谁也看不见我，却紧贴着我亲爱的树；我能摸到它的根，滋养它们。等雨珠从它枝头落下，它们会顺着泥土流到我身边，告诉我它们看到了什么。”

===Original===
“You are foolish,” said the wind again; “but you shall have what you want.”

The wind blew the cloud low down near the earth till it found itself over a big garden, in which there were all sorts of trees and shrubs, and such soft green grass as the cloud had never seen before. And there in the middle of the grass, in a bed of earth to itself, with a railing round it so that no one could injure it, was the tree which the cloud had come so far to seek. Its leaves were falling off, its branches were drooping, and its buds dropped before they opened, and the poor tree looked as if it were dying.

“There is my tree, my tree!” called the cloud. “Blow me down, dear wind, so that I may fall upon it.”

===Chinese===
“你真傻。”风又说，“不过，你会如愿的。”

风把云朵吹得低低的，贴近地面，直到它来到一座大花园的上空。园中栽着各式各样的树木灌丛，还有云朵从未见过的那样柔软青翠的草地。草地中央，有一方专属的土床，四周围着栏杆，谁也伤不着它——正是云朵远道而来寻觅的那棵树。它的叶子纷纷凋落，枝条低垂，芽苞未及绽开便掉下了，可怜的树看上去就像要死了。

“我的树在那儿，我的树！”云朵唤道，“把我吹下去吧，亲爱的风，好让我落在它身上。”

===Original===
The wind blew the cloud lower and lower, till it almost touched the top branches of the tree. Then it broke and fell in a shower, and crept down through the earth to its roots, and when it felt its drops the tree lifted up its leaves and rejoiced, for it knew that the pool it had loved so had followed it.

“Have you come at last?” it cried. “Then we need never be parted again.”

===Chinese===
风把云朵越吹越低，低得几乎碰着树的顶枝。云朵终于碎裂，化作一阵骤雨落下，渗过泥土，淌到树根旁。树一觉出这些水滴，便扬起叶子，欣喜万分——它知道，它深爱着的池塘，一路追随它来了。

“你终于来了？”它喊道，“那我们再也不必分离了。”

===Original===
In the morning when the gardeners came they found the tree looking quite fresh and well, and its leaves quite green and crisp. “The cool wind last night revived it,” they said, “and it looks as if it had rained too in the night, for round here the earth is quite damp.” But they did not know that under the earth at the tree’s roots lay the pool, and that that was what had saved the tree.

And there it lies to this day, hidden away in the darkness where no one can see it, but the tree feels it with its roots, and blooms in splendour, and people come from far and near to admire it.

===Chinese===
清晨，园丁们来了，见树容光焕发、精神抖擞，叶子青翠饱满。“昨夜凉风使它缓过来了，”他们说，“夜里像是还下过雨，这一带的泥土都挺湿润的。”可他们不知道，树的根下、泥土深处，躺着的是池塘——救了这棵树的，正是它。

它至今仍躺在那里，藏在那无人得见的黑暗里；而树用根须感受着它，开得繁花似锦、光华璀璨，人们从远近各地赶来，赞叹不已。

## The Rain Maiden / 雨中少女

===Original===
Once upon a time there lived a shepherd and his wife, who lived in a very lonely little cottage far from town or village, near some mountains. It was a wild neighbourhood, and the wind blew across the mountains fiercely, and the rain often fell so heavily that it seemed as if the cottage would be washed away. One evening when the shepherd was out, there came on a great storm of rain which beat against the doors and the windows violently. As the shepherd’s wife sat listening to it by the fire, it seemed to her as if it sounded louder than she had ever heard it before, and the raindrops sounded like the knock of a hand that was knocking to gain admittance. It went on for a little time, till the shepherd’s wife could bear to listen to it no longer, and she rose and went to the door to open it, though she knew that she would let the wind and rain into the room. As she opened the door a gust of rain was blown in her face, and then she saw that in the doorway stood a woman who had been knocking. She was a tall woman wrapped in a grey cloak with long hair falling down her back. “Thank you,” she said. And though her voice was very low, the shepherd’s wife could hear it plainly through all the storm. “Thank you for opening the door to me. Many would have let me stand outside. Now may I come into your cottage and rest?”

===Chinese===
从前，有一个牧羊人和他的妻子，住在远离城镇的一座孤零零的小茅屋里，屋旁不远处便是群山。那是一处荒僻的地方，风从山间猛烈地刮过，雨也常常下得那么急，仿佛要把小屋整个冲走似的。一天傍晚，牧羊人出门去了，忽然来了一场大暴雨，雨点狠狠地砸在门窗上。牧羊人之妻坐在火炉边听着，只觉得这雨声比她平生听过的任何时候都响，雨点敲打着，就像一只手在叩门，求人放它进去。这声音响了有一阵子，牧羊人之妻实在听不下去了，便起身去开门，虽然她知道一开门，风和雨都会灌进屋里来。门一开，一阵急雨扑在她脸上，随后她看见门口站着一个方才一直在叩门的女子。那是个高高的女子，裹着一件灰斗篷，长长的头发披在背上。“谢谢您，”她说。她的声音虽然极低，牧羊人之妻却穿过满耳的风雨听得清清楚楚。“谢谢您为我开门。多少人都会任我站在门外的。现在，我能进您的茅屋歇一歇吗？”

===Original===
“How wet you must be!” cried the shepherd’s wife; “come in and rest, and let me give you food. Have you come from far?”

“No, I come from quite near,” said the woman, and she came into the cottage as she spoke, and sat down in a chair near the door. “And I want no food, only a glass of water. I must go on directly, but I have not far to go, and I shall be no wetter than I am now.”

===Chinese===
“您一定浑身都湿透了吧！”牧羊人之妻喊道，“快进来歇歇，让我给您拿点吃的。您是从很远的地方来的吗？”

“不，我住得很近。”那女子说着，走进屋来，在门边一把椅子上坐下，“我什么也不要，只要一杯清水。我马上还得赶路，好在路不远，我也不会再比现在更湿了。”

===Original===
The shepherd’s wife stared in surprise, for she saw that apparently the woman’s clothes were not wet at all. And what was stranger still, though she had thought she was only clad in a dull grey cloak, now she saw that she was covered with jewellery﻿—clear stones, like diamonds with many flashing colours; and she also saw that all her clothes were of the finest. She gave her a glass of water, and begged that as well she might give her other food, but the woman shook her head, and said no, water was all she needed. When she had drunk the water she gave back the glass to the shepherd’s wife, and said, “And so this is your home. Have you all that you want in life? Are you happy?”

===Chinese===
牧羊人之妻惊讶地望着她，因为她看见那女子的衣裳分明一点儿也没湿。更奇怪的是，方才她只当女子裹着一件暗灰的斗篷，如今却看见她周身缀满了珠宝——一颗颗清水般的宝石，像钻石一样闪着七彩的光；她还看出女子的衣裳件件都是最上等的料子。她递给她一杯清水，又恳求再给她弄些吃的，可女子摇了摇头，说不用了，清水便是她所要的一切。她喝完水，把杯子还给牧羊人之妻，说道：“原来这就是您的家。您这一生要的都齐备了吗？您过得幸福吗？”

===Original===
“Ay, we are happy enough,” said the shepherd’s wife, “save indeed for one thing. Ten years ago my little baby girl died, and I have no other children. I long for one sorely, that I might take care of it and make it happy, while it is little, and then in turn, when I am old, it would love and care for me.”

===Chinese===
“唉，我们够幸福的了，”牧羊人之妻说，“只是有一桩心事。十年前，我的小女婴夭折了，我再没有别的孩子。我多想要一个啊，好在它还小的时候照看它，让它快活；等我老了，它也会反过来爱我、照看我。”

===Original===
“And if you had a little child,” said the woman, rising up and standing before the shepherd’s wife, “you think you would really love it better than anything in the world. Many women say that, but few do it. Before long a little child will be born to you, and as long as you love it better than anything in the world it will remain with you, but when you love anything else better than your little daughter and her happiness, it will go from you; so remember my words. Goodbye,” and the woman walked to the door and went quietly out into the rain, and the shepherd’s wife saw her disappearing, and the rain pelting around her, but her clothes were not blown about, neither did the rain seem to wet her.

===Chinese===
“倘若您真的有了个小孩子，”那女子说着站起身来，立在牧羊人之妻面前，“您以为您会爱它胜过世上的一切。许多女人都这么说，可做到的没有几个。不久，您就会生下一个小女儿；只要您爱她胜过世上的一切，她便会留在您身边；可当您爱别的什么胜过爱您的小女儿和她的幸福时，她就会离开您。记住我的话。再见了。”那女子走到门口，静静地走进雨里去了。牧羊人之妻望着她的身影远去，雨点在她四周噼噼啪啪地砸落，可她的衣裳既没有被风吹乱，雨水也仿佛湿不了她分毫。

===Original===
A year passed away, and the shepherd’s wife had a tiny daughter, a lovely little baby with the bluest eyes and the softest skin; the evening she was born the wind howled and the rain fell as fiercely as on the night when the grey woman had come into the shepherd’s cottage. The shepherd and his wife both loved their little daughter very dearly, as well they might, as no fairer child was ever seen. But as she grew older, some things about her frightened her mother, and she had some ways of which she could not cure her.

===Chinese===
一年过去了，牧羊人之妻生下一个小小的女儿，是个可爱的小娃娃，生着一双最蓝最蓝的眼睛，皮肤细嫩柔软。她出生的那个傍晚，风呼啸，雨倾盆，正像灰衣女子（grey woman）来到牧羊人茅屋的那一夜一样。牧羊人夫妇都极疼爱他们的小女儿，这也难怪，谁也没见过比她更美的孩子。可是随着她渐渐长大，她身上有些地方叫母亲害怕，有些习性她怎么也纠正不过来。

===Original===
She would never go near a fire, however cold she was, neither did she love the sunshine, but always ran from it and crept into the shade; but when she heard the rain pattering against the windowpanes she would cry, “Listen, mother, listen to my brothers and sisters dancing,” and then she would begin to dance too in the cottage, her little feet pattering upon the boards; or, if she possibly could, she would run out on to the moor and dance, with the rain falling upon her, and her mother had much ado to get her to come back into the cottage, yet she never seemed to get very wet, nor did she catch cold.

===Chinese===
无论多冷，她从不肯靠近火炉；她也不爱阳光，总是躲着日头，缩到荫凉里去；可一听见雨点敲打窗棂，她便喊起来：“听呀，妈妈，听我的兄弟姐妹们跳舞啦！”接着她也在屋里跳起舞来，小脚踏得地板嗒嗒作响；要不就一溜烟跑到荒原上去，让雨浇着，在雨里跳舞，母亲要费好大的劲才能把她拽回屋里来。可她好像从不会淋得多湿，也从不着凉。

===Original===
A river ran near the cottage, and by it she would go and sit for hours dabbling her feet in the water, and singing sweet little songs to herself.

===Chinese===
茅屋附近流着一条河，她常到河边去，一坐就是几个钟头，把脚浸在水里拍打嬉戏，独自唱着甜甜的小曲儿。

===Original===
Still, in all other ways she was a good, affectionate girl, and did all that her mother told her, and seemed to love both her parents tenderly, and the shepherd’s wife would say to herself, “My only trouble is that when she is grown up, she will want to marry, and leave me, and I shall have to do without her.” Time passed, and the old shepherd died, but his wife and daughter still lived on in the little cottage, and the daughter grew to be a most beautiful young maiden. Her eyes were clear light-blue, like the colour of the far-off sea, but it was difficult to say what was the colour of her hair, save that it was very light, and hung in heavy masses over her brow and shoulders. Once or twice her mother felt sorely frightened about her; it was when spring showers were falling, and the young girl had gone into the little garden in front of the cottage to let the rain fall upon her head and face, as she loved to do, in spite of all her mother could say. Then she began to dance, as she always did when the rain fell, and as she danced the sun came out while the rain was yet falling. Her mother watched her from the cottage-window, but while she watched her it seemed to her as if her daughter was covered with jewels of every colour, clear and bright; they hung around her in chains, and made her look more like a king’s daughter than a shepherd’s girl. “Come in, child, come in,” called the shepherd’s wife, and when the young girl came in the cottage all traces of the jewels had gone, and when her mother upbraided her for going out to dance in the rain, she only answered, “It hurts no one, my mother, and it pleases me, why should you stop me?”

===Chinese===
不过在其他各方面，她都是个善良、有情有义的姑娘，母亲吩咐什么她都照做，对双亲也显得十分温柔亲爱。牧羊人之妻常自言自语：“我唯一的心事是，等她长大了，总要嫁人，要离开我，我就得没有她过日子了。”光阴流逝，老牧羊人去世了，妻子和女儿仍住在那座小茅屋里，女儿出落成了一位顶顶美丽的少女。她的眼睛是清澈的浅蓝色，像遥远海面的颜色；她的头发很难说清是什么颜色，只知道颜色极淡，沉甸甸地披在额前和肩头。有一两回，母亲为她担惊受怕：那是春雨纷纷的时候，少女不顾母亲怎样劝阻，照旧跑到茅屋前的小花园里，让雨落在她的头上脸上——她最爱的就是这个。接着她便跳起舞来，每逢下雨她总要跳舞的；她跳着跳着，雨还没停，太阳却出来了。母亲从茅屋的窗口望着她，可望着望着，只觉得女儿浑身缀满了五颜六色的珠宝，晶莹闪亮；珠宝一串串地悬在她周身，使她看上去不像牧羊人的女儿，倒像一位国王的女儿了。“进来，孩子，快进来。”牧羊人之妻喊道。少女走进屋来，珠宝的影子消失得干干净净。母亲责备她不该跑出去淋雨跳舞，她只答道：“这又不碍着谁，我的妈妈，我还欢喜得很，您为什么要拦我呢？”

===Original===
A little way from the cottage on the mountainside stood an old castle, where formerly the Kings of the land used to come and stay, but which now had not been used for very many years. One day, however, the shepherd’s wife saw great preparations were being made to beautify and adorn it, and she knew that the King and his son were coming to stay there again. Soon after they had arrived, the shepherd’s daughter went down to the river, as was her wont, and sat on the bank, dipping her feet in the ripples. Presently there came up a boat, and it was a grand young man dressed all in velvet and gold who leaned over the side to fish.

===Chinese===
离茅屋不远、半山腰上立着一座旧城堡（old castle），从前这个国家的历代国王常来小住，只是已经许多年不曾用了。可是有一天，牧羊人之妻看见人们在大大地修葺装点城堡，便知道老国王和他的儿子又要来住了。他们到了不久，牧羊人之女照着习惯下到河边，坐在岸上，把脚浸在涟漪里。不一会儿，驶来一条船，船边倚着一个华贵的年轻人，一身天鹅绒镶金的装束，正俯身钓鱼。

===Original===
“Who are you, and what are you doing here?” cried the shepherd’s daughter, for she was afraid of no one.

“I am the King’s son,” said he, “and I am coming here to fish. Who are you, and where do you come from, for I have never seen such a beautiful maiden in my life?” and he looked at her and could scarce speak, so beautiful did she seem to him.

“It is cruel to take the fishes out of the water,” cried the shepherd’s daughter, “leave them alone, and come and dance on the bank with me,” and she went under the shade of a large tree, and began to dance, and the King’s son watched her, and again he thought so beautiful a maid there had never been.

===Chinese===
“你是谁？在这儿做什么？”牧羊人之女喊道，因为她谁也不怕。

“我是国王的儿子，”他说，“我来这儿钓鱼。你是谁？从哪里来？我生平从没见过这样美的少女。”他望着她，几乎说不出话来，她在他的眼里实在太美了。

“把鱼儿从水里钓走，太狠心了，”牧羊人之女喊道，“别钓了，上岸来跟我一块儿在岸边跳舞吧。”她走到一棵大树的荫凉下，跳起舞来。国王的儿子望着她，心想这样美的少女，古往今来再没有第二个。

===Original===
Day after day he came down to the river to fish, and day after day he left the line and tackle to sit and watch the shepherd’s daughter, and each time found her more enchanting. Once he tried to kiss her hand, but she sprang from him and left him sitting in his boat alone. At last a day came when the Prince said to his father, “My father, you want me to wed so that I may have an heir to the throne, but there is only one woman that can ever be my wife, and that is the daughter of the poor woman who lives in the little cottage out yonder.”

===Chinese===
他日复一日地到河边钓鱼，又日复一日地撂下钓竿钓具，坐着看那牧羊人之女，每看一回，便觉得她更迷人一分。有一回他想吻她的手，她却一跃躲开，丢下他一个人坐在船里。终于有一天，王子对他的父亲说：“父王，您要我成婚，好为王位留下继承人；可天下只有一个女子配做我的妻子，就是住在那边小茅屋里的穷苦女人的女儿。”

===Original===
At first the old King was very wroth, but he loved his son well, and knew that nothing would shake him from his word, so he told him that if he would bring home his bride, he too would rejoice and love her as his daughter even though she be a beggar-maid. Then the young Prince rode down to the cottage, and went in and told the shepherd’s wife how he had seen her daughter, and loved her and wished to make her his wife, so that she would be Queen of the country.

===Chinese===
起初老国王大为恼怒，但他深爱儿子，也知道什么都不能动摇他的决心，便对他说，只要他把新娘娶回家来，哪怕她是个乞丐姑娘，自己也一样欢欢喜喜地疼她，拿她当女儿看待。于是年轻的王子骑马来到茅屋，进去对牧羊人之妻说，他怎样见到了她的女儿，怎样爱上了她，想要娶她为妻，让她做这个国家的王后。

===Original===
The shepherd’s wife went nearly wild with joy. “To think that my daughter should be the Queen,” she said to herself, and when her daughter came into the cottage she did not know how to contain herself, but folded her in her arms and kissed her, crying and declaring that never was woman so blessed.

“Why, what has happened, my mother? and what has pleased you so?” said her daughter, while still the shepherd’s wife rejoiced and wept for joy.

“It is the King’s son, my girl, the King’s own son, and he has just been here, and he loves you because you are so beautiful, and he will marry you and make you Queen of all the land. Was there ever such luck for a poor woman?”

===Chinese===
牧羊人之妻欢喜得几乎发了狂。“想想看，我的女儿要做王后了！”她自言自语道。女儿一进屋，她不知怎样才好，一把将她搂进怀里亲吻，哭着说自己是有史以来最有福气的女人。

“哎呀，出什么事了，我的妈妈？什么让您这么欢喜？”女儿问，牧羊人之妻还在那儿喜极而泣。

“是国王的儿子，我的孩子，是国王的亲儿子！他方才到这儿来过，他爱你，因为你长得这样美，他要娶你，要让你做全国的王后。一个穷苦女人哪有过这样的福气？”

===Original===
But the daughter only said, “But I don’t want to marry the King’s son, mother, or anyone. I will never be the wife of any man; I will stay with you and nurse you when you are old and sick, for I can live in no house but this cottage, and have no friend but my mother.”

===Chinese===
可女儿只说：“妈妈，我不想嫁给国王的儿子，也不嫁给任何人。我永远不做任何男人的妻子；我要留在您身边，等您老了病了，好服侍您，因为我除了这座茅屋，哪里也住不惯；除了妈妈，我一个亲人也没有。”

===Original===
On hearing this the shepherd’s wife became very angry, and told her daughter that she must be mad, and that she must wait for a day or two, and she would be only too thankful for the love of the King’s son, and for the honour he was going to do her in making her his Queen. But still the daughter shook her head, and said quite quietly, “I will never be the wife of the King’s son.” The shepherd’s wife did not dare tell the King’s son what her daughter had said, but told him that he had better speak to her himself if he wished to make her his wife. Then when he was again sitting in the boat on the river, and the maiden on the bank, the King’s son told her how much he loved her, and that he would share with her all that he had in this world. But the shepherd’s daughter only shook her head and said, “I will never live at the palace, and I will never be a Queen.”

===Chinese===
牧羊人之妻听了这话非常生气，说女儿准是疯了，说等过上一两天，她就会感激国王的儿子的爱情，感激他要立她为王后的荣耀了。可女儿仍旧摇着头，安安静静地说：“我永远不做国王的儿子的妻子。”牧羊人之妻不敢把女儿的话告诉国王的儿子，只说他若想娶她，最好亲自对她讲。后来，当他又坐在河中的船上、少女坐在岸上时，国王的儿子对她说，他多么爱她，愿意把此生所有的一切都与她分享。可牧羊人之女只是摇头说：“我永远不住宫殿，我永远不做王后。”

===Original===
The old King had ordered great preparations to be made for the wedding, which was to take place immediately, and all sorts of fine clothes were ordered for the shepherd’s daughter, that she might appear properly as the wife of the Prince, but for the few days just before the wedding, the rain fell as it had never been known to have fallen; it beat through the roofs of the cottages, and the river swelled and overflowed its banks; everyone was frightened, save indeed the shepherd’s daughter, who went out into the wet and danced as was her wont, letting the torrents fall upon her head and shoulders.

===Chinese===
老国王已下令为婚礼大事操办，婚期就在眼前；又给牧羊人之女订做了各式各样华丽的衣裳，好让她体体面面地做王子的妻子。可就在婚礼前的几天里，下起了前所未见的倾盆大雨；雨水穿透了茅屋的屋顶，河水暴涨，漫过两岸；人人都吓坏了，唯独牧羊人之女例外，她照旧走到雨里跳起舞来，任由急雨浇在头上肩上。

===Original===
But the evening before the wedding-day she knelt beside her mother’s side. “Dear mother,” she said, “let me stop with you and nurse you when you are old. Do not send me away to the palace to live with the King’s son.”

Then the mother was very angry, and told her daughter that she was very ungrateful, and she ought to be thankful that such luck had come in her way, and who was she, the daughter of a poor shepherd, that she should object to marrying the King’s son?

===Chinese===
婚礼前一天的晚上，她跪在母亲身旁。“亲爱的妈妈，”她说，“让我留在您身边，等您老了，好服侍您。别把我送到宫殿里去，跟国王的儿子一起生活。”

母亲气极了，骂女儿不知好歹，说这样的福气落到她头上，她该感激才是；她一个穷牧羊人的女儿，有什么资格嫌弃国王的儿子的婚事？

===Original===
All night long the rain fell in torrents, and when next day the shepherd’s daughter was dressed in all her finery, it was through pools on the ground that she had to step into the grand carriage which the King had sent to fetch her, and while the marriage-service was being read, the priest’s voice could scarcely be heard for the pattering of the drops upon the roof, and when they went into the castle to the banquet, the water burst through the doors opened to receive them, so that the King and the wedding guests had hard ado to keep dry. It was a grand feast, and the King’s son sat at one end of the table, and his young wife was beside him dressed in white and gold. All the courtiers and all the fine guests declared that surely the world had never contained such a beautiful young woman as their future Queen. But just when the goblets were filled with wine, to drink to the health of the bride and bridegroom, there came a cry, “The floods! the floods!” and the servants ran into the hall, crying out that the waters were pouring in, and in one moment the rooms were filled with water, and no one thought of anything but to save themselves. When the hurricane had subsided, and the waters gone down, they looked around for the Prince’s wife, who was nowhere to be found. Everyone said that she had been swept away by the torrents, and that she had been drowned in all her youth and beauty; only the shepherd’s wife wept alone, and remembered the words of the woman who came to her on the night of the storm: “When you love aught on earth better than your daughter and her happiness, she will go from you.”

===Chinese===
大雨整整下了一夜。第二天，牧羊人之女穿戴着满身华丽的衣饰，一路蹚过地上的积水，才登上国王派来接她的华贵马车；行婚礼的时候，雨点噼噼啪啪地打在屋顶上，牧师的声音几乎听不见；等他们走进城堡赴宴，那为他们敞开的大门里竟涌进水来，国王和宾客们费了好大的劲才保住一身干爽。那是一场盛大的婚宴，国王的儿子坐在长桌一端，年轻的新娘穿着白底金花的衣裳坐在他身旁。满朝的侍臣和贵客都说，天底下从来没有过他们未来王后这样美的女子。可就在酒杯斟满、正要举杯祝新郎新娘康健的时候，忽然有人喊起来：“洪水！洪水！”仆人们奔进大厅，叫嚷着大水灌进来了；一转眼，各间屋子都漫满了水，人人只顾逃命。等暴风雨平息、大水退去，人们四处寻找王子的妻子，却哪里也找不到。人人都说她被激流卷走了，说她正当青春年少、花容月貌，就这样淹死了；只有牧羊人之妻独自垂泪，想起了那个暴风雨之夜来叩门的女子说的话：“当您爱世上别的什么胜过爱您的女儿和她的幸福时，她就会离开您。”

===Original===
The King’s son mourned his wife, and for long would not be comforted; but when many years had passed, he married a beautiful Princess, and with her lived very happily; only when the rain fell in torrents and beat against the windowpanes it would seem to him as if he heard the sound of dancing feet, and a voice that called out, “Come and dance with me, come and dance with me and my brothers and sisters, oh, King’s son, and feel our drops upon your face.”

===Chinese===
国王的儿子哀悼妻子，久久不能释怀；可是许多年过去后，他娶了一位美丽的公主，同她过得十分美满。只是每当大雨倾盆、敲打着窗棂的时候，他仿佛总会听见一阵跳舞的脚步声，还有一个声音在呼唤：“来同我跳舞吧，来同我和我的兄弟姐妹们跳舞吧，噢，国王的儿子，来感觉我们的雨点落在你脸上。”

## The Story of a Cat / 一只猫的故事

===Original===
Once there lived an old gentleman who was a very rich old gentleman, and able to buy nearly everything he wanted. He had earned all his wealth for himself by trading in a big city, and now he had grown so fond of money that he loved it better than anything else in the world, and thought of nothing except how he could save it up and make more. But he never seemed to have time to enjoy himself with all that he had earned, and he was very angry if he was asked to give money to others. He lived in a handsome house all alone, and he had a very good cook who cooked him a sumptuous dinner every day, but he rarely asked anyone to share it with him, though he loved eating and drinking, and always had the best of wine and food. His cook and his other servants knew that he was greedy and hard, and cared for nobody, and though they served him well because he paid them, they none of them loved him.

===Chinese===
从前有一位老绅士（old gentleman），是个非常有钱的老绅士，差不多想要什么就能买什么。他的全部家财都是自己在大城市里做买卖挣来的，如今他爱钱爱得入了迷，爱它胜过世上的一切，满脑子只想着怎么把钱攒起来、再多赚一些。可是他挣下这许多钱财，却仿佛从没有工夫享用；若有人求他施舍钱财，他便要发好大的脾气。他独自住在一所漂亮的宅子里，雇了一位手艺极好的厨娘，天天给他做丰盛的晚餐，却难得请谁来同他共享，尽管他贪吃贪喝，桌上永远是最好的酒菜。厨娘和别的仆人都知道他既贪婪又刻薄，心里没有别人；他们看在工钱的份上把他伺候得妥妥帖帖，却没有一个爱戴他。

===Original===
It was one Christmas, and the snow lay thick upon the ground, and the wind howled so fiercely that the old gentleman was very glad he was not obliged to go out into the street, but could sit in his comfortable armchair by the fire and keep warm.

===Chinese===
有一年圣诞节，地上的积雪厚厚的，风声呼啸得那么凄厉，老绅士暗自庆幸自己不必上街，可以坐在炉火边舒舒服服的扶手椅里取暖。

===Original===
“It really is terrible weather,” he said to himself, “terrible weather;” and he went to the window and looked out into the street, where all the pavements were inches deep in snow. “I am very glad that I need not go out at all, but can sit here and keep warm for today, that is the great thing, and I shall have some ado to keep out the cold even here with the fire.”

===Chinese===
“这天气真是可怕，”他自言自语道，“真是可怕。”他走到窗前，朝街上望去，人行道上的积雪已有好几寸厚。“我真高兴今天压根儿不用出门，可以坐在这儿暖暖和和的，这才是顶要紧的。就算守着炉火，待在这屋里也还得费些功夫才挡得住寒气呢。”

===Original===
He was leaving the window, when there came up in the street outside an old man, whose clothes hung in rags about him, and who looked half frozen. He was about the same age as the old gentleman inside the window, and the same height, and had grey, curly hair, like his, and if they had been dressed alike anyone would have taken them for two brothers.

===Chinese===
他正要离开窗口，街上走来一个老头儿，衣衫褴褛地挂在身上，看上去都快冻僵了。他的年纪同窗内的老绅士相仿，个头一般高，也同他一样长着灰色的鬈发；两人若是穿一样的衣裳，谁都会把他们当成一对兄弟。

===Original===
“Oh, really,” said the old gentleman irritably, “this is most annoying. The parish ought to take up these sort of people, and prevent their wandering about the streets and molesting honest folk,” for the poor old man had taken off his hat, and began to beg.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，真是的，”老绅士恼火地说，“讨厌透了。教区就该把这类人收容起来，别让他们在街上晃荡，搅扰正经人。”原来那可怜的老头儿已经摘下帽子，开始乞讨了。

===Original===
“It is Christmas Day,” he said, and though he did not speak very loud, the old gentleman could hear every word he said quite plainly through the window. “It is Christmas Day, and you will have your dinner here in your warm room. Of your charity give me a silver shilling that I may go into an eating-shop, and have a dinner too.”

===Chinese===
“今天是圣诞节，”他说。他的声音并不很响，可老绅士隔着窗子，把他的话字字听得清清楚楚。“今天是圣诞节，您将在暖暖和和的屋里用晚餐。发发善心，赏我一枚银先令（shilling）吧，好让我进一家小饭铺，也吃上一顿晚餐。”

===Original===
“A silver shilling!” cried the old gentleman, “I never heard of such a thing! Monstrous! Go away, I never give to beggars, and you must have done something very wicked to become so poor.”

===Chinese===
“一枚银先令！”老绅士嚷道，“我从没听说过这种事！岂有此理！走开，我从不施舍乞丐，你落到这般穷酸地步，必定是造了什么大孽。”

===Original===
But still the old man stood there, though the snow was falling on his shoulders, and on his bare head. “Then give me a copper,” he said; “just one penny, that today I may not starve.”

===Chinese===
可那老头儿仍旧站在那里，任雪花落在他肩头，落在他光着的头顶。“那就给我一枚铜子儿吧，”他说，“只要一便士（penny），叫我今天不至于挨饿。”

===Original===
“Certainly not,” cried the old gentleman; “I tell you I never give to beggars at all.” But the old man did not move.

===Chinese===
“断然不行，”老绅士喊道，“我跟你说过了，我从来不施舍乞丐。”可老头儿动也不动。

===Original===
“Then,” he said, “give me some of the broken victuals from your table, that I may creep into a doorway and eat a Christmas dinner there.”

===Chinese===
“那么，”他说，“就给我一点您桌上的残羹剩饭吧，我好躲进一处门洞，在那里也算吃上一顿圣诞晚餐。”

===Original===
“I will give you nothing,” cried the old gentleman, stamping his foot. “Go away. Go away at once, or I shall send for the policeman to take you away.”

===Chinese===
“我什么也不会给你，”老绅士跺着脚喊道，“走开！马上走开！不然我叫警察来把你抓走。”

===Original===
The old beggar-man put on his hat and turned quietly away, but what the old gentleman thought was very odd was, that instead of seeming distressed he was laughing merrily, and then he looked back at the window, and called out some words, but they were in a foreign tongue, and the old gentleman could not understand them. So he returned to his comfortable armchair by the fire, still murmuring angrily that beggars ought not to be allowed to be in the streets.

===Chinese===
老乞丐（beggar-man）戴上帽子，静静地转身走了。可有一件事老绅士觉得好生古怪：这老头儿非但没有愁苦之色，反而畅快地笑着，随后还回头望着窗子，大声喊了几句话——那是一种外国话，老绅士听不明白。于是他回到炉边舒适的扶手椅里，嘴里还气鼓鼓地咕哝着，说街上实在不该放任这些乞丐出没。

===Original===
Next morning the snow fell more thickly than ever, and the streets were almost impassable, but it did not trouble the old gentleman, for he knew he need not go out and get wet or cold. But in the morning when he came down to breakfast, to his great surprise there was a cat on the hearthrug in front of the fire, looking into it, and blinking lazily. Now the old gentleman had never had any animal in his house before, and he at once went to it and said “Shoo-shoo!” and tried to turn it out. But the cat did not move, and when the old gentleman looked at it nearer, he could not help admiring it very much. It was a very large cat, grey and black, and had extremely long soft hair, and a thick soft ruff round its neck. Moreover, it looked very well fed and cared for, and as if it had always lived in comfortable places. Somehow it seemed to the old gentleman to suit the room and the rug and the fire, and to make the whole place look more prosperous and cosy even than it had done before.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，雪下得比先前更紧，街上几乎没法通行，可这难不倒老绅士，因为他知道自己不必出门去挨冻受潮。可当他下楼来用早餐时，却大吃一惊：炉前的地毯上蹲着一只猫，正望着炉火，懒洋洋地眨着眼睛。老绅士家里从来养过任何牲畜，他立刻走过去，喝道：“嘘！嘘！”想把它轰出去。可那猫一动不动。老绅士凑近细看，又不由得在心里暗暗赞叹。那是只很大的猫，灰黑两色，毛又长又软，颈间围着一圈厚厚的软毛，如同领巾一般。而且它看上去被喂养照料得极好，仿佛一向住在安逸体面的地方。不知怎么，老绅士只觉得这猫同这屋子、这地毯、这炉火正相配衬，使整个宅子比先前更显得富足温馨。

===Original===
“A fine creature! a very handsome cat!” he said to himself; “I should really think that a reward would be offered for such an animal, as it has evidently been well looked after and fed, so it would be a pity to turn it away in a hurry.”

===Chinese===
“好家伙！真是只漂亮的猫！”他自言自语道，“这么一只分明被人精心喂养照料的猫，想来总会有人悬赏寻它吧，急匆匆把它赶走可就可惜了。”

===Original===
One thing struck him as very funny about the cat, and that was that though the ground was deep in snow and slush outside, the cat was quite dry, and its fur looked as if it had just been combed and brushed. The old gentleman called to his cook and asked if she knew how the cat had come in, but she declared she had not seen it before, and said she believed it must have come down the chimney as all the doors and windows had been shut and bolted. However, there it was, and when his own breakfast was finished the old gentleman gave it a large saucer of milk, which it lapped up not greedily or in a hurry, but as if it were quite used to good food and had had plenty of it always.

===Chinese===
有一件事他觉得好生奇怪：外面满地是深深的积雪和泥雪水，这猫却浑身干干的，那身毛就像刚刚梳刷过一样。老绅士唤来厨娘，问她可知道这猫是怎么进来的，厨娘一口咬定从前没见过它，还说门窗全都关得严严实实、上了闩，她琢磨这猫准是从烟囱里下来的。可不管怎么说，猫就在这儿。老绅士自己用过早餐，便给它一大碟牛奶。它舔得不慌不忙，也不贪馋，仿佛向来吃惯了好的，从来不曾短缺过。

===Original===
“It really is a very handsome animal, and most uncommon,” said the old gentleman, “I shall keep it awhile and look out for the reward;” but though he looked at all the notices in the street and in the newspapers, the old gentleman could see no notice about a reward being offered for a grey and black cat, so it stayed on with him from day to day.

===Chinese===
“这真是个漂亮家伙，极其少见，”老绅士说，“我且把它留下些时日，留意着悬赏的告示。”可他把街上的告示和报纸都看遍了，也没见哪处悬赏寻找一只灰黑色的猫，于是这猫便一天一天在他家住了下来。

===Original===
Every day the cat seemed to his master to grow handsomer and handsomer. The old gentleman never loved anything but himself, but he began to take a sort of interest in the strange cat, and to wonder what sort it was﻿—if it was a Persian or a Siamese, or some curious new sort of which he had never heard. He liked the sound of its lazy contented purring after its food, which seemed to speak of nothing but comfort and affluence. So the cat remained on till nearly a year had passed away.

===Chinese===
在主人眼里，这猫一天比一天俊俏。老绅士平生除了自己谁也不爱，如今却对这只怪猫生出几分兴趣，琢磨它究竟是哪个品种——是波斯猫（Persian），还是暹罗猫（Siamese），或是他闻所未闻的什么新奇品种。他爱听它饭后那懒洋洋、心满意足的呼噜声，那声音仿佛句句说的都是安逸与富足。就这样，这猫一直住了下去，不知不觉将近一年过去了。

===Original===
It was not very long before Christmas that an acquaintance of the old gentleman’s came to his rooms on business. He knew a great deal about all sorts of animals and loved them for their own sakes, but of course he had never talked to the old gentleman about them, because he knew he did not love anything. But when he saw the grey cat, he said at once﻿—

===Chinese===
圣诞节前不久，老绅士的一位熟人因事登门。此人对各种动物都极有见识，真心喜爱它们本身，不过他从不与老绅士谈论动物，因为他知道老绅士什么都不爱。可他一见这只灰猫，立刻便说——

===Original===
“Do you know that this is a very valuable creature, and I should think would be worth a great deal?”

===Chinese===
“您可知道这是只极名贵的家伙？依我看，它能值一大笔钱呢。”

===Original===
At these words the old gentleman’s heart beat high. Here, he thought, would be a piece of great luck if a stray cat could make him richer than he was before.

===Chinese===
这话听得老绅士心怦怦直跳。他想，一只野猫若能叫他比先前更有钱，那可真是天大的好运。

===Original===
“Why, who would want to buy it?” he said. “I don’t know anybody who would be so foolish as to give any money for a cat which is of no use in life except to catch mice, when you can so easily get one for nothing.”

===Chinese===
“嗬，谁肯买它？”他说，“猫这种东西，除了捉老鼠别无用处，白捡也容易得很，我可不知道谁会傻到肯为它掏钱。”

===Original===
“Ah, but many people are very fond of cats, and would give much for rare sorts like this. If you want to sell it, the right thing would be to send it to the Cat Show, and there you would most likely take a prize for it, and then someone would be sure to buy it, and, it may be, would give a great deal. I don’t know what kind it is, or where it comes from, for I have never seen one the least like it, but for that reason it is very sure to be valuable.”

===Chinese===
“啊，可爱猫的人多着呢，遇上这样的稀罕品种，肯出大价钱的尽有。您若想卖它，正路子就是送它去猫展（Cat Show），十有八九能得个奖，然后保管有人来买，说不定还肯出好大一笔钱。我不晓得它是什么品种，从哪里来，因为我从没见过有哪只猫跟它有一丝相像——可正因为这样，它必定非常值钱。”

===Original===
Upon this the old gentleman almost laughed with joy.

===Chinese===
老绅士听了，欢喜得几乎笑出声来。

===Original===
“Where is the Cat Show?” he asked; “and when is it to be held?”

===Chinese===
“猫展在哪儿？”他问，“什么时候举办？”

===Original===
“There will be a Cat Show in this city quite soon,” said his acquaintance; “and it will be a particularly good one, for the new Princess is quite crazy about cats, and she is coming to it, and it is said that she doesn’t mind what she gives for a cat if she sees one she likes.”

===Chinese===
“本城很快就要办一场猫展，”熟人说，“而且场面会特别好，因为新来的公主爱猫爱得入迷，她要亲自来看呢。据说只要让她瞧上一只中意的猫，出多大价钱她都不在乎。”

===Original===
So then he told the old gentleman how he should send his name and the cat’s name to the people who managed the show, and where it was to be held, and went away, leaving the old gentleman well pleased, but to himself he laughed and said, “I don’t think that old man thinks of anything on earth but making money. How pleased he was at the idea of selling that beautiful cat if he could get something for it!”

===Chinese===
接着他告诉老绅士，该怎样把他的姓名和猫的名字报给主办猫展的人，又告诉了他举办的地点，然后便告辞了，留下老绅士满心欢喜。那位熟人独自暗笑道：“我看这老头儿除了赚钱，世上什么都不想。一听说能把那只漂亮猫卖个好价钱，瞧把他乐的！”

===Original===
When he had gone, the grey puss came and rubbed itself about his master’s legs, and looked up in his face as though it had understood the conversation, and did not like the idea of being sent to the show. But the old gentleman was delighted, and sat by the fire and mused on what he was likely to get for the cat, and wondered if it would not take a prize.

===Chinese===
熟人走后，灰猫儿便走过来，在主人的腿边蹭来蹭去，仰起脸望着他，仿佛方才的话它全听懂了，很不乐意被送去参加猫展。可老绅士正得意呢，坐在炉边盘算着这猫能给自己换回多少钱，寻思它说不定还能得个奖。

===Original===
“I shall be sorry to have to send it away,” he said; “still, if I could get a good round sum of money it would be a real sin not to take it, so you will have to go, puss; and it really was extraordinary good luck for me that you ever came here.”

===Chinese===
“把你送走，我还会舍不得呢，”他说，“不过，要是真能换来满满一大笔钱，不拿才是真正的罪过。所以你只好去一趟了，猫儿。你能上我这儿来，实在是我的天大运气。”

===Original===
The days passed, and Christmas Day came, and again the snow fell, and the ground was white. The wind whistled and blew, and on Christmas morning the old gentleman stood and looked out of the window at the falling snow and rain, and the grey cat stood beside him, and rubbed itself against his hand. He rather liked stroking it, it was so soft and comfortable, and when he touched the long hair he always thought of how much money he should get for it.

===Chinese===
日子一天天过去，圣诞节到了，又下起雪来，大地一片白。风呼呼地刮着。圣诞日早晨，老绅士站在窗前，望着外面纷落的雨雪，灰猫站在他身旁，在他手上蹭着。他颇喜欢抚弄它，那毛那么柔软熨帖；可每回手碰到那身长毛，他想起的总是它能给自己换多少钱。

===Original===
This morning he saw no old beggar-man outside the window, and he said to himself: “I really think they manage better with the beggars than they used to, and are clearing them from the town.”

===Chinese===
这天早晨，他没在窗外看见老乞丐，便自言自语道：“我看如今对乞丐的管束倒是比往年像样了，正把他们从城里清出去呢。”

===Original===
But just as he was leaving the window he heard something scratching outside, and there crawled on to the windowsill another cat. It was a very different creature to the grey cat on the rug. It was a poor, thin, wretched-looking animal, with ribs sticking through its fur, and it mewed in the most pathetic manner, and beat itself against the pane. When it saw it, the handsome grey puss was very much excited, and ran to and fro, and purred loudly.

===Chinese===
可他正要离开窗口，忽听得外面有什么东西在抓挠，只见另一只猫爬上了窗台。它同地毯上的灰猫儿全然是两路货色：一个可怜巴巴、瘦骨嶙峋、一脸苦相，肋骨根根凸出毛外，叫声凄惨已极，一下一下往窗玻璃上扑。那俊俏的灰猫儿一见它，登时激动起来，跑来跑去，呼噜呼噜叫得响亮。

===Original===
“Oh you disgraceful-looking beast!” said the old gentleman angrily; “go away, this is not the place for an animal like you. There is nothing here for stray cats. And you look as if you had not eaten anything for months. How different to my puss here!” and he tapped against the window to drive it away. But still it would not go, and the old gentleman felt very indignant, for the sound of its mewing was terrible. So he opened the window, and though he did not like to touch the miserable animal, he took it up and hurled it away into the snow, and it trotted away, and in the deep snow he could not see the way it went.

===Chinese===
“哦，你这丢人现眼的东西！”老绅士怒道，“走开！这里可不是你这号畜生待的地方。这儿没有野猫的好处。瞧你这副样子，倒像几个月没吃过一口饭了，跟我这只猫儿差得多么远！”说着他敲敲窗子赶它走。可它偏不走，老绅士心头火起，那猫叫声实在凄厉难听。于是他打开窗子，虽说不愿碰那可怜的畜生，还是一把抓起它，狠狠掼进雪地里。它一路小跑着去了，雪太深，他望不见它跑去的路径。

===Original===
But that evening, after he had had his Christmas dinner, as he sat by the fire with the grey puss on the hearthrug beside him, he heard again the noise outside the window, and then he heard the stray cat crying and mewing to be let in, and again the grey and black cat became very much excited, and dashed about the room, and jumped at the window as if it wanted to open it.

===Chinese===
可那天晚上，他用过圣诞晚餐，坐在炉边，灰猫儿卧在身旁的炉前地毯上，忽又听得窗外响动，接着便听见那只野猫哭叫着、喵喵哀鸣着要进来。那灰黑色的猫儿又激动起来，满屋乱窜，朝窗户扑跳，仿佛想自己把窗子打开。

===Original===
“I shall really be quite glad when I have sold you at the Cat Show,” said the old gentleman, “if I am going to have all sorts of stray cats worrying here,” and for the second time he opened the window, and seized the trembling, half-starved creature, and this time he threw it with all his might as hard as he could throw. “And now there is an end of you, I hope,” he said as he heard it fall with a dull thud, and settled himself again in his armchair, and the grey puss returned to the hearthrug, but it did not purr or rub itself against its master.

===Chinese===
“早知道要有各色野猫上这儿来纠缠，等你一进猫展卖掉，我还真求之不得呢。”老绅士说着，第二次打开窗子，一把抓住那浑身发抖、饿得半死的畜生，这一回他用尽全身力气，掼得远远的。“这下你可完了，但愿如此。”他说。他听得那猫闷闷地一声跌落，便重新在扶手椅里坐定。灰猫儿回到炉前地毯上，却不呼噜了，也不再到主人身上来蹭。

===Original===
Next morning when he came down to breakfast, the old gentleman poured out a saucer of milk for his cat as usual. “You must be well fed if you are going to be shown at the show,” he said, “and I must not mind a little extra expense to make you look well. It will all be paid back, so this morning you shall have some fish as well as your milk.” Then he put the saucer of milk down by the cat, but it never touched it, but sat and looked at the fire with its tail curled round it.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨下楼用早餐时，老绅士照例给他的猫倒了一碟牛奶。“要去参展，就得喂得饱饱的，”他说，“为着把你打扮体面，多破费一点我也不能心疼。这些将来都会连本带利回来的，所以今早除了牛奶，再给你添点鱼。”他把牛奶碟放到猫跟前，可它碰也不碰，只是蹲坐着望炉火，尾巴拢拢地盘在身边。

===Original===
“Oh, well, if you have had so much already that you don’t want it, you can take it when you do,” so he went away to his work and left the saucer of milk by the fire. But when he came back in the evening there was the saucer of milk and the piece of fish, and the grey cat had not touched them. “This is rather odd,” said the old gentleman; “however, I suppose cook has been feeding you.”

===Chinese===
“哦，好吧，你要是先前吃得太多不想吃，想吃了再吃也不迟。”于是他自去忙他的营生，把那碟牛奶留在炉边。可傍晚回来，牛奶和鱼还原样摆着，灰猫一口也没动。“这就有点怪了，”老绅士说，“不过，我猜是厨娘私下喂过你了。”

===Original===
Next morning it was just the same. When he poured out the milk the cat wouldn’t lap it, but sat and looked at the fire. The old gentleman felt a little anxious, for he fancied that the animal’s fur did not look so bright as usual, and when in the evening and the next day and the next, it would not lap its milk, or even smell the nice pieces of fish he gave it, he was really uncomfortable. “The creature is getting ill,” he said, “and this is most provoking. What will be the use of my having kept it for a year, if now I cannot show it?” He scolded his cook for having given it unwholesome food, but the cook swore it had had nothing. Anyhow it was growing terribly thin, and all day long it sat in front of the fire with its tail hanging down, not curled up neatly round it, and its coat looked dull and began to come out in big tufts of hair.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨还是老样子。他倒上牛奶，猫不肯舔，只是蹲坐着望炉火。老绅士有点着慌了，他觉得这畜生的毛色瞧上去不及往日光亮。到了晚上、第二天、第三天，它还是不肯舔牛奶，连他给的好鱼闻也不闻，他心里当真不安起来。“这畜生病了，”他说，“真是气死人。我养了它整整一年，到头来若是不能参展，那不全白费了？”他骂厨娘准是给它吃了不干净的东西，厨娘赌咒发誓说它什么也没吃过。反正它是眼见着瘦下去，整日蹲在炉火前，尾巴耷拉着，不再整整齐齐盘在身边；一身毛暗淡无光，还开始一绺一绺大片脱落。

===Original===
“Now really I shall have to do something,” said the old gentleman, “it is enough to make anyone angry! No one would believe that this could be a prize cat. It looks almost as wretched as that stray beast that came to the window on Christmas Day.” So he went to a cat and dog doctor, who lived near, and asked him to come in and see a very beautiful cat which had nothing the matter with it, but which refused to eat its food. The cat’s doctor came and looked at the cat, and then looked very grave, and shook his head, and looked at it again.

===Chinese===
“这下子真得想个法子了，”老绅士说，“这叫谁能不冒火！谁还能相信这是只得奖的猫。它瞧上去简直同圣诞节那天爬上窗台的野东西一样可怜了。”于是他去请附近一位猫狗医生，求他进来看看一只非常漂亮的猫——什么毛病也没有，就是不肯吃东西。医生来了，看了看猫，脸色登时凝重起来，摇了摇头，又端详了一回。

===Original===
“I don’t know what sort of cat it is,” he said, “for I never saw any other like it, but it is a very handsome beast, and must be very valuable. Well, I will leave you some physic for it, and I hope you may be able to pull it round, but with these foreign cats you never know what ails them, and they are hard to cure.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道这是哪个品种的猫，”他说，“我从没见过第二只像它的。它是只漂亮畜生，必定十分名贵。好吧，我给它留下些药，但愿您能把它调理过来。只是这些外洋猫，您永远猜不透它们病在哪里，难治得很。”

===Original===
Now the day was close at hand when the cat should have been sent to the show, and the old gentleman was getting more and more uneasy, for the grey cat lay upon the rug all day and never moved, and its ribs could almost be seen through its side, so thin had it grown. And oddly enough the old gentleman, who had never cared for anyone or anything in his life except himself, began to feel very unhappy, not only because of not getting the money, but because he did not like to think of losing the cat itself. He sent for his friend who had first told him about the Cat Show, and asked his advice, but his friend could not tell him what to do with it.

===Chinese===
眼看送猫去参展的日子就要到了，老绅士越来越坐立不安：灰猫整日趴在地毯上不动弹，瘦得肋骨几乎要从身侧透出来。说来也怪，这位一辈子除了自己谁也不放在心上的老绅士，竟觉得十分愁闷——不光是因为到手的钱要飞了，还因为他一想到要失去这只猫本身，心里就难受。他派人请来最先告诉他猫展消息的那位朋友讨主意，可朋友也说不出该拿它怎么办。

===Original===
“Well, well,” he said, “this is a bad business, for I have told everyone that you are going to exhibit a most extraordinarily beautiful cat, and now this poor creature is really fit for nothing but the knacker’s yard. I think, maybe, some naturalist would give you a good price for its skin, as it is so very uncommon, and if I were you I should kill it at once, for if it dies a natural death its skin won’t be worth a brass farthing.” At these words the grey cat lifted its head, and looked straight into the old gentleman’s face, as if it could understand, and for the first time for many a long year, the old gentleman felt a feeling of pity in his heart, and was angry with his friend for his suggestion.

===Chinese===
“唉，唉，”朋友说，“这可糟了，我已经逢人便说您要展出一只能美得出奇的猫，如今这可怜的家伙真就只配送屠场了。我想，兴许有哪个博物学家肯出好价钱买它的皮，它这么罕见嘛。我要是您，就立刻宰了它，它若是自己病死，那张皮可就一文不值了。”听到这话，灰猫抬起头，直直地望进老绅士的脸，仿佛全听懂了。多年来头一遭，老绅士心里生出一种怜悯之情，倒为朋友这个主意生起气来。

===Original===
“I won’t have it killed,” he cried; “why, I declare, though it does seem absurd, I have lived with this creature for a year, and I feel as if it were my friend, and if it would only get well and sit up on the hearthrug, I shouldn’t mind about the money one bit!”

===Chinese===
“我不许宰它，”他喊道，“说真的，虽然听着荒唐，这畜生同我作伴一年了，我觉得它就像我的朋友。只要它能好起来，重新蹲上炉前的地毯，那点钱我一点儿也不放在心上了！”

===Original===
At this his friend was greatly astonished, and went away wondering, while the old gentleman sat by the fire and watched the cat lying panting on the rug.

===Chinese===
朋友听了大为惊愕，纳闷地走了。老绅士坐在炉边，望着那猫喘息着躺在地毯上。

===Original===
“Poor pussy, poor old pussy!” he said, “it is a pity that you can’t speak and tell me what you want. I am sure I would give it to you.” Just as he spoke there came a noise outside, and he heard a mewing, and looking through the window he saw the same thin ugly brown cat that had come there last Christmas, and it looked as thin and wretched as ever. When she heard the sound the grey cat stood up on her tottering feet and tried to walk to the window. This time the old gentleman did not drive it away, but looked at it, and almost felt sorry for it; it looked almost as thin and ill as his own grey puss.

===Chinese===
“可怜的猫儿，可怜的老猫儿！”他说，“可惜你不会说话，不能告诉我你要什么。只要你说出来，我准会给你。”话音未落，外面传来响动，他听见一声喵叫，往窗外一望，看见的正是去年圣诞节来过的那只又瘦又丑的褐猫，还是那样瘦骨伶仃、一副苦相。灰猫一听这声音，颤巍巍地站起身，挣扎着要往窗前走。这一回老绅士没有赶它，只是望着它，心里几乎生出怜意——它瞧上去几乎同他自己的灰猫儿一样瘦弱病态。

===Original===
“You are an ugly brute,” he said, “and I don’t want you always hanging about; still, maybe you would be none the worse for a little milk now, and it might make you look better.” So he opened the window a little, and then he shut it and then he opened it again, and this time the brown cat crawled into the room, and went straight to the hearthrug to the grey puss. There was a big saucer of milk on the hearthrug, and the brown cat began to lap it at once, and the old gentleman never stopped it.

===Chinese===
“你是个丑东西，”他说，“我也不愿你老在这儿转悠；不过，这会儿喝点牛奶对你许有好处，兴许能让你体面些。”于是他先把窗子开了一条缝，又关上，再打开；这一回，褐猫爬进屋来，径直走向炉前地毯上的灰猫儿。地毯上摆着一大碟牛奶，褐猫立刻舔了起来，老绅士也没有拦它。

===Original===
He thought as he watched it, that it grew fatter under his eyes as it drank, and when the saucer was empty he took a jug and gave it some more. “I really am an old fool,” he said; “that is a whole pennorth of milk.” No sooner had he poured out the fresh milk than the grey cat raised itself, and sitting down by the saucer began to lap it as well, as if it were quite well. The old gentleman stared with surprise. “Well, this is the queerest thing,” he said.
