So he took some fish and gave it to the strange cat, and then, when he offered some to his own puss, it ate it as if there was nothing the matter. “This is most remarkable,” said the old gentleman; “perhaps it was the company of a creature of its own sort that my cat needed, after all.” And the grey cat purred and began to rub itself against his legs.

===Chinese===
他看着它喝，只觉得它在自己眼皮底下喝得渐渐丰润起来。碟子空了，他便拎起奶罐又给它添了些。“我真是个老糊涂，”他说，“这可是整整一便士的牛奶啊。”新奶刚一倒上，灰猫便撑起身子，挨着碟子坐下，也舔了起来，仿佛全然无恙。老绅士看得目瞪口呆。“嗬，这可真是天下奇事。”他说。于是他取了些鱼给那只怪猫，再把鱼递到自家猫儿面前时，它也吃了，吃得像没病没灾一样。“这真是不可思议，”老绅士说，“或许我这猫缺的，到底还是个同类的伴儿。”灰猫呼噜呼噜叫着，又在他的腿边蹭了起来。

===Original===
So for the next few days the two cats lay together on the hearthrug, and though it was too late to send the grey cat to the show, the old gentleman never thought about it, so pleased was he that it had got well again.

===Chinese===
此后几天，两只猫一同卧在炉前地毯上。虽说送灰猫去参展已经来不及了，老绅士却压根儿不再想这件事——它重新好了起来，他就欢喜成这样。

===Original===
But seven nights after the stray cat had come in from outside, as the old gentleman lay asleep in bed at night, he felt something rub itself against his face, and heard his cat purring softly, as though it wanted to say “goodbye.” “Be quiet, puss, and lie still till the morning,” he said. But when he came down to have his breakfast in the morning, there sat the brown tabby, looking fat and comfortable by the fire, but the grey cat was not there, and though they looked for it everywhere, no one could find it, though all the windows and doors had been shut, so they could not think how it could have got away. The old gentleman was very unhappy about it, but he looked at the strange cat on his hearth and said, “it would be unkind now to send this poor thing away, so it may as well stay here.”

===Chinese===
可就在那只野猫进屋后的第七个夜里，老绅士夜里睡得正沉，觉得有什么东西在脸上蹭了蹭，听见他的猫轻轻地呼噜着，仿佛想说一声“再见”。“安静些，猫儿，乖乖躺到天亮吧。”他说。可早晨下楼用早餐时，炉火边坐着的却是那只褐色虎斑猫，吃得胖胖的、舒舒服服，灰猫却不见了。他们四处寻找，谁也没找着；门窗全都关得严严实实，谁也想不出它是怎么走的。老绅士为此闷闷不乐，可他望着炉边那只怪猫，说道：“这会儿再把这可怜的东西赶走，就太狠心了，它不如就留下吧。”

===Original===
When she heard him speaking of its being unkind, his old cook burst out laughing. “Perhaps,” she said, “ ’twas a fairy cat, as it could get away through bolts and locks, and nothing but a fairy could have taught my master to think of a thing being unkind or not. I only hope that now he’ll think of someone in this world besides himself and his money.” And sure enough from that time the old gentleman began to forget about his money, and to care for the people about him, and it was all the doing of the strange cat who had come from no one knew where, and gone away to no one knew where.

===Chinese===
老厨娘听见主人竟说起什么狠心不狠心，不由得放声大笑。“说不定啊，”她说，“那是只仙猫呢，不然怎么能穿门越锁地走了？也唯有仙子，才能教会我家主人懂得一件事狠心不狠心。我只盼着，从今往后他心里能装着这世上的人，别光装着他自己和他的钱。”果不其然，从那时起，老绅士渐渐把钱财淡忘了，开始体贴起身边的人来。而这一切，都是那只怪猫的功劳——它来时无人知晓从何处来，去时也无人知晓往何处去。

## The Windfairies / 风仙子

===Original===
There was once a windmill which stood on the downs by the sea, far from any town or village, and in which the miller lived alone with his little daughter. His wife had died when the little girl, whose name was Lucilla, was a baby, and so the miller lived by himself with his child, of whom he was very proud. As her father was busy with his work, and as little Lucilla had no other children to play with, she was alone nearly all day, and had to amuse herself as best she could, and one of her greatest pleasures was to sit and watch the great sails of the windmill figures like them, and they held each other by the hand, and were dancing and springing from the ground as lightly as if they had been made of feather-down.

===Chinese===
从前，海边丘原上有一座风车磨坊，四下远离城镇村落，磨坊主带着小女儿独自住在里面。妻子去世时，小女儿露西娅（Lucilla）还在襁褓之中，磨坊主便独自带着孩子过活，并把这个女儿当作心头的骄傲。父亲整日忙于活计，小露西娅又没有别的孩子一同玩耍，几乎终日独处，只得自己想方设法取乐。她最大的乐趣之一，便是坐在那里，望着风车巨大的翼板转啊转。一天夜里，她看见翼板旁出现了一群小小的灰色身影，同翼板一般颜色；它们手牵着手，从地面上又蹦又跳地腾起，轻盈得仿佛羽绒做成的一般。

===Original===
“Come, sisters, come,” cried the one nearest Lucilla. “See, here is a little human child out here alone at twelve o’clock at night. Come and let us play with her.”

“Who are you?” asked Lucilla; “my name is Lucilla, and I live in the mill with my father.”

“We are windfairies,” said the first grey figure.

“Windfairies!” said Lucilla, “what are they?”

===Chinese===
“来呀，姐妹们，来呀，”离露西娅最近的那一个喊道，“瞧，半夜十二点，这里有个小小的人类孩子独自待着。快来，咱们跟她玩儿吧。”

“你们是谁呀？”露西娅问，“我叫露西娅，跟爸爸住在磨坊里。”

“我们是风仙子（windfairies），”第一个灰色身影说。

“风仙子！”露西娅说，“那是什么呀？”

===Original===
“We blow the winds and sweep the earth. When there are many of us together we make a great hurricane, and human beings are frightened. We it is who turn your mill wheel for you, and make all the little waves on the sea. See, if you will come with us we will take you for a ride on one of the sails of your mill. That is, if you will be brave, and not cry.”

“I will not cry one bit,” said Lucilla, and she sprang up, and held out her arms.

===Chinese===
“我们吹动风儿，扫过大地。我们许多聚在一起，就能刮起一场大飓风，吓得凡人心惊胆战。替你们推动磨盘的，正是我们；海上那些小小的浪花，也是我们造出来的。瞧，要是你肯跟我们走，我们就带你到你们磨车的一块翼板上去兜一圈。当然啦，前提是你得胆子大，不许哭。”

“我一点儿也不哭，”露西娅说着一跃而起，张开了双臂。

===Original===
At once she was lifted up, and felt herself going higher and higher, till she rested on one of the great windmill sails, and, with the little grey elves beside her, was sweeping through the air, clinging to the sail.

===Chinese===
她立刻被托了起来，只觉得自己越升越高，最后稳稳落在风车巨大的一块翼板上；小小的灰精灵们陪在她身边，随着翼板一同扫过长空，她紧紧抓着翼板。

===Original===
“She is quite good,” whispered one, as she held Lucilla in her tiny white arms. “I really think we might teach her to dance, for she has not cried at all.”

“No, she would surely tell someone if we did,” said another. “Little human child, would you like us to teach you how to dance as we dance?”

“Yes, yes,” cried Lucilla; and now they were sweeping down near the ground, and the fairies slid off the sail with Lucilla in their arms, and let her slide gently to earth. “Teach me to dance, I beg. I will never tell anybody.”

===Chinese===
“她真乖，”一个精灵用细白的小胳膊搂着露西娅，悄声说，“我看咱们真可以教她跳舞，她压根儿没哭过一声。”

“不行，真教了她，她准会说出去的，”另一个说，“小小的人类孩子，你愿意我们教你跳我们这样的舞吗？”

“愿意，愿意！”露西娅喊道。这时翼板正扫下来，掠近地面，仙子们抱起露西娅滑下翼板，轻轻把她放到地上。“求求你们教我跳舞吧。我永远不会告诉任何人的。”

===Original===
“Ah, but that is what all mortals say,” whispered one who had not spoken yet, “no mortal can keep a secret. Never yet was one known who could be silent.”

“Try me,” cried Lucilla again, “I will never tell. Indeed I will not,” and she looked entreatingly from one to another of the elves.

“But if you did,” said they, “if you broke your promise to us when once you had made it, we should punish you severely.”

“But I promise faithfully,” repeated Lucilla, “I will never tell anyone.”

===Chinese===
“唉，凡人个个都这么说，”一个还没开过口的精灵悄声道，“没有一个凡人守得住秘密。从来也没听说有谁能不吭声的。”

“那就试试我吧，”露西娅又喊道，“我决不说出去，真的决不。”她一双眼睛哀求地从这个精灵望到那个精灵。

“可你要是说了呢，”她们道，“你要是许下了诺言又背弃它，我们可要重重地罚你。”

“可我是真心实意地起誓，”露西娅再三说，“我永远不会告诉任何人。”

===Original===
“Well then, you may try,” they said. “Only remember, if you break your word to us, and tell any mortal who it was that taught you how to dance, you will never dance again, for your feet will become heavy as lead, and not only that, but some great misfortune will overtake whatever you love best in this world. But if you keep faith with us, then the windfairies will never forget you, but will come to your help in your direst hour of need.”

===Chinese===
“那么好吧，就试试你，”她们说，“只是记住：你若对我们失信，把教你跳舞的告诉了任何凡人，你就再也跳不成舞了，因为你的双脚会变得铅一般沉重；不止如此，这世上你最珍爱的东西，还会大祸临头。可你若守信于我们，风仙子就永远不会忘记你，在你最危难的时辰，定会前来相助。”

===Original===
“Teach me, teach me,” cried Lucilla; “indeed I will never, never tell, and I long to dance as you do.”

===Chinese===
“教我吧，教我吧，”露西娅喊道，“我真的永远、永远不说，我多想跳得和你们一样啊。”

===Original===
“Come then,” they said, and some came behind her, and some went in front of her, and some took her arms and some her feet, and all at once Lucilla felt as if she were made of feather-down. She swayed up and down as lightly as they, and it seemed to her quite easy. Never had she been so happy, and she would gladly have danced for hours, but suddenly, just as the sun was beginning to show a red light in the sky, she heard her father’s horse galloping over the downs, and in an instant the windfairies had vanished.

===Chinese===
“那就来吧，”她们说。于是有的跟在她身后，有的走到她前面，有的扶着她的胳膊，有的托着她的双脚，露西娅顿时觉得自己像羽绒做的一样轻。她像仙子们一样轻盈地起伏回旋，只觉得易如反掌。她从没这样快活过，真想一连跳上几个钟头。可就在太阳刚开始在天空透出红光的时候，她忽然听见父亲的马蹄声掠过丘原，一眨眼，风仙子们全都不见了。

===Original===
When the miller came up to her, he was angry with her for being out on the grass instead of warm in bed, but Lucilla dared not tell him what had kept her, or say that she had been playing with windfairies.

===Chinese===
磨坊主走到她跟前，见她不在暖和的床上睡觉，却在外头草地上，不由得生气；可露西娅不敢说出是什么绊住了她，更不敢说自己一直在和风仙子玩耍。

===Original===
Years passed, and Lucilla never saw the windfairies again, though she watched for them every night. She grew up to be a beautiful young woman, and her father was very proud of her. She was as tall and as lithe as a willow wand, and when she ran or danced it seemed as if she were as light as a feather blown in the wind. There were few people to see her, or tell her she was beautiful, for save the fisher folk who lived in little cottages on the beach, scarce anybody came to the downs. But all who saw her admired her beauty, and most of all her wonderful dancing. Sometimes she would go out on the downs, and dance and run there by herself, and her father would look at her and say: “Heaven help the maid! I don’t know whom she has learned it from, but I have never seen a dancer who can come nigh her.” Then sometimes she would go down to the seashore, and this she loved to do best of all, and there she would dance with the waves, and move with them as they slid up to her feet and drew back, and to those who watched, it seemed as if she and they were one together.

===Chinese===
一年年过去，露西娅再没见过风仙子，尽管她夜夜守望着。她长成了美丽的少妇，父亲为她深感骄傲。她身量高挑，腰肢柔软如柳条，跑起来、跳起来，仿佛一根迎风飘舞的羽毛。能看到她的人很少，能夸她美貌的人更少，因为除了住在海滩小屋里的渔家，几乎没有人到丘原来。但凡见过她的，无不赞叹她的美貌，更赞叹她妙不可言的舞姿。有时她会到丘原上去，独自又跳又舞，父亲望着她便说：“上天保佑这丫头！不知道她这是跟谁学的，反正跳得能同她比肩的人，我还没见过呢。”有时她又下到海边去——这是她最最爱做的事——在浪花间起舞，随着涌到脚边又退去的海浪一同起伏；在旁观的人眼里，她和海浪简直融为了一体。

===Original===
The time came when her father wished her to be married, and among the young fishermen and the country folk who came to the mill from the farms across the country, she had suitors enough, but always she said when a young man came to woo her, “First let me see how you can dance, for as dancing is the thing I love best in the world, it would be a pity that I and my husband should not be able to dance together,” and as none of them could dance as she did, she sent them all away, saying she would wait for a husband till she could find a man who could dance to her liking.

===Chinese===
后来到了父亲盼她出嫁的年纪。年轻的渔夫们，还有从四乡农庄来磨坊的乡下人里，向她求婚的着实不少。可每当有小伙子来向她求爱，她总说：“先让我看看你舞跳得怎么样。跳舞是我在这个世界上最心爱的事，要是我和丈夫不能一同跳舞，那就太遗憾了。”他们谁也跳不过她，便都被她打发走了，说她要等一个舞跳得合她心意的丈夫。

===Original===
But one day there was a great storm, and a big ship was blown on to the shore close to the mill, and among the sailors was a young fellow with black curly hair and bright eyes and white teeth, and when he saw Lucilla, he said to himself, “I will wed that girl and take her home for my wife.” So one day as they sat on the downs together he begged her to marry him, and go back with him to his own land; he said he would give up going to sea, and would live with her in a little cottage and make their bread by fishing. Then Lucilla said, as she had said to all her other suitors, “First let me see how you can dance, for I will never marry any man who cannot dance with me.” The sailor swore he could dance as well as any man in the world, for all sailors can dance, he said, and they began to dance together on the downs. The sailor danced well and merrily, but Lucilla danced faster, and seemed as if she were made of feather-down; and then the sailor, seeing that his dancing was as nothing to hers, caught her by the waist, and held her still, crying, “My sweetheart, I cannot dance as you can, but my arms are strong enough to hold you still and keep you from dancing with any man but me.”

===Chinese===
可是有一天起了大风暴，一条大船被吹到岸上，就搁在磨坊近旁。水手中有个小伙子，一头黑色鬈发，眼睛明亮，牙齿雪白。他一见露西娅，便暗自想道：“我要娶这姑娘，带她回家做我的妻子。”于是一天，两人一同坐在丘原上时，他便求她嫁给他，随他回自己的家乡去；他说他愿从此不再出海，同她住在一座小屋里，靠打鱼挣饭吃。于是露西娅像回绝其他求婚者一样说道：“先让我看看你舞跳得如何，凡不能与我共舞的人，我决不嫁。”水手赌咒说，他跳舞不输世上任何汉子，还说凡是水手，个个都会跳舞。两人便在丘原上跳起舞来。水手跳得欢快尽兴，可露西娅跳得更轻快，简直像羽绒做的一般。水手见自己的舞在她面前算不得什么，便一把揽住她的腰，把她抱定，喊道：“我的心肝儿，我跳舞比不上你，可我这双胳膊结实得很，能把你牢牢抱住，叫你除了我，再也别想跟任何男人跳舞！”

===Original===
So Lucilla married the sailor, and went with him to live in his little cottage by the sea, many miles away from the mill, and as her father was growing old and no longer cared to work, he went with her too.

===Chinese===
于是露西娅嫁给了水手，随他住到海边的一座小屋里，离磨坊许多英里路远。父亲年事渐高，不想再操劳，也随他们一同去了。

===Original===
For some time the sailor and Lucilla lived together very happily, and they had two little children, and her husband fished and sold his fish, and often still, Lucilla would go down to the waves and dance with them as she had done in her old home. She tried to teach her little children to dance as she did, but they could not learn because the windfairies had never touched them. But one winter her husband’s boat was dashed to pieces, and the sea froze so that all the fish died, and they became so poor that they could barely get enough to eat. Then it chanced that a big ship came to the village where they lived, and the captain wanted men for a long journey, and her husband told Lucilla that he had best go with him, and then he would have enough money to buy another boat, and then next year they must hope for better luck. So Lucilla was left alone in the cottage with her father and her two little children, and she felt very lonely and sad without her husband, and often she thought of the mill and the windfairies, and when the wind blew, she would go down to the water’s edge and hold out her arms and pray them to take care of her husband’s ship, and bring it safe home again.

===Chinese===
有一段日子，水手和露西娅过得很快活，添了两个小孩子。丈夫打鱼卖鱼，露西娅还常常像在旧家时那样，下到浪花边与海浪共舞。她想教两个孩子跳她那样的舞，可孩子们学不会，因为风仙子从来没有碰过他们。可是有一年冬天，丈夫的船被撞得粉碎，海水封冻，鱼都死绝了，他们穷得几乎吃不饱肚子。恰在此时，一条大船来到他们住的村子，船长要招人跑一趟远航。丈夫对露西娅说，他最好随船去一趟，回来就有钱再买一条船，来年指望能交上好运。于是露西娅独自留在小屋里，守着父亲和两个年幼的孩子。没了丈夫，她又孤单又伤心，常常想起磨坊和风仙子来。每逢起风，她便走到水边，张开双臂，祈求风仙子保佑丈夫的船，平平安安把他送回家来。

===Original===
“Oh, kind windfairies,” she cried, “see, I have kept faith with you, so do you now keep faith with me, and do me no hurt.” And often she would dance by the edge of the waves, as she used to do in her old home, and think that the windfairies were dancing with her, and holding up her steps.

===Chinese===
“噢，善良的风仙子，”她喊道，“看哪，我守信于你们，如今也请你们守信于我，莫降祸于我。”她还常常像在旧家时那样，在浪花边上跳舞，心想风仙子正陪着她共舞，托着她轻盈的舞步。

===Original===
Now it chanced that one day, as Lucilla was dancing on the shore, there rode by two horsemen, and they stopped and watched her as she danced, with the waves coming close to her feet. Then they got down from their horses, and asked who she was, and where she had learned such dancing. She told them she was only the wife of a poor fisherman, but she had danced for long years, since she was a little child, when she had lived in a windmill, on the downs far away. They rode away, but next day they came again, and brought others with them, and begged Lucilla that she would go down to the water’s edge and dance with the waves as she had done yesterday. So she ran down the beach, and danced in time to the sea as it moved, and the strangers all applauded, and said to each other, “It is wonderful, it is marvellous.”

===Chinese===
且说有一天，露西娅正在岸上跳舞，恰巧有两个骑马人经过，便停下马，看她伴着涌到脚边的海浪起舞。随后两人下了马，问她是何人，这身舞艺是从哪里学来的。她答说，自己不过是个穷渔夫的妻子，可从小孩子时起就跳舞了，那时她住在远方丘原上的一座风车磨坊里。两人骑马走了，第二天却又来了，还带来了一群同伴，恳求露西娅再到水边去，像昨天那样与海浪共舞。于是她沿着海滩跑下去，随着海浪的起伏应节起舞，陌生人们个个喝彩，彼此叹道：“真是奇妙，真是不可思议。”

===Original===
They then told her that they came from a country where the King loved nothing so much as beautiful dancing, and that he would give great sums of money to anyone who danced well, and if she would go back with them to his court, and dance before the King, she should have a sack of gold to take home with her, and this would make her a rich woman, and her husband would never have need to work any more.

===Chinese===
随后他们告诉她，他们来自一个国度，那里的国王爱美妙的舞姿胜过世上一切，谁舞跳得好，他就肯出大把的钱财；只要她肯随他们回宫，在国王面前跳舞，就能得满满一袋金子带回家，从此成为富家妇人，丈夫再也不用劳苦了。

===Original===
At first she refused, and said her husband was away, and would not know where she was gone, and she did not like to leave her two little children; but still the courtiers persuaded her, and said it would not be for long, and her father persuaded her too, since he said it would make them all rich if she brought home a sack of gold. So at last Lucilla agreed that she would go back with them to the King’s court and dance there, but she made them promise that before the spring came they would send her back to her own little cottage. On hearing this, the strangers were much delighted, and bid Lucilla make ready to start at once, and that night she said goodbye to her little ones, and left them, to go with the travellers. Her eyes were red with crying at leaving her home, and before she started, she went out alone on to the cliffs, and stretched out her arms, and called to the windfairies to go with her and help her, for she feared what she was going to do, and she begged them to be true to her, as she had been true to them.

===Chinese===
起初她不肯，说丈夫在外，回来找不到她会不知去向；她又舍不得丢下两个小孩子。可廷臣们再三劝说，说去不了多久；父亲也劝她，说只要她带回一袋金子，全家就都富足了。露西娅终于答应随他们回王宫献舞，但要他们起誓：开春之前务必送她回自己的小屋。陌生人们听了大喜，叫露西娅即刻收拾动身。当夜她吻别了两个小孩子，随旅人们上路了。离别家门时，她的双眼哭得通红。动身之前，她独自走上悬崖，张开双臂，呼唤风仙子随她同去、助她一臂之力，因为她害怕此行凶吉难卜；她恳求风仙子们不负她，正如她从未负过她们。

===Original===
They sailed for many days, till at last they came to a country of which Lucilla had never even heard, and to a big town, which seemed to her as if it must hold all the people in the world, so crowded was it, and above the town on the hill, they pointed out to her a royal palace, and told her it was where the King dwelt, and there she would have to dance ere the week was out.

===Chinese===
他们航行了许多天，终于来到一个露西娅连听都不曾听说过的国度，来到一座大城。那城拥挤不堪，在她看来，仿佛天下的人全都装在里面了。在城上方的小山上，他们指给她看一座王宫，说国王就住在那里；不出这个星期，她就得在那里献舞了。

===Original===
“And it is most lucky we saw you just now,” said they, “for the King is just going to be married, and in a few days the Princess will arrive, and there will be festivities and rejoicing for days, and at some of these you will appear before their Majesties, and be sure you dance your very best.”

===Chinese===
“幸好我们眼下遇见了你，”他们说，“因为国王就要成婚了，过几天公主就要驾到，接连许多天都要饮宴庆贺；在几场庆典上，你就要在国王王后两位陛下跟前露面献舞，届时千万要拿出你最精妙的舞艺来。”

===Original===
Then Lucilla went with them into a great hall close to the palace, where musicians were playing on every kind of instrument, and here the courtiers bid her dance on a platform at one end of the hall, in time to the music; and when they had seen it, the musicians one and all lay down their instruments, and rose together, clapping and applauding, and all declared that it was the greatest of luck that the travellers had met with Lucilla, and that it would delight the King more than anything they had prepared for him.

===Chinese===
随后他们带她进了王宫旁的一座大厅，乐师们正奏着各式各样的乐器。廷臣们请她在大厅一端的一座高台上，应着乐声起舞。一曲舞罢，乐师们不约而同放下乐器，一齐起身鼓掌喝彩，个个都说，旅人们寻到露西娅真是天大的运气，她准能比他们预备下的一切更叫国王欢喜。

===Original===
By and by the Princess who was to marry the King arrived, and the wedding was celebrated with much magnificence, and after the wedding there was a feast, and in the evening there was to be singing and dancing, and all sorts of play for the royal couple and the court to see, and then Lucilla was to dance. The courtier who brought her wished her to be dressed in the most gorgeous dress, with gold and jewels, but she pleaded that she might wear a light grey gown like the windfairies, because she remembered how they looked when they danced on the downs.

===Chinese===
不久，将要嫁给国王的公主到了，婚礼办得极尽奢华。婚宴之后，晚间有歌有舞，还有各式各样的余兴，供王室和宫廷观赏，然后便轮到露西娅献舞了。领她来的那位廷臣要她穿最华丽的衣裙，缀金带宝，可她恳求让她穿一件风仙子那样的浅灰色长裙，因为她记得它们在丘原上起舞时的模样。

===Original===
When the evening came when she was to dance before the King, she threw wide her window and held out her arms, and cried out, “Now help me, dear windfairies, as you have done before; keep faith with me, as I have kept faith with you.” But in truth she could scarce keep from crying with thoughts of her husband at sea, and her little ones at the cottage at home.

===Chinese===
到了在国王面前献舞的那个傍晚，她推开窗子，张开双臂，高声喊道：“亲爱的风仙子，如今像往常一样帮帮我吧；请守信于我，一如我守信于你们。”其实，一想到丈夫漂在海上、小儿小女留在家里的小屋中，她几乎忍不住要落泪。

===Original===
The hall was brilliantly lighted, and in the middle on the throne sat the King and the young Queen. The musicians began to play, and then Lucilla stepped forth on the platform and began to dance. She felt as light as the sea foam, and when she swayed and curved to the sound of the music, it seemed to her as if she heard only the swish of the waves as they beat upon the shore, and the murmur of the wind as it played with the water, and she thought of her husband out at sea, with the wind blowing his ship along, and of her little babies living in the cottage on the beach.

===Chinese===
大厅里灯火通明，正中的王座上坐着国王和年轻的王后。乐声奏起，露西娅步上高台，翩然起舞。她觉得自己轻得像海上的浪花；当她应着乐声俯仰回旋时，耳中仿佛只听见浪涛拍岸的沙沙声，和风儿拂水时的低语。她想起远在海上的丈夫，风正吹送着他的船；想起住在海滩小屋里的两个孩子。

===Original===
When she stopped, there was such a noise of applauding and cheering in the hall, as had never been heard there before, and the King sent for her, and asked her where she came from, and who had taught her such wonderful steps, but she only answered that she was the daughter of a poor miller, who lived in a windmill, and she thought she must have learnt to dance from watching the windmill’s sails go round. Every night the King would have her dance again and again, as he never tired of watching her, and every night Lucilla said to herself, “Now another night is gone, and I am one day nearer to their taking me back to my own home and my children, with a bag of gold to give to my husband when he comes back from sea.”

===Chinese===
她一停舞，大厅里便响起前所未有的喝彩欢呼。国王召她上前，问她从哪里来，这样奇妙的舞步是谁教的。她只答说，自己是穷磨坊主的女儿，住在一座风车磨坊里，想来是看风车翼板转啊转，才学会了跳舞。国王夜夜都召她一再起舞，百看不厌；露西娅也夜夜在心里念叨：“又过去一夜了，他们送我回家、回到孩子们身边的日子又近了一天；等丈夫出海回来，我就能把一袋金子交到他手里。”

===Original===
The new Queen was a handsome woman, but she was very jealous, and it made her angry that the King should admire the new dancer’s dancing so much, and she thought she would like to be able to dance like her. So one evening when no one was watching her, she put on a big cloak that covered her all over, and asked her way to where the dancer lived. Lucilla sat alone in the little house that they had given her to live in, and the Queen came in behind her, and took off her cloak, and bade her be silent and not say her name, for fear someone should be listening and know that she was there.

===Chinese===
新王后生得标致，却善妒。国王如此赞赏新舞伶的舞艺，叫她心里冒火；她又很想自己也能跳得像她那样。于是一天晚上，趁没人留意，她披上一件连头带脚罩住的大斗篷，一路打听，寻到舞伶的住处。露西娅正独自坐在拨给她住的小屋里，王后悄悄走到她身后进了屋，解下斗篷，吩咐她不许作声、不许说出自己的名号，生怕有人偷听，走漏了她在此的风声。

===Original===
“Now,” she said, “I have come to you that you may tell me, though no one else knows it, who taught you to dance, that I may go and learn from them also to dance like you; for in the home that I come from, I was said to be the most graceful woman in the land and the best dancer, so that there is no dancing that I cannot learn.”

===Chinese===
“你听好，”她说，“我来找你，是要你告诉我——这事除了我再没人知道——教你跳舞的究竟是谁，我也好去寻那人学艺，跳得跟你一样。因为在我娘家，人人说我是全国最窈窕的女子、最出色的舞者，天下没有我学不会的舞。”

===Original===
Lucilla trembled, but she answered:

“Your Majesty, I lived in a little windmill by the sea when I was a child, far from teachers or dancers, but I watched the windmill sails go round, morn, noon, and night; and perhaps it is that that taught me to dance as I do now. And if your Majesty wishes to learn to do what I do, I will gladly teach you all I know, and doubtless you will soon learn to dance far better than I.”

===Chinese===
露西娅浑身发颤，却答道：

“陛下，我小时候住在一座海边的小风车磨坊里，远离名师舞师，只是从早到晚、日日夜夜望着风车的翼板转啊转；或许正是这个，教会了我如今这样的舞。陛下若想学我的舞，我甘愿倾囊相授，您一定很快就能跳得远胜过我。”

===Original===
Upon this the Queen was delighted, and flung aside her cloak, and stood opposite to Lucilla, and begged her to begin to teach her at once, that she might learn as soon as possible. All that evening they danced, but when the Queen thought she looked just as Lucilla did, she appeared to be quite awkward and heavy beside her, and was dancing just as other mortals might. When she went away she was very much pleased, and said that she would come twice more to learn from her, and then she was sure that she would be perfect. In her heart Lucilla was very much frightened, because she knew that the Queen did not dance as she did, and never could. However, the next night she came again, and the next again, and then there was to be a grand court ball; and at this the Queen thought she would first show her husband how she could dance. The King himself was fond of dancing, and danced well, although not half so well as Lucilla’s husband the sailor; and the Queen thought how delighted he would be when he saw what a graceful wife he had got. As the ball began, all the fine people were saying to each other, it really seemed silly to dance after they had seen the wonderful new dancer, but the Queen smiled and thought to herself, “Now they will see that I can do quite as well as she.” When her turn came she tripped lightly forward and danced as best she could, and thought it was just like Lucilla, and the courtiers said among each other, “Our new Queen dances well,” but no one thought of saying that it was like Lucilla’s dancing, and the King said nothing at all on the matter; therefore the Queen felt herself growing hot and angry, and she turned red and white by turns.

===Chinese===
王后听了满心欢喜，甩掉斗篷，站到露西娅对面，求她立刻开教，好早日学成。那一晚她们跳了个够，可王后自以为舞姿已同露西娅一般无二，站在她身旁却显得笨拙沉重，跳得同寻常凡人没有两样。临走时她心满意足，说还要再来学两回，那时她的舞就必定尽善尽美了。露西娅心里却怕得厉害，因为她知道，王后跳不成她那样的舞，永远也跳不成。然而第二天夜里王后又来了，第三天夜里又来。随后宫中要举行一场盛大的舞会，王后打算在舞会上先让国王瞧瞧自己的舞艺。国王本人也爱跳舞，且跳得不坏——只是还不及露西娅的丈夫、那个水手一半好。王后一心想着，国王见自己娶了这样一位身姿曼妙的王后，该多么欢喜。舞会开始时，满堂的贵人们彼此嘀咕：看过了那位奇妙的新舞伶，再看谁跳舞都显得可笑了。王后却面带微笑，暗自想道：“这回他们该瞧瞧，我跳得一点也不比她差。”轮到她时，她轻盈款步上前，竭尽所能地跳着，自以为同露西娅一模一样。廷臣们彼此称道：“咱们的新王后舞跳得真好。”可谁也没说这舞像露西娅的舞；国王对此更是只字不提。王后只觉得心头火起，一阵红一阵白。

===Original===
“That lying wench has been tricking me,” she said to herself, “and she has not taught me right at all; but I will punish her for her deception, and soon she shall know what it is to deceive a Queen.”

===Chinese===
“那个撒谎的贱人一直在耍弄我，”她自语道，“她压根儿没好好教我；我要治她的欺瞒之罪，很快她就会知道，欺骗王后是什么下场。”

===Original===
So the next day she went to her husband and said, “Husband, I have thought much of the new wonderful dancer whom we all admire so much, and truly I have never seen anyone on earth who could dance as she can; but now I think we should do well before she goes back to her own home to know who has taught her her marvellous art, so that we may have our court dancers taught, that they may be there to please us when she is gone, for really there is nothing on earth that cannot be learnt if it is taught in the right way.”

===Chinese===
第二天，她便去对丈夫说：“夫君，那位人人叹赏的奇妙舞伶，我想了许多。说真的，世上再没有第二个人跳得她那样好了。可我想，趁她回家之前，我们最好先问清楚，她这手惊人的绝艺究竟是谁教的，也好叫我们的宫廷舞师去学来，日后她走了，还有人给我们取乐；须知天下的事情，只要教得得法，没有学不会的。”

===Original===
The King agreed, and they sent for Lucilla, and the King asked her to tell him where she had learnt her dancing, that they might summon the same teachers to teach their court dancers. But Lucilla answered as before—she did not know—she thought she must have learnt dancing from watching the windmill sails going round. At this the King became angry, and said, “That is nonsense, no one could learn dancing from looking at windmill sails, neither was it possible that she, a poor miller’s daughter, could have learnt such dancing by nature;” then he threatened her, that if she would not tell him the truth he should be obliged to punish her, and he said she should have a day to think of it in, but at the end of the next day, he should expect her to tell him everything he wanted to know quite plainly.

===Chinese===
国王依允了，便召来露西娅，要她讲出在何处学的舞，好传召同一位师傅来教宫廷舞师。可露西娅仍像先前那样回答——她不知道——她想，多半是望着风车翼板转啊转才学会跳舞的。国王听了大怒，说：“胡说八道，看风车翼板哪能学会跳舞；一个穷磨坊主的女儿，天生也学不会这样的舞。”接着他又威吓她：若再不从实招来，他就要罚她了；他说给她一天工夫细细思量，到明天日暮，他可要她把想知道的事原原本本、一五一十地讲出来。

===Original===
When she was gone away the King said to the Queen, “Wife, if this dancer persists in her silence, and will not tell us how she has learnt, there is another thing which we must do. We must keep her here to dance for us as much as we choose, and not let her return at all to the home from which she came.”

===Chinese===
露西娅退下后，国王对王后说：“王后，这舞伶若死不改口，不肯说出师承，我们就只有另一条路：把她留在宫里，随我们高兴叫她跳多久就跳多久，再也不放她回老家去。”

===Original===
The Queen was silent for a little, but she felt very jealous at the thought of the dancer remaining at the court, so she nodded her head and said, “Yes, but I think she ought to tell us more about it; for myself, I begin to think that it is witchcraft, and perhaps she has been taught by the Evil One, and then we shouldn’t like her to remain here and dance to us however beautiful it be, for who knows what ill luck it might not bring upon us?” Upon this the King looked grave, and said he did not believe much in ill luck or good luck, but he should be loth to lose the dancer, so they had better settle to keep her if she declined to tell them how the other dancers were to be taught.

===Chinese===
王后沉默了片刻。一想到要把这舞伶留在宫里，她心里妒火直冒，便点头道：“好啊。只是我想，她总该对我们多招认一些；拿我来说，我越想越觉得这是巫术，说不定是恶魔教她的。若当真如此，她跳得再好看，我们也不愿她留在此地为我们献舞了——谁知道会不会给我们招来什么灾祸？”国王听了神色凝重，说他并不大信什么厄运好运，可实在舍不得这位舞伶，于是两人商定：她若不肯说出旁人该如何学舞，就把她留下。

===Original===
Meantime Lucilla went back to her little house, and wept bitterly. “Would that I had never left my babes and my home,” she cried, “for I cannot break my word to the windfairies, and if I did they might do some terrible harm to my little ones or to my husband at sea; yet if I refuse to tell them they will most likely put me into prison, and there I shall remain for my life, and my husband and children will never know what has become of me.” And she knelt down before the windows and lifted her arms and cried out, “Oh, dear windfairies, I have not broken faith with you, so don’t break faith with me, and come to my help and save me in my trouble.”

===Chinese===
露西娅回到小屋里，哭得伤心极了。“唉，我不该丢下我的孩子和我的家，”她哭道，“因为我不能背弃对风仙子许下的诺言；我若背弃了，她们说不定会降大祸给我的孩子，或给我海上的丈夫；可我若不肯说，他们多半要把我关进牢里，一辈子待在那儿，丈夫和孩子永远不知道我的下落。”她跪在窗前，举起双臂，高声喊道：“噢，亲爱的风仙子，我从未失信于你们，也求你们莫要失信于我，来救救我，救我出这场灾殃。”

===Original===
Next evening Lucilla went again before the King, and he said to her, “Well, now will you tell us what we asked you last night, so that we may send for your teachers, and have others taught to dance as you do?”

===Chinese===
第二天晚上，露西娅又被带到国王面前，国王对她说：“怎么样，昨夜问你的话，如今肯说了么？我们也好运你的师傅来，教旁人跳得同你一样。”

===Original===
“My gracious liege,” answered Lucilla, “I can tell you nothing that I have not told you before. Since I was a child I have danced as I dance now, and I watched the sails of my father’s windmill, and I danced in time to the waves, and perhaps that is what taught me to keep time and step so well. I was dancing by the seashore when the travellers who brought me here found me, and they promised me a bag of gold to take home to my husband if I would come and dance at your Majesty’s court; and now you have seen me dance, and I have done all I can do, so I entreat you to give me the bag of gold, and let me go home again.”

===Chinese===
“仁慈的君王陛下，”露西娅答道，“我说不出先前没说过的话来。我从小孩子时起，就一直跳如今这样的舞；我望着父亲风车的翼板，应着海浪的节拍跳舞，或许正是这个，教会了我这样好的步子与节拍。带我来的旅人们发现我时，我正在海边跳舞；他们许我一袋金子带回给丈夫，条件是我来陛下宫廷献舞。如今你们已看过我跳舞，我也尽了全力，所以我恳求陛下把金袋给我，放我回家去吧。”

===Original===
The King was silent, but the Queen was still more angry, and in her heart was determined that Lucilla should never return to her home until she had found out about her dancing. So when they were alone she said to her husband, “It is now quite clear, it is by witchcraft that this woman has learned, and we should do very wrong if we let her go till she has confessed all.” So again they sent for Lucilla and ordered her to confess, and again she wept and declared that she could tell no more. Then the King said, “Well, let us give the woman her bag of gold and let her go,” but the Queen stopped him, and said, “No indeed, let us first try shutting her up in prison for a bit, and see if that won’t open her lips.”

===Chinese===
国王默然，王后却越发恼怒，心里打定主意：不查清她舞艺的来历，绝不放她回家。待到只剩他们二人，她便对丈夫说：“如今一清二楚了，这女人是靠巫术学的舞。不逼她招认个明白就放她走，我们可就大错特错了。”于是他们又召来露西娅，逼她招供，她又哭着申明，实在无可再说了。国王说：“罢了，把金袋给她，放她走吧。”王后却拦住道：“万万不可，先把她关进牢里一阵子，看看能不能撬开她的嘴。”

===Original===
At first the King refused, for he said that Lucilla had done no wrong, but the Queen insisted that she was deceiving them, and that her dancing must be witchcraft, and at last the King began to listen to her. Also he was very angry with Lucilla for wanting to go home, and much disappointed to think he should see her dancing no more; so he consented, and said that either she must tell him how it was she came to be able to dance better than anybody else in this world, and who taught her, or else they should think her dancing witchcraft, and she must go to prison and wait her punishment.

===Chinese===
起初国王不肯，说露西娅并无过错。可王后咬定她欺瞒众人，咬定她的舞必是巫术，国王终于渐渐听信了她。加之露西娅一心想回家，国王恼她，又想到从此看不成她的舞，大失所望，便依允了，说：她要么讲出她怎会跳得比世上所有人都好、是谁教的，要么他们便认定她的舞是巫术，把她下狱候惩。

===Original===
Poor Lucilla wept most bitterly. “Alas!” cried she to herself, “woe is me, for I dare not break faith with the windfairies, and yet if I do not, I shall never see my husband or my babies again, for I fear lest they may put me to death here.”

===Chinese===
可怜的露西娅哭得肝肠寸断。“唉！”她自悲自叹，“我这苦命人！我不敢背弃对风仙子的信义；可若不背弃，只怕再也不能见到丈夫和我的小宝贝们了，他们说不定会把我处死在这里呢。”

===Original===
However, she continued to be silent, and the King ordered her to be put into prison until she should speak out and tell them the truth; and the guards came and led her away to prison, and locked her into a dark cell. It was dreary and cold, and the walls were so thick that she could not hear any of the noises from without, and there was only one little window, which was too high up for her to see through. Here she lay and lamented, and almost wished she could die at once, for she believed that they would burn her, or drown her, and bitterly did she grieve that she had left her home and her children.

===Chinese===
然而她依旧守口如瓶。国王便下令把她关进牢里，等她肯说出实话再放；狱卒们走来，把她押进牢中，锁进一间黑牢。牢里又冷又凄凉，墙厚得听不见外面一丝声响，只有一扇小窗，高得望不出去。她躺在那儿哀哀哭泣，几乎恨不得立刻死去，因为她认定他们要烧死她，或是淹死她；她悔不该离开家、离开孩子，悲恸欲绝。

===Original===
Every day the King sent down to ask if she had changed her mind, but every day she answered that she had nothing to say. One evening she sat in her dark cell alone, grieving as usual, when the prison door opened, and there entered a woman wrapped in a cloak and with her face hidden by a mask. When she took off the mask Lucilla saw it was the Queen, and she sprang up hoping that she had come to tell her that she was to be released, but the Queen said, “Now I have come to you alone that you may tell me the truth. Who taught you to dance, and where can I learn to do what you do? If you will tell me I will ask the King to forgive you, and you shall have your bag of gold, and go when you like.”

===Chinese===
国王天天差人来问，她可回心转意了；她天天答说，无可奉告。一天傍晚，她正独自坐在黑牢里照例伤心，牢门开了，进来一个女子，裹着斗篷，脸上罩着面具。那女子摘下面具，露西娅认出是王后，一跃而起，只当她来传旨释放。王后却说：“我这回独自来见你，是要你说实话。教你跳舞的是谁？我上哪儿能学你会的那些？你若说出来，我便求国王饶了你，把金袋给你，随你几时走。”

===Original===
Then poor Lucilla began to cry afresh, and said, “My gracious lady, I can tell you one thing that I have not yet told to anyone, that is, that I did learn my dancing, but who told me, or how it was, is a secret that I swore I would never tell to anyone. And now I implore your Royal Highness to let me go back to my fisherman husband, and my babies. Alack! alack! it was an evil hour for me when I left my home.”

===Chinese===
可怜的露西娅又哭了起来，说：“仁慈的娘娘，有一件事我还不曾对任何人讲过：我这舞确实是学来的；可谁教的、怎么学的，是我起过誓永不告诉任何人的秘密。如今我求殿下垂恩，放我回到我那渔夫丈夫和孩子们身边去吧。唉！唉！我离开家门的那一刻，真是个凶时辰啊。”

===Original===
Upon this the Queen became furious, but she hid her anger, and first she tried to coax Lucilla to confess all, then she threatened her with the King’s wrath, and then, as Lucilla still wept and said that she could not break her promise, she started up in a rage, and said, “Indeed, it is of little use, however much you love your husband and your children, for you will never see them again. The King has settled that you shall be killed this very week, so now you know what you have gained by your wicked obstinacy.”

===Chinese===
王后听了勃然大怒，却把怒气按住。她先是哄露西娅全盘招认，又拿国王的雷霆之怒威吓她；露西娅只是啼哭，说不能背弃自己的诺言。王后终于按捺不住，霍地跳起来，说：“老实告诉你，你再疼你的丈夫孩子也没用了，你休想再见他们了。国王已定下，就在这一礼拜内处死你。如今你可知道，你那顽固不化给你挣来了什么。”

===Original===
So the Queen returned to the King, and told him that the dancer had confessed that she had learned her dancing, but she would not say from whom, therefore it must be from the Evil One, and therefore there was nothing for it but that she should be killed. So they settled that first they would try to drown Lucilla, and if she were a witch she would not sink, and the King gave orders that she should be taken out to sea next day and thrown overboard, and also that she should have heavy weights tied to her feet, and her arms should be bound to her sides.

===Chinese===
王后回去见国王，说那舞伶已招认舞是学来的，却死不肯说跟谁学的；可见教她的必是恶魔，那就别无办法，只好处死她。于是他们商定，先试着淹死露西娅：她若是女巫，便沉不下去。国王下令，第二天把她押出海去扔下船，还要在她脚上缚上重物，把双臂绑在身侧。

===Original===
Next morning the guards fetched her, and they bound her arms to her sides, and tied heavy weights to her feet, and they took her down and placed her in a boat on the seashore, and they rowed her out to sea, and all along the beach stood crowds of people, shouting and jeering, and calling out, “She is a witch! she is a witch! the King has done well to have her killed.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，狱卒们来提她，把她的双臂绑在身侧，脚上缚了重物，押她下去，放进海滩边的一条小船，划出海去。海滩上沿路站满了人，又喊又笑地起哄，高叫着：“她是女巫！她是女巫！国王杀她杀得对！”

===Original===
“Alas! alas!” cried Lucilla, “what have I done to deserve this? surely I have done no wrong to be so cruelly treated. Dear windfairies, come to my help, for in truth now is the time of my direst need, and if you desert me I am lost; but I pray you keep faith with me, as I have kept faith with you.” Then, when they had rowed the boat out a little way, the guards seized her, and threw her into the water, and the salt waves splashed over her face and through her hair; but in spite of the heavy weights on her feet she never sank, but felt as light as when she danced with the waves on the seashore by her home, and she knew that the windfairies held her up; and the waves rocked her gently, and drew her in towards the land, and laid her on the sand, and all the crowd yelled with rage.

===Chinese===
“唉！唉！”露西娅喊道，“我做了什么孽，要受这样的罪？我实在没有作恶，却遭这般狠毒的对待。亲爱的风仙子，来救救我吧，如今真是我最危难的时辰了；你们若丢下我，我就完了。求你们守信于我，一如我守信于你们。”船划出不远，狱卒们便抓住她，把她抛进水里。咸涩的浪花溅上她的脸，灌进她的头发；可尽管脚上缚着重物，她却始终不沉，只觉得自己轻快得很，就像从前在家乡海边同浪花共舞时一样。她知道，是风仙子托住了她。浪涛轻轻摇着她，把她缓缓推向岸边，放在沙滩上。人群气得齐声狂吼。

===Original===
When they found that Lucilla could not be drowned both the King and Queen were very angry, and said that now it was quite clear that she was a witch, and that she must be burnt, so they must take her back to prison, and arrange for her to be burnt in the marketplace. So Lucilla was again taken back to her little dark cell, and she kneeled on the ground and looked up to the window, and murmured, “Thank you, dear windfairies, you have kept faith with me, as I have kept faith with you.”

===Chinese===
国王和王后见淹不死露西娅，恼恨万分，说这下更清楚了，她分明是个女巫，非烧死不可；得先押回牢里，安排在集市广场上把她烧死。露西娅又被押回那间小小的黑牢。她跪在地上，仰头望着小窗，喃喃说道：“谢谢你们，亲爱的风仙子，你们守信于我，一如我守信于你们。”

===Original===
Then again the guards came, and took her by the arms and led her to the marketplace, and here she saw a great pile of wood made, whereon she was to be laid, and already men were busy setting fire to it. But as Lucilla and the guards came to the spot, there arose a little breeze, and it blew on to the faces of the crowd who went to see her burnt. The men who were trying to light the pile of wood, said they could not make it catch for the wind; when at last it did catch fire, the flames would not rise in the air, but were blown along the ground. Still they brought Lucilla up to the pile, and placed her upon it, and then the flames divided on each side, and were blown away from her all round, so she sat in the midst quite unhurt.

===Chinese===
随后狱卒们又来了，架着她的胳膊，把她押到集市广场。那里已经架起一大堆柴，就要把她放上去，已经有人忙着点火了。可露西娅和狱卒们刚到跟前，忽地起了一阵小风，吹在来看烧人的人群脸上。点火的人说，风大，柴堆点不着；好不容易点着了，火苗又不往天上蹿，只贴着地面烧。他们仍把露西娅押上柴堆，放到上面；火苗随即向两边分开，在她四周四下里吹散，她端坐在柴堆中央，毫发无伤。

===Original===
At this the people all cried out, “Now we know that she really is a witch, since she will not drown and the fire will not burn her,” and they ran to tell the King and the Queen that the dancing woman did not mind the fire, but sat in the midst of it unhurt. On hearing this the King and Queen came down to the marketplace together, and saw Lucilla sitting on the pile of wood, and the flames blown away from her on all sides, and causing a great hubbub; so they told the guards to take her back to prison and keep her there, till they could arrange for her to be beheaded. And again Lucilla bent her head, and said, “Now I know, dear windfairies, that you will never desert me, and I have nothing to fear, for while I keep faith with you, you will keep faith with me.”

===Chinese===
众人见了都叫嚷起来：“这下可知她真是个女巫了，淹不死，火烧不着！”他们跑去禀报国王和王后，说那舞女不怕火烧，坐在火当中安然无恙。国王和王后闻讯一齐来到集市广场，见露西娅坐在柴堆上，火焰朝四面八方吹散开去，顿时闹作一团；便吩咐狱卒把她押回牢里严加看管，等安排好将她斩首。露西娅又低下头，说：“如今我知道了，亲爱的风仙子，你们永远不会丢下我；我什么也不怕了，因为只要我守信于你们，你们便会守信于我。”

===Original===
By now it was getting late in the day, and the King commanded that Lucilla should not be executed till next day, and that the scaffold should be erected in the marketplace, on which the block should be put, so that all the crowd might see, and both he and the Queen would be there. But in order to give her one last chance that everyone might see how fair they were, the King offered that if she would confess, even when she was upon the scaffold, who had taught her to dance, she should be allowed to return whence she came, and take her bag of gold with her, and therefore the bag of gold was placed on the scaffold so that all the people might see, and the bag was so large that Lucilla could scarcely lift it.

===Chinese===
这时天色已晚。国王传旨，露西娅明日方可处决；刑台要搭在集市广场上，台上安放断头墩，好教众人看得清楚，他和王后也要亲临。不过，为了给她最后一个机会，也教众人看看他们何等公道，国王传话说：她纵然上了刑台，只要肯招出教她跳舞的是谁，便准她返回家乡，还可带走那袋金子。因此那袋金子就摆在刑台上，好教人人看见。那袋子实在大得很，露西娅几乎提都提不动。

===Original===
That evening Lucilla felt no fear, and she would have slept calmly in her cell, but the wind was beginning to blow in all directions, and all round she heard it roaring, and the trees were bending and breaking in the gale. When the morning came, the King and Queen said to each other, “This is the morning when they should execute the dancer, but it will be hard to get her on to the scaffold with a gale like this blowing.” However, the guards came to Lucilla’s cell, and took her out as before, and led her towards the marketplace, though they had much ado to get along, for the wind blew so hard that they could scarce keep upright in it. All along the coast the little boats were being blown in to shore, and there were big ships, which had been driven in, to take refuge from the storm. But Lucilla felt no fear, only she looked up to the wind, and in her heart she said, “Now, dear windfairies, help me for the last time, and keep faith with me, as I have kept faith with you.”

===Chinese===
那天晚上，露西娅心里毫不害怕，本可安安稳稳睡上一觉。可是风四面八方刮了起来，四下里到处是风的咆哮，树在狂风里弯折断裂。天亮时，国王和王后彼此说道：“今天该处决那舞女了，可刮这样的大风，要把她弄上刑台，怕是难上加难。”话虽如此，狱卒们还是来到牢房，照旧提出露西娅，押往集市广场，一路走得万分吃力，风刮得他们几乎立不住脚。沿海的小船都被风往岸上吹，还有躲风暴进港来的大船。可露西娅毫不畏惧，只仰头望着风，心里默念：“亲爱的风仙子，最后再帮帮我一次吧，请守信于我，一如我守信于你们。”

===Original===
Near the shore came a big ship with shining white sails, riding over the crested waves, and although all the other boats seemed troubled by the wind, and some were dismasted and others were wrecked, this boat seemed no way hurt by it, and the people who saw it called out, “What a gallant ship it was, and how brave the captain must be, who knew so well how to manage wind and water.” But when they knew that the time had come for Lucilla to be beheaded, the people did not trouble further about the boats, and in spite of the gale they flocked to the marketplace, and crowded round the scaffold on which was the block.

===Chinese===
近岸处驶来一条大船，张着雪亮的白帆，破浪而来。别的船都被风折腾得够呛，有的断了桅，有的撞得粉碎，唯独这条船丝毫不见损伤。望见的人都喊：“多神气的一条船！那船长真有胆识，竟这样通晓驾驭风浪的本事。”可后来人们知道露西娅就要被斩首了，便顾不得再操心船只，冒着大风涌向集市广场，围在安着断头墩的刑台四周。

===Original===
Then the guards and Lucilla mounted the scaffold, and Lucilla began to fear that at last the windfairies had forsaken her, and she wept and held out her arms, and cried out, “Oh, dear windfairies, indeed I have kept my faith with you, surely, surely you will keep yours with me.” In spite of the terrible gale, the King and the Queen came down to the marketplace, though they could scarce see or hear for the wind, though all the time the sun was shining and the sky was blue. Then the guards bid Lucilla kneel down and place her head upon the block, and the bag of gold was beside her, and they said, “This is your last chance, speak now and confess the truth to the King, and here is your gold, and you shall go.” And Lucilla answered as before, “I have spoken the truth, and there is no more that I can tell, since I have sworn never to say from whom I learnt my dancing.”

===Chinese===
狱卒们押着露西娅登上刑台。露西娅开始害怕风仙子终究弃了她，流着泪张开双臂，高声喊道：“噢，亲爱的风仙子，我实在守信于你们了，你们千万、千万也要守信于我呀！”任凭风狂，国王和王后还是来到了集市广场，虽被风吹得几乎睁不开眼、听不见声；而天上的太阳一直朗照着，天空湛蓝。狱卒们叫露西娅跪下，把头搁上断头墩，那袋金子就放在她身旁。他们说：“这是你最后的机会了。说吧，向国王招出实话，这金子便是你的，即刻放你走。”露西娅仍像先前那样回答：“我说的都是实话，再无可奉告了，我起过誓，决不说出教我跳舞的是谁。”

===Original===
Then the executioner lifted the axe in the air, but before it fell, there came a sudden roar of wind, and the axe was swept from his hand, and the houses in the marketplace tottered and fell, and high up on the hill the palace was a mass of ruins. Only Lucilla knelt upon the scaffold unhurt, for the King and the Queen and all the people were blown right and left, amidst the ruins of the houses, and no one thought of anything save how they could save themselves.

===Chinese===
刽子手把斧头高高抡起。可斧头还没落下，忽然刮起一阵狂风，斧头从他手里被卷走了；集市广场上的房屋摇摇晃晃，纷纷倒塌，山上的宫殿也塌成一片废墟。只有露西娅跪在刑台上安然无恙。国王、王后和众人都被吹得东倒西歪，跌在塌屋的瓦砾之间，谁都顾不上别的，只想着怎么逃命。

===Original===
Then Lucilla lifted her head and looked out to sea, and saw the big ship coming in, and she heard the sailors cry, “Heyday, these poor folk are in a sad plight, we had better go and help them,” and they all trooped up into the marketplace, and the wind troubled them no more than it had troubled their ship. But when Lucilla looked at them, the first whom she saw was her husband, and she gave a great cry, and held out her arms, and called out, “Now, dear windfairies, do I indeed know that you have kept faith with me, and saved me in my direst hour of need.”

===Chinese===
露西娅抬起头来，望向大海，看见那条大船正驶进来。她听见水手们喊：“嘿，这些可怜人遭了大难，咱们快去帮他们一把！”他们成群结队走上集市广场，风搅扰他们，不比搅扰他们的船更多。露西娅望过去，头一个看见的，正是她的丈夫。她大叫一声，张开双臂，呼喊道：“亲爱的风仙子，如今我真真切切知道，你们守信于我，在我最危难的时辰救了我！”

===Original===
Then she told her husband all that had happened, and showed him the bag of gold, and prayed him take her back to her little cottage and her babies by the sea; and she knew that it was the windfairies that had brought her husband to her, for he told her that whatever way they steered the ship it would only take one course, and the wind had blown it without their guidance straight to the town where she was to be killed.

===Chinese===
随后她把遭遇的一切都讲给丈夫听，把那袋金子指给他看，求他带她回海边的小屋、回到孩子们身边去。她心里明白，是风仙子把丈夫带到她面前的，因为丈夫告诉她，无论他们怎样转舵，船只认一条道；风不用他们掌舵指引，径直把船吹到了这座她将被处死的城。

===Original===
So Lucilla and her husband took the bag of gold, and went back to the little cottage by the seashore, and her father and her babies, and the King and the Queen and all the rest of the people were left to build up their town as best they could, and Lucilla never saw nor heard of them any more, but lived happily with her husband for the rest of her life.

===Chinese===
于是露西娅和丈夫带上那袋金子，回到海边的小屋，同父亲和孩子们团聚。国王、王后和其余的众人，只得自己尽力把城池重新建起来。露西娅从此再没见过他们，也没有再听到他们的音讯，同丈夫快快活活地过了一辈子。

## Vain Kesta / 虚荣的凯斯塔

===Original===
Once upon a time there lived a young girl called Kesta who was the dairymaid at a large farm. She milked the cows and made the cheese and butter, and sometimes took them into the town to sell for her master.

===Chinese===
从前，有个少女名叫凯斯塔（Kesta），在一家大农庄当挤奶女工。她挤牛奶，做奶酪和黄油，有时还替主人把它们运进城去卖。

===Original===
On the farm worked a man named Adam. He drove in the cows for Kesta to milk and watched her milking them. As she was a comely-looking girl and did her work well, he thought she would make him a good wife; so one day he said, “Kesta, how would you like to marry me? and then we can save our money and some day buy a farm for ourselves, and I should be a farmer and you should be the farmer’s wife, and have servants to wait on you.”

===Chinese===
农庄里有个帮工名叫亚当（Adam）。他把奶牛赶进来给凯斯塔挤奶，常常在一旁看她挤。见她模样俊俏，活计又干得好，他心想她准能做个好妻子；于是有一天他说：“凯斯塔，嫁给我好不好？这样咱们可以把钱攒起来，将来买一座自己的农庄，我当农夫，你当农夫的太太，还有仆人伺候你。”

===Original===
“That I should like very much,” said Kesta, “but I can’t say yes, at once. Tomorrow I am going to town with my cheeses, when I come back I will give you an answer.”

===Chinese===
“那我是很乐意的，”凯斯塔说，“可我不能马上就答应。明天我要带奶酪进城，回来就给你答复。”

===Original===
At night Kesta looked into her glass and said, “I wonder why Adam wishes to marry me? but as he does, most likely some better man would like to do so; it would be folly to marry him till I see if I can’t do better. I must look about me when I go to town tomorrow, and see who I can meet.”

===Chinese===
夜里，凯斯塔照着镜子自语：“不知亚当为什么想娶我？既然他都想，多半还有比他更好的男人也愿意；不等看看能不能寻个更好的就嫁给他，那才叫傻呢。明天进城我得留心张望，瞧瞧能遇上些什么人。”

===Original===
In the morning she dressed herself with great care in her best clothes, and set out for the town with the cheeses in a basket under her arm. When she had got a little way she passed a mill, and the miller all white with flour stood in the yard directing his men. He was an oldish man, and his wife was recently dead, and Kesta thought as she drew near, it would be a better thing to marry him than to marry poor Adam, so she said, “Good day, would you kindly let me rest a little?”

===Chinese===
早晨，她细细梳妆，换上最好的衣裳，胳臂上挎着一篮奶酪，动身进城。走出不多远，她经过一座磨坊，磨坊主（miller）满身面粉白，正站在院子里指挥伙计们干活。他上了点年纪，新近死了妻子。凯斯塔走近时心想，嫁给他可比嫁给穷亚当强，于是开口道：“日安，能容我歇一会儿吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly, my girl,” said the miller, “you seem to be out of breath?”

===Chinese===
“当然可以，姑娘，”磨坊主说，“你像是一路喘着气来的？”

===Original===
“And well I may be,” said Kesta, “such a run as I have had. I’ve come from the farm yonder, and it was as much as I could do to get away, for the farmer’s man was very angry because I would not marry him, and of course I am too good for him, a pretty girl like me.”

===Chinese===
“能不喘吗，”凯斯塔说，“我一路跑了那么远。我是从那边农庄来的，好不容易才脱开身，因为农庄那个帮工见我不肯嫁给他，气得不得了。像我这么漂亮的姑娘，他自然是配不上的。”

===Original===
“Are you really a pretty girl?” said the miller; “let me see, perhaps you are. Well, if you are too good for the farmer’s man perhaps you would suit me. How would you like to marry me, and live in the millhouse yonder?”

===Chinese===
“你真是个漂亮姑娘？”磨坊主说，“让我瞧瞧——兴许真是。这么说，农庄帮工配不上你，兴许你倒配得上我。嫁给我，住到那边儿的磨坊宅子里，你愿意不愿意？”

===Original===
“I think I should like it well,” said Kesta, “but I have some business in the town, and must go there first, so I’ll stop here and tell you as I come back.” So she said goodbye, and went on her way feeling very merry.

===Chinese===
“我想我是很愿意的，”凯斯塔说，“只是我在城里还有点事，得先去一趟，回来路过时再给你回话吧。”于是她道了别，一路欢欢喜喜地往前走。

===Original===
“It would be much better to marry the miller than to marry Adam, but who knows if I may not do better than either, so I must not be in any hurry.” So she walked on, and near to the town she met a man on a white horse, and saw it was the bailiff of the great Duke at the Palace. “Who knows but that he may want a wife?” she said to herself, “I can but try.” So she sat down by the roadside and called out, “Ah me, what a thing it is to be a poor girl who has to run away from all the men she meets!”

===Chinese===
“嫁给磨坊主自然比嫁给亚当强得多，可谁知道我会不会遇上比他们俩都好的呢，所以千万急不得。”她这样想着往前走，快到城边时，遇见一个骑白马的人，认出是宫殿（the Palace）里大公爵的管家（bailiff）。“谁知道他是不是正想娶房太太呢？”她自忖道，“试试总无妨。”于是她在路边坐下，扬声喊道：“唉，我这苦命的姑娘，碰上什么男人都得躲着跑，这是什么世道啊！”

===Original===
“Why,” cried the bailiff, stopping his horse. “Why have you to run? who tries to hurt you?”

===Chinese===
“咦，”管家勒住马喊道，“你干吗要跑？谁要伤害你？”

===Original===
“No one tries to hurt me,” said Kesta, “but I have to run from men who want to marry me, because I am so pretty. At first it was a man at our farm, and now it is the miller, who would not let me pass his door unless I promised to come back and marry him, but I am far too good for such as he.”

===Chinese===
“倒没有人要伤害我，”凯斯塔说，“可那些想娶我的男人逼得我只好跑，谁叫我生得这么漂亮。起先是农庄里的一个帮工，如今又是那磨坊主，我不答应回来嫁给他，他就不许我走过他家门口。可他那样的人，实在远远配不上我。”

===Original===
“Is this really so?” cried the bailiff, who hated the miller; “did the miller really want to marry you? If you’re too good to marry him, it may be you would suit me.”

===Chinese===
“当真如此？”管家喊道——他本就恨那磨坊主——“磨坊主真想娶你？既然嫁他委屈了你，兴许你倒合我的意。”

===Original===
“Indeed,” said Kesta, “I think that might do well, for I should live in a nice house and have plenty of servants. But I have to go into the town on business, and you’re sure to be somewhere about here, and when I come back we will arrange it.” So she set off, leaving the bailiff chuckling at the thought of how angry the miller would be if he married Kesta.

===Chinese===
“可不，”凯斯塔说，“我看这倒不错，我能住上好房子，还有的是仆人使唤。只是我还得进城办事，你横竖就在这一带，等我回来咱们再商量。”说罢她又上了路，留下管家独自得意——一想到娶了凯斯塔、磨坊主会气成什么样，他便暗暗直笑。

===Original===
On went Kesta in high good-humour. “Now am I indeed doing well,” said she; “how clever I was not to marry Adam before I came to town.” Presently she reached the town, and in the high street she passed the bank, and the banker himself stood in the doorway. He was fat and ugly and old, but his hands were covered with rings, and Kesta knew his pockets were full of gold. Kesta said, “It would be a fine thing to marry him, and I could hold up my head with anyone. I think I’ll speak to him, as it would be folly to pass him without trying.” So she gave a loud sigh and said, “Alack a day, how hard is my lot!”

===Chinese===
凯斯塔一路兴高采烈。“这下我真是干得漂亮，”她说，“幸亏进城之前没先嫁给亚当，我多聪明啊。”不多时她进了城，在大街上走过银行门口，银行家（banker）本人正站在门洞里。他又肥又丑又老，可双手戴满了戒指，凯斯塔知道他口袋里装满了金子。她想：“嫁给他才叫美事呢，往后我在谁面前都抬得起头。我看该跟他搭句话，白白走过不试一试，那才是傻呢。”于是她高声叹道：“唉唉，我的命好苦哇！”

===Original===
“Why, what is wrong, my pretty lass?” said the banker.

===Chinese===
“哟，怎么啦，我漂亮的小妞？”银行家说。

===Original===
“Pretty you may well say,” answered Kesta. “Would I were not so, for thence come all my troubles.”

===Chinese===
“漂亮二字你说着了，”凯斯塔答道，“但愿我没这么漂亮才好，我的一切烦恼都打这儿来。”

===Original===
“And what are they?” asked the banker.

===Chinese===
“都是些什么烦恼？”银行家问。

===Original===
“Only wherever I go, I have no peace, for all the men want to marry me. First it is the farmer, then the miller, and lastly the duke’s bailiff, who would scarcely let me pass on the road till I had promised him; and of course it is impossible, and I am much too pretty for any of them.”

===Chinese===
“还不是我走到哪儿都不得安生，男人们个个想娶我。先是农庄的帮工，接着是磨坊主，末了又是公爵的管家，我不答应了他，他几乎不肯放我上路。这自然都是办不到的事，我太漂亮了，他们哪一个都配不上。”

===Original===
“Is this really true?” cried the banker; “if so, there must be something very superior about you. Perhaps you would be good enough for me. How would you like to be my wife, and ride in a fine carriage, and wear silk gowns all day?”

===Chinese===
“这话当真？”银行家喊道，“若当真如此，你身上必有过人之处。兴许你倒配得上我。做我的太太怎么样？坐华丽的马车，成天穿着绸缎衣裳。”

===Original===
“Nay, that would be much more fitting,” cried Kesta, “and from the first I thought you would be much more suitable to be my husband than any of the others I have met; but I must go down the town first, so I will come in here on my way back.” So she went on till she came to a great square in front of the barracks where the soldiers were drilling, with their helmets and swords glittering in the sun, and at their head rode the General of the army. His voice was hoarse with shouting at his men, and he swore dreadfully, but he was covered with gold, and looked very grand. “Now supposing he has no wife,” thought Kesta, “it would be a really fine thing to marry him: I can but try.” So she waited till the soldiers were marching into the barracks, and then, when he was riding away, she went so close under the horse’s feet that he shouted to her in case she should be run over. “Alas! what a life is mine,” she cried very loud that he might hear, “hunted here and there till I don’t know where I go!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，那才更般配呢，”凯斯塔喊道，“其实打一见面我就觉得，比我一路遇上的那些人都合适得多，你才最配做我的丈夫；只是我得先往城那头去一趟，回来时再进你这儿来。”于是她继续往前走，来到兵营（barracks）前的大广场。士兵们正在操练，头盔和刀剑在太阳底下闪闪发亮，队伍前头骑着的是统领军队的将军（General）。他冲着部下吆喝，嗓子都喊哑了，嘴里还骂骂咧咧难听得很，可他浑身金光闪闪，好不威风。“且慢，倘若他还没娶太太，”凯斯塔心想，“嫁给他才真是天大的美事：试试总无妨。”于是她等到士兵们列队开进兵营，趁将军骑马要走时，凑到马蹄底下那么近，吓得他冲她大吼，生怕她被踩着。“唉！我这过的什么日子哟，”她放开嗓门大喊，好让他听见，“被人东追西赶，赶得我连自己去哪儿都不知道了！”

===Original===
“Why, who hunts you?” cried the General angrily; “what nonsense you talk, my good girl.”

===Chinese===
“胡说，谁追你了？”将军怒气冲冲地喊道，“你这话没道理，好姑娘。”

===Original===
“How dare you say I talk nonsense,” cried Kesta, “when it is as much as I can do to get through your town for the men who want me to stop and marry them!”

===Chinese===
“你好大的胆子，竟说我没道理，”凯斯塔喊道，“男人们拦着我要娶我，我连你们这座城都好不容易才走得过来！”

===Original===
“And why do they want you to marry them?” asked the General.

===Chinese===
“那他们为什么都想娶你？”将军问。

===Original===
“Because I’m so pretty, of course,” said Kesta promptly, and she took off her hat and looked up at the General.

===Chinese===
“还不是因为我太漂亮了，”凯斯塔应声答道，说着摘下帽子，仰起脸望着将军。

===Original===
“I don’t think you are so pretty,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我看你没多么漂亮。”他说。

===Original===
“But I am,” cried Kesta angrily, “and it’s only stupid people who don’t see it. Go and ask the men in the town. First it was a man at the farm, then the miller, then the duke’s bailiff, then the banker﻿—they all wanted to marry me, and I am much too good for any of them!”

===Chinese===
“我偏就这么漂亮，”凯斯塔气呼呼地喊道，“只有蠢人才看不出来。不信去问问城里那些男人。先是农庄的帮工，再是磨坊主，再是公爵的管家，再是银行家——他们个个都想娶我，我太好了，他们哪一个都配不上！”

===Original===
“If this is all true,” said the General, “of course you must be exceedingly pretty, and as you say you are much too good for them, perhaps you might suit me. How would you like that?”

===Chinese===
“倘若这些全是真的，”将军说，“那你必定漂亮极了。既然你说他们都配不上你，兴许你倒合我的意。你觉得如何？”

===Original===
“That might be better,” said Kesta, “and as you wish it very much I will agree, and I hope you will try to make me a good husband; but I am obliged to go a little further on important business, and I will meet you here on my way back,” and on she went laughing to herself. “Indeed I am fortunate,” thought she; “and as they all seem willing to marry me why should I not try higher, and see what the Duke himself would say? There is nothing like being practical, and it would be downright silly not to speak to the Duke now I am here.” By this time she had come to the Duke’s palace, so she stopped a servant who was coming out and asked if he were at home, for she said, “I have special business with him.” “He is sitting by the stream in the garden, where he sits fishing all day, and you can go and speak to him if you choose,” said the servant. So Kesta went through the courtyard into the garden, and straight on to where the Duke sat beside the stream with a long rod in his hand fishing. He was dressed all in green, and seemed to be half asleep, and Kesta came quite near him before he saw her. Then she said, “Ah, pity me, your Grace, and listen to my sad story.”

===Chinese===
“那样兴许更好，”凯斯塔说，“既然你这么盼着，我就应下了，也希望你尽力做个好丈夫；只是我还得再往前去办件要紧事，回来路过时就在这儿见你。”说罢她继续往前走，一路独自笑着。“我可真是走运，”她想，“他们既然个个都肯娶我，我何不再往高处试试，看看公爵（Duke）本人怎么说？做事就得讲实际，人都到这儿了不同公爵搭句话，那才叫十足的傻呢。”这时她已来到公爵的宫殿，便拦住一个正往外走的仆人，问公爵在不在家，她说：“我有要紧事找他。”“他正坐在花园的小溪边，成天坐在那儿钓鱼，你愿意就自己去同他说吧。”仆人说。于是凯斯塔穿过庭院走进花园，径直走到公爵坐着钓鱼的地方，他手里握着一根长钓竿。他通身穿着绿衣裳，像是半睡半醒，凯斯塔走到离他很近了他才看见她。她便开口道：“唉，爵爷可怜可怜我，听我讲讲我的伤心事吧。”

===Original===
“Good gracious! who are you?﻿—don’t you know I am the Duke?” said he.

===Chinese===
“天哪！你是谁？——你不知道我是公爵吗？”他说。

===Original===
“And that is why I have come to you to ask you to protect me from all the men who pursue me,” said Kesta.

===Chinese===
“正因如此，我才来投奔您，求您护着我，别让那些追我的男人缠住我。”凯斯塔说。

===Original===
“Why do they pursue you?” asked the Duke.

===Chinese===
“他们为什么追你？”公爵问。

===Original===
“Because I am so pretty,” replied Kesta. “They all want to marry me: first the man at the farm, then the miller I met on the road, then your bailiff, then the banker, then the General of your army, and he would only let me go when I promised to go back to him.”

===Chinese===
“因为我太漂亮了，”凯斯塔答道，“他们个个都想娶我：先是农庄的帮工，接着是路上遇见的磨坊主，再是您的管家，再是银行家，再是您军队的将军，我答应回去找他，他才肯放我走。”

===Original===
“The General!” said the Duke. “Is this true? does he really want to marry you?”

===Chinese===
“将军！”公爵说，“这是真的？他真想娶你？”

===Original===
“Of course he does,” said Kesta; “if you doubt what I say you had better send to the town and ask.”

===Chinese===
“当然是真心，”凯斯塔说，“您若不信我的话，大可差人进城去问问。”

===Original===
“Indeed,” said the Duke, “I should not have thought you so very pretty, but if what you say is true you must be. I’m not sure if it would not suit me to marry you myself; but mind, I shall be exceedingly angry if I find you have not told me the truth, and they did not want to marry you. Of course you would be delighted to marry me and be the Duchess?”

===Chinese===
“说来，”公爵道，“我本不觉得你有那么漂亮，可你若说的属实，那你必定是的。说不定由我亲自娶你，倒正合适；不过你听着，倘若我发现你没用真话、他们并不想娶你，我可要大发雷霆的。嫁给我做公爵夫人（Duchess），你自然求之不得吧？”

===Original===
“Aye, that I should,” cried Kesta, and she grinned with delight.

===Chinese===
“那还用说，我自然愿意！”凯斯塔喊道，欢喜得咧着嘴直笑。

===Original===
Then the Duke took from his side a horn and blew it loudly. There came from the palace four pages, dressed in blue and gold, who stood in a row to receive his orders. “See,” cried the Duke, “I am going to marry this lady, who everybody thinks is very beautiful, so see that you treat her with respect; and go to the palace and bid them to prepare a feast and fitting clothes for the bride, and tell the chaplain to be ready, for I mean to marry her at once.”

===Chinese===
于是公爵从腰间取下一支号角，大声吹响。宫殿里跑出四个侍童，穿一身蓝底金绣，排成一列听候吩咐。“都听着，”公爵喊道，“我要娶这位小姐了，人人都夸她美貌，你们须对她恭敬有礼；回宫去吩咐他们备下喜宴和配得上新娘的衣裳，再告诉随行牧师作好准备，我要立刻娶她。”

===Original===
“And now,” he said to Kesta, when all his pages had returned to the palace, “come and sit by me and watch me fish till all is ready.”

===Chinese===
“现在，”等侍童们都回宫去后，他对凯斯塔说，“过来坐在我身边，看我钓鱼，等一切备齐。”

===Original===
So Kesta sat by his side and watched him fishing with his long rod, but after a time she grew tired of being silent, and said, “What have you caught?”

===Chinese===
凯斯塔便坐在他身边，看他握着长钓竿钓鱼。过了半晌，她闷得忍不住了，开口道：“您钓着什么了？”

===Original===
“Nothing yet,” said the Duke.

===Chinese===
“还没有。”公爵说。

===Original===
“Then why do you go on?” asked she.

===Chinese===
“那您为什么还钓？”她问。

===Original===
“Because I’m sure to catch something soon, and it’s amusing. Wouldn’t you like to hold the rod a little?”

===Chinese===
“反正很快就该钓着了，再说这也好玩。你想不想来握一会儿钓竿？”

===Original===
“Yes, very much,” answered Kesta, who was afraid of offending him. So she put out her hand to take the rod, and as she did so the basket fell from her arm and the cheeses rolled out.

===Chinese===
“想，很想。”凯斯塔答道，她生怕得罪了他。于是她伸手去接钓竿，一伸手，臂弯里的篮子掉了下来，奶酪骨碌碌滚了一地。

===Original===
“What are those round balls?” asked the Duke, “and what an odd smell they have.”

===Chinese===
“这些圆滚滚的是什么？”公爵问，“味儿也怪得很。”

===Original===
“They are my cheeses,” cried Kesta; “I made them yesterday, and was taking them to sell, when﻿—”

===Chinese===
“是我的奶酪呀，”凯斯塔喊道，“我昨天做的，正要拿去卖，谁知——”

===Original===
“Good gracious, you made them!” cried the Duke with a scream. “Then you must be a common dairymaid, and your hands are quite rough. How terrible! And I was just going to marry you. How dare you think yourself good enough to marry me!” and he sprang to his feet in a towering passion, and seizing his horn blew it so loudly that the four pages ran up in great alarm. “Hunt her away,” cried the Duke, “she is an impostor﻿—a common farm wench and makes cheeses. She thought herself good enough to be the Duchess!”

===Chinese===
“天哪，是你做的！”公爵尖叫起来，“那你一定是个下等挤奶女工，手上准是粗糙的。太可怕了！我刚才还差点娶你。你竟敢觉得自己配得上我！”他勃然大怒，一跃而起，抓起号角拼命吹得震天响，四个侍童慌慌张张跑了上来。“把她赶走，”公爵喊道，“她是个骗子——一个做奶酪的下等农庄丫头，还自以为配当公爵夫人！”

===Original===
Away flew Kesta, with the pages after her hooting and shouting, “Down with the impertinent hussy who wanted to marry the Duke, a common dairymaid who makes cheeses.”

===Chinese===
凯斯塔撒腿就逃，侍童们在后面起哄叫喊：“打倒这个想嫁公爵的厚脸皮贱蹄子，一个做奶酪的下等挤奶女工！”

===Original===
On rushed Kesta till she came to the General’s house, and at his window he sat in his fine uniform. He sat waiting for her, but when he saw the pages behind her he called, “Hey-dey, what is all this fuss about?”

===Chinese===
凯斯塔一路狂奔，跑到将军的府邸，他正穿着漂亮的军装坐在窗前。他坐在那儿等她回来，可一看见她身后的侍童，便喊道：“嗬，这是闹的哪一出？”

===Original===
“It is nothing,” said Kesta. “See, I have come back to marry you as I promised.”

===Chinese===
“没什么，”凯斯塔说，“瞧，我依约回来嫁你了。”

===Original===
But here the pages shouted, “Away with the impertinent dairymaid, who thought herself good enough to marry the Duke.”

===Chinese===
可侍童们这时喊了起来：“撵走这个厚脸皮的挤奶女工，她竟自以为配嫁给公爵。”

===Original===
“And wouldn’t the Duke marry her?” asked the General.

===Chinese===
“公爵不肯娶她？”将军问。

===Original===
“Of course not; she is nothing but a farm wench,” cried the pages, “and she is to be chased from the town for her impertinence.”

===Chinese===
“当然不娶，她不过是个农庄丫头，”侍童们喊道，“她这般无礼，要把她撵出城去。”

===Original===
“And so she shall,” cried the General; “she thought she was fit for me too﻿—it is disgraceful!” and he cried to some soldiers who stood by his door, “Here, my men, help to chase this good-for-nothing hussy out of the town.”

===Chinese===
“撵，一定得撵！”将军喊道，“她还自以为配得上我呢——简直丢人！”他冲门口站着的几个士兵大喊：“喂，弟兄们，帮着把这个没用的贱蹄子撵出城去！”

===Original===
But before he had finished Kesta was running down the street with all her might to the banker’s. At last she came to the banker’s big square house standing beside the bank, and on the steps was the banker himself in his shiny black clothes with gold rings on his hands.

===Chinese===
不等他喊完，凯斯塔已顺着大街没命地朝银行家那边跑去。她终于跑到银行旁边那座方方正正的银行家大宅，台阶上正站着银行家本人，穿一身油亮的黑衣裳，双手戴满金戒指。

===Original===
“Here I am,” cried Kesta; “and let me in quickly, for I am out of breath with running.”

===Chinese===
“我来了，”凯斯塔喊道，“快让我进去，我跑得都快喘不上气了。”

===Original===
“Why have you hurried so?” cried the banker, and as he spoke the pages and the soldiers came round the corner, “and what is all this shouting for?”

===Chinese===
“你干吗跑成这样？”银行家喊道，话音未落，侍童和士兵们已拐过街角，“外头喊的又是什么？”

===Original===
“Nay, how should I know?” cried Kesta, running into the house.

===Chinese===
“嗨，我哪知道？”凯斯塔喊着，一头跑进屋里。

===Original===
But up came her pursuers, crying, “Away with her! down with her!”

===Chinese===
可追她的人们也赶到了，喊道：“撵走她！打倒她！”

===Original===
“Who is it you are calling after?” asked the banker.

===Chinese===
“你们叫嚷的是谁？”银行家问。

===Original===
“That wench in the yellow dress who has gone into your house.”

===Chinese===
“那个穿黄衣裳、跑进你家的丫头。”

===Original===
“Why, what has she done?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“哟，她做了什么？”他问。

===Original===
“Why, she thought herself good enough to marry the Duke and the General, and she is to be hooted out of the town for her impudence!”

===Chinese===
“嘿，她自以为配嫁公爵和将军，她这样厚脸皮，要被轰出城去！”

===Original===
“But didn’t the General want to marry her?” asked the banker.

===Chinese===
“可将军不是想娶她吗？”银行家问。

===Original===
“Our General!” cried the soldiers angrily; “why, she’s only a dairymaid, and not fit for him.”

===Chinese===
“我们的将军！”士兵们气呼呼地喊道，“嘿，她不过是个挤奶女工，哪配得上他。”

===Original===
“Then I’m sure she can’t be good enough for me, for I’m quite as good as he,” said the banker, and he ran into the house in a great rage, crying, “Begone, you impertinent jade! how dare you think yourself good enough for me to marry!” It chanced at this moment that the clerks were coming out of the bank next door, and when he saw them he cried, “Here, my good fellows, help to chase this minx from the town; she wishes to be my wife, when she is nothing but a common dairymaid.” On this the clerks burst out laughing, and one and all ran after Kesta, who ran with all her might and main.

===Chinese===
“那她也必定配不上我，我可不比他差。”银行家说，随即怒气冲冲地冲进屋去，大喊：“滚出去，你这厚脸皮的贱人！你竟敢觉得自己配让我娶！”恰在这时，隔壁银行的职员们正往外走，他一见便喊道：“喂，好伙计们，帮着把这个骚蹄子撵出城去；她想给我做太太，可她不过是个下等挤奶女工。”职员们听了哄然大笑，一个个都追着凯斯塔跑，凯斯塔则拼了命地飞奔。

===Original===
“It’s too hard,” sobbed she; “what have I done to be treated like this?” But run as fast as she might she could not reach the bailiff’s house before them, and the pages, soldiers, and clerks were all close to her, shouting and laughing.

===Chinese===
“这也太狠了，”她抽泣道，“我到底做了什么，要受这样的对待？”可她跑得再快，也赶不到追兵前头进管家的家，侍童、士兵和职员们已紧紧逼到身后，又叫又笑。

===Original===
“Why, what’s the matter?” cried the bailiff, “and why are you shouting at this poor maid?”

===Chinese===
“咦，这是怎么了？”管家喊道，“你们冲着这可怜的姑娘喊什么？”

===Original===
“Why,” said they, “she wanted to marry first the Duke, and the General, and the banker, and of course they would not have her, because she is only a common dairy wench.”

===Chinese===
“嘿，”他们说，“她先后想嫁公爵、将军和银行家，人家当然不肯要她，因为她不过是个下等挤奶丫头。”

===Original===
“What impertinence!” cried the bailiff; “and, now I come to think of it, she asked to marry me too; indeed she merits punishment for such behaviour,” and seeing some of his farm people close at hand, he bid them run after Kesta and drive her out of the town. But this time she had started first, and had got on to the mill before they could reach her, and she ran into the garden where the miller was. “Well, I’m glad to see you back,” said he, “but how hard you have run.”

===Chinese===
“好大的胆子！”管家喊道，“这么说来，她连我也求过婚；这样的行径，实在该受罚。”他看见自己的几个庄稼汉就在近旁，便吩咐他们去追凯斯塔，把她赶出城去。可这一回她先动了身，没等他们追上就到了磨坊，一头跑进磨坊主所在的园子。“哟，真高兴见你回来，”磨坊主说，“可你怎么跑得这么急。”

===Original===
“I was in such a hurry to get back. Now let’s go into the house,” she said.

===Chinese===
“我急着赶回来嘛。走，咱们进屋去。”她说。

===Original===
“Come along,” said the miller; “but what are all those people shouting for?”

===Chinese===
“来吧，”磨坊主说，“只是外头那些人喊的是什么事？”

===Original===
“ ’Tis only the farmers bringing home pigs from the market,” said Kesta, but she felt frightened, for she heard the people calling after her.

===Chinese===
“不过是庄稼人从市场把猪牵回家罢了。”凯斯塔说，心里却怕得慌，因为她听见人群在后面冲她叫喊。

===Original===
“Pigs don’t make a noise like that,” said the miller, “I will go and see what it is about.” And when he heard that they were all shouting at Kesta, he flew into a violent rage and cried, “If she wasn’t good enough for the bailiff I’m sure she’s not fit for me,” and he called to some of his men who were working at the mill, “See there, my men, do you see that girl? throw some flour at her, for she is an impudent hussy, and asked me to marry her.”

===Chinese===
“猪可不会吵成那样，”磨坊主说，“我去瞧瞧是怎么回事。”待他听说众人叫骂的正是凯斯塔，登时勃然大怒，喊道：“管家都嫌配不上的，肯定也配不上我。”他叫来几个在磨坊干活的伙计：“瞧见没，弟兄们，瞧见那姑娘没有？朝她扔面粉，她是个厚脸皮的贱蹄子，还求过我娶她。”

===Original===
Away flew Kesta again, and after her came all the crowd in a long line. “How unfortunate I am,” she sobbed; “but anyhow I can go back to Adam; he’s sure to be glad to have me,” and on she sped, and at last she came to the farm and ran in, calling to Adam.

===Chinese===
凯斯塔又没命地逃，身后一大长串人紧追不舍。“我的命真苦啊，”她抽泣道，“不过不管怎样，我总还可以回去找亚当；他见了我一定高兴。”她拼命往前赶，终于回到农庄，跑进门去喊亚当。

===Original===
“Is that you, Kesta?” cried Adam, coming to meet her, and kissing her. “I’m glad to see you, but why are you so hot?”

===Chinese===
“是你吗，凯斯塔？”亚当喊着迎上来，吻了她。“见到你真高兴，可你怎么浑身这么烫？”

===Original===
“It is the sun, it was so strong,” said Kesta.

===Chinese===
“是太阳晒的，日头太毒了。”凯斯塔说。

===Original===
“Then sit down and grow cool,” said Adam. “But I wonder what all that shouting outside can be?”

===Chinese===
“那就坐下凉快凉快，”亚当说，“只是外头那片叫喊声又是怎么回事？”

===Original===
“It is only people making holiday,” cried Kesta. But for all she could say Adam went out to ask the people what they wanted at the farm?

===Chinese===
“不过是人们过节寻乐子罢了。”凯斯塔喊道。可任她怎么说，亚当还是出门去问那些人上农庄来做什么。

===Original===
“We want nothing at the farm,” they cried, “but we followed that impudent wench dressed in yellow.”

===Chinese===
“我们上农庄没事，”他们喊道，“我们只是跟着那个穿黄衣裳的厚脸皮丫头。”

===Original===
“Why, what has she done?” asked Adam.

===Chinese===
“哟，她做了什么？”亚当问。

===Original===
“Done!” they cried. “Why, she came up to the town and asked to marry the miller, and the banker, and the bailiff, and the General, and even the Duke himself, so she deserves to be punished for her presumption.”

===Chinese===
“做了什么！”他们喊道，“嘿，她进了城，先后求嫁磨坊主、银行家、管家、将军，连公爵本人都不放过，这般自不量力，就该受罚。”

===Original===
Then Adam looked very grave, and went back to the farm and said, “Indeed, Kesta, I cannot marry you now, since you’ve been to the town and tried to get a finer husband than me,” and he went back to his work, and left Kesta sitting all alone; and there she sat and cried by herself, and did not get any husband after all, because she was so false and vain.

===Chinese===
亚当听了神色凝重，回到农庄里说：“凯斯塔，你进了城，想寻个比我体面的丈夫，如今我可不能娶你了。”说罢他回去干活，撇下凯斯塔独自坐在那里；她就那么坐着，独自哭泣，到头来一个丈夫也没捞着，只因她这么虚伪又虚荣。
